# Architecture Decisions

Log of significant design and architecture decisions. Append new entries at the bottom.

---

### 001 — Multi-module Maven structure
**Date**: 2026-05  
**Decision**: Split gametime-service into `gametime-api` (generated stubs) and `gametime-app` (hand-written code).  
**Rationale**: Keeps generated OpenAPI code isolated from application logic. The api module is a pure build artifact — no hand-written code lives there. The app module depends on it as a Maven dependency.  
**Alternatives considered**: Single module with generated sources in target/ (simpler but generated and hand-written code mixed in IDE).

### 002 — OpenAPI delegate pattern
**Date**: 2026-05  
**Decision**: Use `delegatePattern=true` in openapi-generator-maven-plugin.  
**Rationale**: Generated controller delegates to a hand-written `V1ApiDelegate` implementation. This means we never touch generated controller code — the delegate is the integration point.

### 003 — Postgres for local dev, H2 for tests
**Date**: 2026-05  
**Decision**: Local development runs against Postgres in Docker. `mvn test` uses H2 in-memory with no Docker dependency.  
**Rationale**: Postgres for local dev gives realistic behavior (triggers, schemas, data types). H2 for tests keeps the test suite fast and CI-friendly with zero infrastructure.  
**Trade-off**: Liquibase changesets must be dual-compatible. Postgres-specific SQL gated with `dbms:postgresql`.

### 004 — Dedicated gametime schema
**Date**: 2026-05  
**Decision**: All application tables live in the `gametime` schema, not `public`.  
**Rationale**: Clean separation from system tables. H2 supports schemas, so this works in both environments.

### 005 — JDK 21 LTS via SDKMAN
**Date**: 2026-05  
**Decision**: Target Java 21 (SDKMAN: `21.0.9-tem`). Do not use Homebrew JDK 25.  
**Rationale**: Lombok 1.18.x is incompatible with JDK 25. JDK 21 is the current LTS release. All Maven commands must set `JAVA_HOME` explicitly.

### 006 — Attribute-driven skill calculation
**Date**: 2026-05 (pre-existing design)  
**Decision**: 16 raw player attributes feed into 13 derived skill calculations via weighted formulas with threshold bonuses/penalties.  
**Rationale**: Attributes are the "DNA" of a player — stable, slowly changing. Skills are the observable output — what a player can actually do on the court. This two-tier model allows the same attribute profile to produce different skill outcomes based on formula tuning.

### 007 — Cucumber on JUnit 5 Platform
**Date**: 2026-05  
**Decision**: Migrated from JUnit 4 vintage to JUnit 5 Platform (`@Suite` + `cucumber-junit-platform-engine`).  
**Rationale**: JUnit 4 vintage engine is deprecated. JUnit 5 Platform is the standard runner. Removed junit-vintage-engine dependency.

### 008 — Attribute scale: 1–20, average = 10
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision**: Canonical player attribute scale is **1–20, with 10 = league average**. Applies to all 21 attributes (16 original + 5 new). The existing 16 attributes were rescaled to mean ~10 / stdev ~3.5 via a distribution-based (rank → inverse-normal) transform.  
**Rationale**: The original 1–10 scale was too coarse to separate ~420 players — values compressed around 5–6, and stars had to be hand-bumped past 10 (the data had drifted to a nominal 1–15 with most values still in 3–8). The real fix was widening the *distribution*, not just the ceiling. Centering average at 10 leaves deliberate headroom for genuine outliers (stars 16–18, generational 19–20) and below-average players (3–5), and matches how rating systems keep "average" well below max.  
**Trade-off**: The existing 13 skill calculators' threshold branches (`>9`, `>7`, etc.) were written against the old ~1–10 range and must be re-tuned for 1–20, or they mis-fire (an "above average" bonus now triggers for most of the league).  
**Alternatives considered**: 1–15 (keep current data — marginal variability gain); 1–100 (false precision, can't meaningfully assign across 420 hand-curated players); linear shift+stretch instead of distribution-based (preserves exact gaps but amplifies existing data artifacts).

### 009 — Player data is CSV-driven
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision**: Player seed data is the source-of-truth CSV `db/players.csv` (422 players), loaded via a Liquibase `loadData` changeset. Replaced the ~420 inline SQL `INSERT` statements previously in `release.1.0.1.dataload.sql`.  
**Rationale**: A CSV is far easier to inspect, sort, bulk-transform (rescale/re-center), and eyeball for outliers than 420 single-line inserts. Follows the existing `gm.csv` / `loadData` precedent. The old `player.csv` had drifted stale (421 rows, missing a player); the canonical CSV was regenerated from the authoritative SQL. Team inserts stay in SQL (FK parent — must load before players).  
**Trade-off**: Schema changes now require both a migration (add column) and a matching CSV column; load order in `changelog.yml` must keep teams → add-columns → players. Existing dev DBs need `docker compose down -v` to pick up schema changes.  
**Alternatives considered**: Keep editing inline SQL (error-prone at 420 rows); append columns to the stale `player.csv` (would bake in the old compressed distribution and the missing player).

### 010 — Dropped fetchConference endpoint
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision**: Removed `GET /v1/conference/{confId}` (`fetchConference`) from the OpenAPI spec. Clients that want a conference view filter the `GET /v1/league` response by `Team.conference` themselves.  
**Rationale**: The endpoint's spec was already inconsistent (declared a single `Team` response while its summary promised a list), and conference grouping is a trivial client-side filter over the full-league payload the client already fetches. Dropping it removes a redundant server endpoint rather than fixing a half-specified one.  
**Trade-off**: If the league grows large enough that returning all 40 teams to filter for 10 is wasteful, a server-side filtered endpoint can be reintroduced. Acceptable at current scale.

### 011 — Player→team link: nullable team_id, decoupled from Team entity (SUPERSEDED by #012)
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Superseded**: The interim step — a plain nullable `player.team_id` FK — was replaced by the `player_team` / `player_team_hist` model in #012 before it shipped. Kept here for the trail. The original reasoning (separate create/assign endpoints require a "created but unassigned" state; the player aggregate should not hold a `Team` object reference) carried forward into #012.

### 012 — Player↔team is a join model with history (player_team + player_team_hist)
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision**: The player→team relationship is fully decoupled from both the player and team entities and modeled as two tables:
- `player_team` — **current** roster assignment. `player_id` is the PK (at most one team per player); columns `team_id`, `transaction_type`, `assigned_date`. A **free agent has no row**.
- `player_team_hist` — **append-only** history of roster transactions (assignments only). One row per move; never updated or deleted.

`player.team_id` was removed entirely. `TeamEntity` no longer has a JPA `players` association — the service sources a team's roster via `PlayerTeamRepo.findByTeamId` and `EntityMapper.entityToTeam(entity, roster)` composes it. In the API, `Player` exposes a read-only `currentTeamId` (derived, nullable), and `GET /v1/player/{playerId}/history` returns the `PlayerTransaction` list (most-recent-first). `createPlayer` makes a free agent; `addPlayerToTeam` inserts the `player_team` row + appends a `player_team_hist` row (type `SIGN`), 409 if already assigned. Seed roster is loaded from `roster.csv` into `player_team` with type `SEED`; history starts empty.  
**Rationale**: Trades and free agency mean a player moves between teams over time, and the game needs that history as first-class data — which a single current-state FK cannot represent. Splitting current state (`player_team`) from the immutable transaction log (`player_team_hist`) keeps "who is on this team now" a cheap PK lookup while preserving the full trail.  
**Trade-off**: Two-table writes per assignment (kept atomic in one `@Transactional` service method). Roster reads are an extra query (player_team → players) rather than a JPA join. Since there are no users/data yet, the migrations were **consolidated from scratch** into `release.1.0.1.sql` (player table created without team_id; `player_team`/`player_team_hist` part of the base schema) rather than layered as patch migrations — the interim `release.1.0.2/1.0.3/1.0.4` SQL patches were removed.  
**Alternatives considered**: Single nullable `team_id` FK (#011 — no history); a normalized `transaction` table modeling multi-player trades as one event (deferred — not needed until trades exist); season-scoped stints (no season/calendar model exists yet).

### 013 — Player status (availability) split from lineup role (team slot)
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision**: Two previously-overlapping concepts are now separate enums with no shared values:
- `Player.status` — player-**intrinsic availability**, independent of any team: `ACTIVE, INJURED, SUSPENDED`.
- `player_team.lineupRole` — the player's **slot on a specific team**: `STARTER, ROTATION, BENCH, INACTIVE, MINORS`.

Roster membership ("on a team or not") stays **derived from `player_team`** (no row = free agent) — it is *not* encoded in status (no `UNSIGNED`). `MINORS` moved out of status into `lineupRole` (it's a roster placement, not an availability state). The old `Status` enum (`STARTER, BENCH, ROTATION, MINORS, INJURED, SUSPENDED`) conflated both axes; the seed `players.csv` had been using `status` to carry lineup intent (200 STARTER / 143 ROTATION / 79 BENCH). That intent was migrated into `player_team.lineup_role` (seeded via `roster.csv`) and all player statuses set to `ACTIVE`.  
**Rationale**: One field answering two questions ("is this player available?" and "what's their lineup slot?") is a duplicate source of truth waiting to disagree. Availability is a fact about the person; lineup slot is a fact about the assignment. Separating them removes the four-value collision and gives each a single owner.  
**Alternatives considered**: Add `UNSIGNED` to status to mirror roster membership (rejected — second source of truth vs. `player_team`); keep `MINORS` in status (rejected — it's a roster concept).

### 014 — Lineup is sticky persistent state; rotationOrder is the bench queue
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision**: `PUT /v1/team/{teamId}/lineup` is **replace-all** and sets persistent state on `player_team` (`lineup_role` + `rotation_order`). It is **set-on-change**, not called per game: once set, the lineup persists until explicitly changed, and every team boots with a valid 5-starter lineup from the seed. In-game substitutions are **transient game-simulation state** (Phase 3) and never write back to `player_team`. Validation: exactly 5 `STARTER`; no duplicate players; all players must be on the team (else 404). `rotationOrder` semantics: **starters are an unordered set of 5 and carry no rotation order**; `rotationOrder` is the **bench substitution queue** (first off the bench = 1, then 2, 3…), and must be unique among all non-null values.  
**Rationale**: The coach's lineup is a standing plan, not a per-game action — modeling it as sticky state means unchanged rotations need no API call, and the engine simply reads it. Forcing starters to carry an order would fabricate data the engine doesn't need (all 5 are on the floor at tip-off); ordering only has a real meaning for the bench sub queue.  
**Trade-off**: rotationOrder has no consumer until the Phase 3 engine exists — it is correct-but-latent plumbing laid ahead of its use.  
**Alternatives considered**: Per-game lineup submission (rejected — churns roster state every game); a full 1→N depth chart including starters (deferred — additive if the engine later needs starter ordering); starters all sharing `rotationOrder = 1` (rejected — not-null but not-distinct, the worst of both).

### 015 — Team is the single source of roster state; `/roster` endpoint removed
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision**: `GET /v1/team/{teamId}` is the **one** complete view of a team's state with respect to its players. `Team.players` is now a list of `RosterEntry` (`player` + `lineupRole` + `rotationOrder`) rather than bare `Player`. The separate `GET /v1/team/{teamId}/roster` endpoint and the `Roster` schema were **removed**; `PUT /lineup` now returns the updated `Team`. `GET /v1/league` likewise exposes lineup slots per player.  
**Rationale**: The two read endpoints each carried half of "a team's state w.r.t. players" — `/team/{id}` had team context but no lineup, `/roster` had lineup but no team context — forcing clients to call both and stitch. A player's lineup slot is part of the team's state; there should be one endpoint. (Same overload-elimination principle as #013.)  
**Trade-off**: `GET /team` and `GET /league` now hydrate lineup fields for every player even when a caller only wanted names; trivial at 40 teams, and a lean projection can be added later if it ever matters. `PUT /lineup` and `DELETE /{playerId}` are unchanged (they are actions, not redundant reads).  
**Alternatives considered**: Keep both as summary vs. detail tiers (rejected — two endpoints to keep coherent, no real consumer for the split); fold team context into `/roster` instead (rejected — `Team` is the more fundamental resource).

### 016 — Roster size caps; signed players default to `INACTIVE`
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision**: A team's roster is capped at **15 active** (every `lineupRole` except `MINORS`) + **5 minors**. Signing a free agent (`POST /v1/team/{teamId}/{playerId}`) now defaults the new `player_team.lineupRole` to **`INACTIVE`** ("on the active roster, not yet slotted") and rejects with **409** when the active roster is already full. The lineup PUT also re-checks both caps over the *resulting* roster (request overlaid on current, omitted players keep their role) and rejects with **400** if active > 15 or minors > 5. Caps live as constants `MAX_ACTIVE_ROSTER` / `MAX_MINORS` in `GametimeServiceImp`.  
**Rationale**: The sign endpoint had no role to set, leaving a just-signed player in a fourth, null-bucket state that size limits couldn't count. Defaulting to `INACTIVE` (chosen over a new `RESERVE` value or splitting `lineupRole` into a separate `rosterStatus` field) eliminates the null without reversing #013/#014's single-field model. The seed data made this safe with no migration: all 422 `roster.csv` rows already carry a bucket (zero nulls), `INACTIVE` was referenced nowhere but the enum decl, and every seeded team is 9–12 players — under the 15 cap, so no grandfathering. The active cap is enforced at *both* sign and lineup PUT so role shuffles can't grow the active roster past what signing allows.  
**Trade-off**: `INACTIVE` now carries a slight double meaning — "just signed, unslotted" and the eventual in-game "dressed but not playing"; acceptable since the latter has no consumer yet. Position min/max is still unbuilt (deferred to the lineup PUT where the full assignment is visible).  
**Alternatives considered**: Add a dedicated `RESERVE` lineupRole (rejected — extra enum value for a state `INACTIVE` already covers); split `lineupRole` into membership-tier + in-game-slot fields (rejected — partially reverses #013/#014 and still needs a sign-time default); treat null as a first-class "unassigned" state (rejected — pushes null-handling into every count + the engine).

### 017 — No position min/max roster rules (won't build)
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision**: Roster construction is **not** constrained by player position. There are no per-position (or position-group) minimums or maximums on a roster or lineup. A team may carry any positional mix it likes.  
**Rationale**: Two reasons. (1) The seed data can't support minimums — no team carries all 9 positions (each has 6–8 of 9), and CG/WING are thin league-wide, so any per-position floor would invalidate most seeded rosters on day one (same trap avoided in #016). (2) Maximums solve a problem the game engine should own: a lopsided roster (e.g. 10 centers) is naturally punished by losing — poor guard play, no spacing — rather than by an API validation rule. Pre-empting that with a roster-layer cap fabricates a constraint ahead of any consumer, against the "let the engine decide" principle (cf. #014 deferring rotationOrder to the engine).  
**Trade-off**: A user *can* build a nonsensical roster. Accepted — it's their loss in simulation, and it keeps the roster API free of balance rules that belong in gameplay.  
**Revisit if**: the game engine ships and playtesting shows unconstrained rosters produce degenerate or unfun results that the simulation alone doesn't correct — then position rules become an engine-informed feature, not a guessed-at one.

### 018 — Coach attributes are continuous (1–20, avg 10), not categorical enums
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision**: Resolves design decision **#3**. Coach decision-making is modeled as **continuous numeric attributes on the 1–20 / avg-10 scale** (the same scale as player attributes, #008), **not** as categorical style enums (`FAST | BALANCED | SLOW`, etc.). Any categorical "archetype" label (e.g. "modern offense") is **derived on read** from the numbers for UI/legibility — the model itself is never categorical. The attribute set and engine-facing interface are specified in [coach.md](coach.md).  
**Rationale**: The only consumer that matters is the Phase 3 game engine, and the engine fundamentally wants *numbers*. With continuous attributes, a coaching effect is arithmetic — `teamPace = basePace × f(pace)` where `f` is a formula over the deviation from 10 — so coach and player numbers **compose by multiplication with no translation layer**, the same deviation-from-10 machinery the skill calculator already uses is reusable, and league balance is a single tuning coefficient per effect rather than a hand-filled table. An enum model can't escape this: `basePace × ?` always needs a number, so each enum value would have to carry a hidden coefficient — reinventing the continuous scale with an extra layer of indirection and a second source of truth (the enum *and* its lookup table) to keep coherent. Enum's one genuine advantage — human legibility ("this is a press defense") — is something the engine never reads and the derived archetype label recovers for free. Continuous also keeps **Phase 6 coach progression** open at zero cost today (nudge a number vs. jump discontinuously across buckets); since whether coaches develop is undecided, the continuous model is the one that doesn't force that decision now.  
**Trade-off**: Continuous can store precision the sim may not render (pace 12 vs 13). Mitigated two ways: at season scale a ~1% pace delta is real accumulated separation (~1.5 possessions/game × 82), and coaches will be **seeded with a deliberately wide spread** (not clustered at 9–11) so 40 coaches feel as distinct as enums would — recovering enum's distinctiveness without its lumpiness. Multi-attribute interactions (e.g. shot mix depending on both scheme and pace) stay a single formula over two numbers rather than a combinatorial enum matrix.  
**Alternatives considered**: **Enum-as-truth + derived numbers** (hand-author ~40 coaches as archetypes, expand to modifiers via lookup) — rejected: best for distinctiveness but kills smooth progression and reinvents continuous-with-indirection for the engine. **Pure enum, no numbers** — rejected: the possession math has nowhere to plug a label. **Continuous but timid seed** (cluster around 10 like the original #008 player drift) — rejected: produces a blur of near-identical coaches; the wide-spread seed is part of this decision. **A coarser 1–10 scale** (vs. matching the player 1–20) — rejected: the engine reads coach and player attributes in the *same* arithmetic (`basePace × f(coach.pace)`), so a second scale reintroduces the translation layer this decision chose continuous attributes to eliminate, forks the skill-calculator's avg-10 deviation helper (#006), and re-coarsens a population we explicitly want spread (#008). The "easier to author" appeal of 1–10 mostly evaporates once coaches are *sampled*, not hand-picked.

### 019 — No API pagination (not needed at current scale)
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision**: The API is **not** paginated. The `pageNumber`/`pageSize` parameters defined in `components.parameters` of the OpenAPI spec are orphaned — no operation `$ref`s them, and nothing is wired in the generated stubs or hand-written code. They are left in the spec as-is rather than deleted.  
**Rationale**: No endpoint returns a large collection. The biggest collection served is 40 teams (`GET /v1/league`); a team's roster is bounded at ~15–20 players (`GET /v1/team/{teamId}`), and players are fetched one at a time by ID — there is no endpoint that returns all 422 players in a single response. The 422-player count is a red herring: players are partitioned by team, never listed flat. At this scale, shipping the full collection and letting the client display it (table, virtualized scroll, etc.) is correct; server-side pagination would add machinery with no payload to justify it.  
**Trade-off**: Later phases will have genuinely large result sets — Phase 4 stats (per-season game logs, league leaders), Phase 4 play-by-play (hundreds of events/game), and Phase 5 schedule (an ~82-game × 40-team season). Those endpoints, when built, are where pagination (or filtering/date-range bounding) becomes real. The orphaned param definitions are kept so they're ready to wire then.  
**Alternatives considered**: Wire pagination on the existing list endpoints now (rejected — no collection is large enough to need it); delete the orphaned params (rejected — harmless, and a ready starting point for Phase 4/5).

### 020 — Game domain model: persist every GameEvent; minimal Game/BoxScore/GameEvent shape
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision**: Resolves roadmap Design Decision **#9** and defines the Phase 3.1 Game model (the data a simulated game produces; the engine that fills it is §3.2+). Five facets of one cohesive modeling choice:
- **Event persistence (the headline call): every `GameEvent` is persisted.** Play-by-play (§3.6 `GET /{gameId}/play-by-play`) replays from stored rows; it is not re-simulated. The `BoxScore` is **accumulated during simulation** and reconciled against the event log, with **events as the source of truth**.
- **`Game` status lifecycle**: `GameStatus { SCHEDULED, IN_PROGRESS, FINAL }` — modeled now (FINAL needed by §3.6 simulate, SCHEDULED by Phase 5). Kept minimal: no CANCELLED/POSTPONED/OT-specific states until a consumer exists.
- **`Game` references home/away `team` only — no season FK.** No schedule/season table exists yet (Phase 5); the link is not fabricated ahead of its consumer.
- **`BoxScore` is keyed `(game_id, player_id)`** with FKs to `game` and `player` and the §4.1 stat counters. **No `team_id` on the row** — the player's team is derivable from `game` (home/away) + `player_team`; storing it would be a third copy of that fact. "Team in *this* game" across a mid-season trade is derivable from `player_team_hist` by date if ever needed.
- **`GameEvent` shape is intentionally minimal and additive**: `game_id`, `sequence`, `period`, `offense_team_id`, `defense_team_id`, `play_type` (enum `SHOT, TURNOVER, REBOUND, FOUL, FREE_THROW`), `outcome` (free text for now), `primary_player_id`. **`sequence` is monotonic across the whole game and does not restart per period** (play-by-play is one `ORDER BY sequence` read). **No in-game clock column** yet (only meaningful once §3.2 decides per-possession time elapse — additive later). Richer participation (assister/defender/rebounder) is additive when §3.2 defines the possession flow.  
**Rationale**: A possession-by-possession simulation is the core product (roadmap §3.2); discarding the event log would make play-by-play a non-faithful re-simulation and leave Phase 4 stats unable to reconcile against actual plays. Storage (~150–200 events/game) is cheap relative to that value, and #019 already names play-by-play as the large result set that motivates pagination at the *read* endpoint — so the volume is bounded at §3.6/Phase 4, not by dropping data. The minimal/additive shaping (one primary player, free-text outcome, no clock, no season FK) follows the #014/#017 discipline: shape the data the consumer will need, don't guess the algorithm or fabricate columns ahead of a live consumer. Single-`sequence` ordering keeps the play-by-play read a trivial total order instead of a fragile `(period, sequence)` sort. Keeping team off the box-score row avoids the duplicate-source trap of #013/#015.  
**Trade-off**: Event-row volume grows with games played (mitigated by paginating the read, not the write — #019). The minimal `GameEvent` will need real work when §3.2 lands (more participants, enum outcomes, possibly a clock) — accepted as additive migrations rather than a guessed-at full shape now. `primary_player_id` alone can't express assists/contests yet — those events lose detail until §3.2 extends the model. **API surface deferred**: `Game`/`GameEvent`/`BoxScore` get no OpenAPI schemas or endpoints in §3.1 (persistence + mapping only); they land in §3.6 with their real consumers (`POST /simulate`, `GET /{gameId}`, `.../play-by-play`), avoiding orphaned schemas (cf. the orphaned pagination params, #019).  
**Alternatives considered**: **Box-score-only persistence** (cheaper storage, but no replayable play-by-play — §3.6 would re-simulate non-deterministically unless a seed were stored) — rejected; events are the product. **`sequence` restarting per period** — rejected (forces a compound sort for no benefit). **`team_id` on box_score** / **season FK on game** / **in-game clock column now** — all rejected as fabricating data ahead of a consumer (#014/#017). **Defining the OpenAPI schemas up front** — rejected as orphaning them until §3.6 wires the operations.

### 021 — Possession engine (§3.2): seeded RNG, configurable possession count, logistic-contest probabilities, skill-weighted selection, game-level transactional simulate()
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision A — RNG / determinism**: The Phase 3.2 possession engine sources randomness from a **seeded `java.util.random.RandomGenerator`**, where the **seed is a `long` parameter on the `simulate(...)` call** (not a Spring-injected bean, not a properties-file value). The method builds a generator from that seed and threads it through the possession loop and every resolver (shot / turnover / foul). Same seed + same teams + same pace ⇒ a byte-identical game. **Tests** pass a fixed seed (e.g. `simulate("BOS","LAL",seed=42,pace=100)`); **production / the §3.6 endpoint** passes a *fresh* seed each call (e.g. `System.nanoTime()` or a random `long`) so real games vary. The seed is **not persisted** in §3.2.  
**Decision B — Possession granularity / clock model**: A game is **possession-by-possession with an abstract possession count** (not a ticking second-clock). The possession count / **pace is a configurable input to `simulate()`** (the calibration knob — tune until simulated box scores resemble real games; roadmap §3.4 anticipates this tuning). `period` + the game-wide `sequence` (#020) order events; possession count bounds the game. **Event time is derived, not stored, in §3.2**: each event's clock/elapsed time is a pure function of that game's pace + `period` + `sequence`, so it is **computed on read** (e.g. by §3.6 play-by-play) for display — §3.2 adds **no time column** (consistent with #020's "no in-game clock column"). **Persisting a per-event time column, and its shape (single value vs. an explicit range), are DEFERRED to §3.6**, where the play-by-play *display* is the consumer that reads it; it lands then as one small migration populated by the engine.  
**Decision C — Probability formula shape (constants tunable, not binding)**: Shot/turnover/foul resolution uses a **logistic contest model over the avg-10 deviation** the skill system already uses: `p = base(path) + SENSITIVITY * (offenseSkill − defenseSkill) / 10`, clamped to a sane range (e.g. `[0.02, 0.97]`). Two average players (10 vs 10) yield exactly `base`. **All constants** — `base` per shot/turnover/foul/FT path, `SENSITIVITY`, and the pace from Decision B — **live in one injectable `SimConfig`** (or constants holder), never magic numbers in the resolvers. Skill→path mapping: rim (`drive`/`finishing` vs `rimProtection`), perimeter (`perimeter` vs `individualDefense`/`shotContest`), three (`longRange` vs `shotContest`), post (`post` vs `individualDefense`); turnover (`ballSecurity` vs `stealing`/`individualDefense`); foul (`foulDrawing` vs `foulProne`) → free throws via `freeThrows`. **The specific starting numbers are suggested placeholders, NOT part of this decision** (see todo.md §3.2 for the suggested table); the implementing session picks them and **§3.4 rebalances them empirically** by simulating games. §3.2's acceptance bar is *plausible / bounded / reconciling* output (believable score, average-vs-average ≈ base rates, lopsided matchup the better team wins, box-score points reconcile with the event log) — explicitly **not** NBA accuracy.  
**Decision D — Shot selection**: Each possession picks the shooter by a **skill-weighted random draw** over the 5 on-floor offensive players (higher offensive skill ⇒ more attempts), then picks that player's **shot type** by a weighted draw over their relative `drive`/`finishing`/`perimeter`/`post`/`longRange` skills. Both draws use the seeded RNG (Decision A) so selection is reproducible. **Raw skill weighting only** — `acumen`-driven shot *quality* and `passing`-driven ball movement are **§3.4** and are not read here. **Assists are deferred: `BoxScore.assists` stays 0 in §3.2**; assist attribution is a §3.4 ball-movement model, not a trivial "assist on every made shot."  
**Decision E — Output contract / transaction boundary**: `simulate(homeTeamId, awayTeamId, seed, pace)` is a **single game-level `@Transactional` service method** that runs a whole game and **persists atomically** — the `Game` (status `FINAL`, period + final scores), all `GameEvent`s, and all `BoxScore`s via `GameRepo`/`GameEventRepo`/`BoxScoreRepo`. A game is the atomic unit (no "simulate a quarter" — box/final scores only exist on completion). It **returns a small result object** (`gameId` + final score), not the bare id (so the engine's own tests assert without a DB read-back) and not the full event list (persisted, re-readable). The **pure simulation** (loop + resolvers + math) lives in **side-effect-free classes that return data**, unit-testable with no DB; persistence happens **only at the service boundary**. **Configurable retrieval — game / quarter / half / N events / pagination — is a §3.6 read-endpoint concern, NOT an engine return mode**: events are persisted (#020), so any slice is a `findByGameId...` query, and the front end requests whatever window it wants from `GET /{gameId}/play-by-play` (#019 already earmarks it for pagination there).  
**Rationale**: *(A)* Determinism is a *correctness/testability* requirement, not polish — the per-package JaCoCo gate (80% line) can't be met for a branchy probability engine whose outcomes can't be reproduced and asserted. The seed belongs to the *call* because a game is the unit of randomness: a per-call parameter lets each test pin its own value to drive a specific branch (high-skill make vs. low-skill miss), whereas a single global seed in `application-*.properties` would force every test (and every production game) to share one value. *(B)* Pace is an *input*, same as the seed: tuning it doesn't break seed-replay, it simply defines a different (still reproducible) game. A persisted game is **frozen history** — retuning pace afterward only affects *future* games, never a stored one — so a derived-at-simulation-time event time carries **no staleness risk** even once it is stored. We defer the stored-time column (and single-vs-range shape) because §3.2 has **no consumer that reads it** (play-by-play display is §3.6); adding it now would fabricate a half-decided schema ahead of its consumer (#014/#017). #020 explicitly left the clock column as "additive when a consumer needs it" — §3.6 is that consumer. *(C)* The logistic-contest shape over avg-10 deviation keeps engine and skill layers on **one scale with no translation** (the #018 win) and makes every constant a meaning you can move; `base` at deviation 0 means an average-vs-average matchup lands at a known league rate by construction. We lock the *shape* but not the *numbers* because the numbers are guesses either way and the only honest way to set them is empirically — which the roadmap already assigns to §3.4 ("rebalance formulas once possessions exercise them"). The decision that actually matters is putting all constants in one `SimConfig`: that is the precondition for the §3.4 calibration loop (Decision B's tunable pace lives there too); scattered magic numbers would make tuning impossible. §3.2 needs *plausible/bounded/reconciling*, not accurate — accuracy is unattainable without sim output to calibrate against, so requiring it now would be backwards. *(D)* Skill-weighted selection is the simplest model that makes better offensive players shoot more (the behavior that matters for plausible box scores) without fabricating a possession-design model; it's a function the §3.4 coach/chemistry layer can later bend (shot distribution, `acumen` quality) through the same weighting seam. Deferring assists avoids inventing a ball-movement model with no real basis now — 0 is honestly "not modeled yet," whereas a reflexive assist-per-make would bake in a wrong number that later code must unwind. *(E)* A game is the atomic unit of simulation, so the engine returns a game-level result and persists everything once; building "return a quarter / N events" into `simulate()` would push a *read/projection* concern into a *write/compute* method and fabricate a return mode with no §3.2 consumer (the read endpoint is §3.6) — events are persisted, so slicing is a query, free to the front end later. Separating the pure sim from persistence is what lets the branchy math be unit-tested without a DB, which is how the per-package coverage gate gets met.  
**Trade-off**: *(A)* Callers must supply a seed (slightly more ceremony than a hidden global); a fixed production seed would be a bug (identical games), so §3.6 must vary it. *(B)* Until §3.6, play-by-play time is computed on read rather than stored; accepted — it's display-only and cheap to derive. The single-vs-range decision is consciously left open. *(C)* The starting constants will almost certainly produce unrealistic distributions until §3.4 tunes them; accepted by design — §3.2 ships a *correct, tunable* engine, not a calibrated one. *(D)* Box scores show 0 assists until §3.4; accepted — an explicit "not modeled yet" beats a fabricated number. *(E)* `simulate()` returns only a small result, not event slices; accepted — slicing is the §3.6 read endpoint's job and costs nothing to defer since events are persisted.  
**Alternatives considered**: *(A)* **Seed in `application-*.properties`** — rejected: one global value can't let individual tests choose seeds to drive branches, and would make all games identical unless overridden. **Inject a `RandomGenerator` bean** — rejected: app-scoped rather than game-scoped, awkward for the "same seed ⇒ same game" test. **`Math.random()` / un-seeded `new Random()`** — rejected: un-seedable, untestable. *(B)* **Ticking real-time clock + stored per-event timestamp now** — rejected: reverses #020 and fabricates schema with no §3.2 consumer. **Create the two time columns now, decide their meaning later** — rejected: leaves half-decided, possibly mis-named columns and engine code written ahead of the decision; deferring the whole thing to §3.6 defers the same choice without the schema cost. *(C)* **Hard-author "realistic" NBA constants up front** — rejected: requires expertise the project doesn't have and can't be validated without sim output; §3.4 calibration is the real path. **Magic numbers inline in the resolvers** — rejected: makes §3.4 tuning impossible; constants must be in one place. **A more elaborate model (true logistic `1/(1+e^-x)`, multi-factor regressions)** — deferred: the linear-deviation form is simpler to reason about and tune now; can be swapped behind the same `SimConfig` seam later if §3.4 shows it's needed. *(D)* **Fixed "best player shoots every time"** — rejected: produces degenerate one-man box scores. **Assist-on-every-made-shot** — rejected: fabricates a ball-movement number with no basis (deferred to §3.4). **Possession-design / set-play model** — deferred: far more than §3.2 needs; weighted draw is sufficient for plausible output. *(E)* **Configurable return slices on `simulate()`** (return a quarter / half / N events) — rejected: a read concern, belongs on §3.6's `play-by-play` endpoint, no §3.2 consumer. **Return the full event list from `simulate()`** — rejected: events are persisted and re-readable; a small result (id + score) suffices. **Persist inside the pure-sim classes** — rejected: couples math to a DB and breaks no-DB unit testing.  
**Status of §3.2 decisions**: A–E are all resolved by this entry. The remaining pre-code doc task is filling in game.md's `GameEvent` possession-flow detail (todo.md §3.2 task 1).  
**§3.6 follow-up (carry forward)**: stored per-event time column — single value vs. range — to be decided and migrated when §3.6 play-by-play needs it.

### 022 — Team chemistry & coaching effects (§3.4): avg-10 deviation modifiers, first-class reconcilable assists (assist_player_id), acumen/team-efficiency hooks, a calibration harness, and a rotation-attr scope fence
**Date**: 2026-06  
**Decision A — Coach/chemistry modifier shape**: A coach attribute (or team-chemistry skill) bends a baseline value through the **avg-10 deviation multiplier** the skill system already uses (#008/#018/#021), so coach numbers and player numbers compose with no translation layer:
```
effectiveValue = baseValue × (1 + COACH_SENSITIVITY × (attr − 10) / 10)
```
An attribute of 10 ⇒ ×1.0 (no effect). Start with a **single** `COACH_SENSITIVITY` constant in `SimConfig`; split per-effect only if calibration (Decision D) shows one knob can't fit all effects. All new constants live in `SimConfig` (the #021 discipline), never inline. **Pace sub-question (settled while building)**: `pace` scales the **possession count** (the realistic reading — it changes how many possessions a game runs, the natural seam at `PossessionEngine.simulate()`'s loop bound), not merely shot-clock urgency.

**Decision B — Assist model (the scope-defining call): B1, assists are first-class and reconcilable.** A nullable **`assist_player_id`** column is added to `game_event` (the assister participant) — the **first schema change since §3.1**, so §3.4 DOES touch the schema (unlike §3.3): a Liquibase change (appended to the unreleased `release.1.0.4.game.sql`), a `GameEventEntity` field, and event persistence in `GameSimulator`. **OpenAPI is deferred to §3.6** (no `GameEvent` model field or operation here — surfacing the assister in the API lands with play-by-play). **Attribution**: on a **made field goal**, roll whether it was assisted (probability scaled by the other on-floor offensive players' `passing` / team `teamOffense`); if assisted, **pick the assister by weighted `passing` draw** over the other 4 offensive players (the shooter is excluded), mirroring `ShotSelector.pickShooter`. Credit that player an assist (`recordAssist()`) and stamp `assist_player_id` on the SHOT event. **Not every make is assisted.** `BoxScore.assists` is derived from / reconciled against the count of made-SHOT events carrying an `assist_player_id` (events are the source of truth, #020); the integration test extends the existing points/rebound reconciliation to assists.

**Decision C — Where acumen / teamOffense / teamDefense enter resolution**: Keep the individual skill contests (#021) as they are; layer two new modest modifiers, both using the Decision-A form. **`acumen` → a small shot-make-probability modifier in `ShotResolver`** (better shot selection ⇒ higher-quality looks ⇒ a modest make bonus — it bends the make rate, NOT the shot-type draw). **`teamOffense` / `teamDefense` → a single possession-level efficiency multiplier** (the roadmap's "overall team efficiency" deliverable), applied once per possession rather than folded into every individual contest. Both are a thumb on the scale over the player-skill contest, not a replacement; sensitivities live in `SimConfig` and are tuned in calibration. `PlayerGameState` extracts `acumen` / `teamOffense` / `teamDefense` / `passing` (same way §3.3 added rebound skills).

**Decision D — Calibration approach: D1, build a harness.** §3.4 is the empirical-tuning phase (#021, roadmap §3.4): the placeholder `SimConfig` `BASE_*` rates plus the new §3.4 coach/chemistry sensitivities get tuned against real-basketball benchmarks. Build a small **calibration harness** — a disabled-by-default test (reusing the `@SpringBootTest` H2 setup, kept off the coverage-gate path) that simulates N games over the seeded league and reports aggregate distributions (avg team points, FG%, 3P%, reb%, assists/game, TO/game). Tune the constants watching the aggregates converge; keep the harness for future re-tuning. **Agreed target benchmarks (modern NBA, per team per game)**: ~112 points, ~47% FG, ~36% 3P, ~26 assists, ~14 turnovers. **⚠️ SUPERSEDED as the source of truth (2026-08, §3.12): the targets now live in [`calibration.md`](calibration.md), which is authoritative — this sentence is historical, recording what §3.4 first agreed.** Two of the five (**points** and **FG%**) are flagged CONTESTED there: both were set here from unsourced estimates, and current figures suggest points may be ~114–117 and FG% ~47–48. Read calibration.md before treating any of these five as settled. "Calibrated" is verified against these via the harness, not by eye. §3.4 ships a *calibrated-ish, tunable* engine, not a perfect one.

**Decision E — Scope fence: rotation attributes are §3.5, not §3.4.** §3.4 reads `pace`, `offensiveScheme`, `defensiveScheme` ONLY. `rotationDepth` and `substitutionAggressiveness` are coach attributes but their consumers are **§3.5** (minutes / fatigue / substitution) — coach.md tags them §3.5. §3.4 must NOT read them (the same discipline §3.3 held on fatigue/blocks). Starters play the whole game in §3.4; no minutes model.

**Rationale**: *(A)* Reusing the avg-10 deviation form is the #018 win carried into the engine — coach and player numbers multiply on one scale with no lookup table, and league balance is one tuning coefficient per effect. A single sensitivity to start keeps the calibration surface small; splitting is a calibration-driven follow-up, not a guessed-at up-front complication. Pace-as-possession-count is the honest reading: a faster coach plays *more* possessions, which the loop bound already expresses — bending only shot urgency would model tempo without the box-score consequence (more shots, more points) that pace is supposed to produce. *(B)* Assists were left at 0 in §3.2 as an explicit "not modeled yet" (#021 Decision D) rather than a fabricated assist-per-make; §3.4 is the ball-movement phase, so they become real now. Making them first-class + reconcilable (B1) keeps events the source of truth (#020): `BoxScore.assists` is a derived count, not an independent accumulator that could silently disagree with the play-by-play. The weighted-`passing` draw mirrors the established `pickShooter`/`pickOffensiveRebounder` selection seam, so the attribution model adds no new machinery. The column lands on `game_event` (not a new table) because an assist is a participant in an existing SHOT event, not its own event. *(C)* Layering `acumen` as a make-rate nudge (not a shot-type reweight) matches its meaning — shot *quality*, not shot *choice* — and keeps it from double-counting against the existing weighted shot-type draw. Folding `teamOffense`/`teamDefense` into one possession-level multiplier (rather than every individual contest) is both the roadmap's "overall team efficiency" framing and cheaper to reason about and tune — one knob, applied once. Keeping all three modest preserves the #021 principle that the player-skill contest is the engine; chemistry is a thumb on the scale. *(D)* Without an instrument to observe aggregate output, "calibrated" is unfalsifiable (risks.md "Seed data realism") — D1 builds the observation instrument the #021/roadmap calibration mandate assumed. Disabled-by-default keeps the N-game run off the normal build and out of the coverage gate while reusing the existing H2 integration-test setup. Agreeing the benchmark numbers up front makes "calibrated" a checkable claim rather than a judgment call. *(E)* The rotation attributes have no §3.4 consumer — their consumers are §3.5 minutes/fatigue — so reading them now would pull §3.5 work forward and fabricate behavior ahead of its consumer (the #014/#017 discipline §3.3 also held).  
**Trade-off**: *(A)* A single sensitivity may not fit every effect equally; accepted as a starting point, splittable in calibration. Making the loop bound coach-derived is a structural change to `PossessionEngine.simulate()`. *(B)* B1 adds the first migration since §3.1 (H2/Postgres dual-compatibility risk, risks.md) and threads an assister through the event record + persistence; existing dev Postgres DBs need `docker compose down -v` to pick up the column (#009). Adding the assist roll consumes from the seeded RNG, shifting the stream — existing seed-pinned determinism assertions re-baseline. *(C)* Two more modifiers widen the calibration surface; kept modest and single-knob to contain it. *(D)* The harness is a maintained test artifact; benchmarks are a judgment even when agreed (the seed data itself may be unrealistic). *(E)* Starters play 48 minutes with no fatigue until §3.5 — an accepted, explicit gap.  
**Alternatives considered**: *(A)* **Per-effect sensitivities from the start** — rejected as premature; calibration decides if one knob suffices. **Pace as shot-urgency only** (fixed possession count) — rejected: models tempo without its scoring consequence. *(B)* **B2, assists as a derived box-score number with no event participant** (no schema change) — rejected: not reconcilable against the event log, breaking the #020 events-are-truth contract; a play-by-play could show a made shot the box score credits an assist for with no assister on any event. **Assist-on-every-made-shot** — rejected (the same fabricated number #021 declined). **A separate assist event row** — rejected: an assist is a participant in the SHOT, not an independent play, and a second row would double the made-FG event count and complicate reconciliation. *(C)* **`acumen` reweights the shot-type draw** — rejected: that models shot *selection*, double-counting against the existing draw; `acumen` is quality, so it bends the make rate. **Fold team efficiency into every individual contest** — rejected: more expensive and harder to tune than one possession-level multiplier. *(D)* **D2, guess-and-check against the existing pass/fail plausibility bounds** — rejected: the bounds assert plausibility, not realism, so you can't *see* the distribution you're tuning toward; the user explicitly wanted a fast automated observe-and-converge loop. **A standalone `main()` runner** — considered; the disabled-by-default test was chosen to reuse the `@SpringBootTest` H2 setup. *(E)* **Read rotation attrs now for a partial minutes model** — rejected: pulls §3.5 forward and fabricates a consumer.  
**Status of §3.4 decisions**: A–E are all resolved by this entry (mirrors how #021 captured §3.2). The pace-vs-loop and harness-form sub-questions are settled above.  
**§3.6 follow-up (carry forward)**: surface `assist_player_id` in the `GameEvent` OpenAPI model + play-by-play read endpoint when §3.6 builds them.

### 023 — Minutes, fatigue & substitution (§3.5): derived minutes from possession share, single-energy fatigue multiplier, deterministic starter-priority substitution, foul-outs as a derived predicate, and a within-game (schema-free) scope fence
**Date**: 2026-07  
**Decision A — Minutes model: A1, possession-share → derived minutes.** The engine keeps no second-clock (#021) — it stays clock-free. Each on-floor player accumulates a **possession counter** while on the floor; at box-score-write time the game's total possessions map to `PERIODS × 12` (+5 per OT) minutes by ratio, and each player is attributed their share. **Minutes are a display projection of possession share**, computed at write time — consistent with #021's "event time is derived, not stored." No schema change (`box_score.minutes` already exists; §3.5 replaces `setMinutes(0)`). Team minutes sum to `5 × game-minutes` by construction.

**Decision B — Fatigue model: single energy → one skill multiplier.** A single per-player **`currentEnergy`** on `PlayerGameState`. It **drains per on-floor possession** (drain scaled by `endurance` — high endurance drains slower) and **recovers while benched** (recovery scaled by the `energy` attribute). Its only effect is **one fatigue multiplier over the player's skills** at contest time (`effectiveSkill = skill × fatigueFactor(energy)`) — the same avg-10 "modify a baseline, don't replace it" discipline as #021/#022, kept a **modest thumb on the scale** (not a cliff), applied to the player's skills broadly rather than a physical-only subset. All constants in `SimConfig`, tuned via the calibration harness (Decision E).

> **Implementation note — flat starting energy (deviation from the wording above).**
> As built, every player **tips off at a full tank** (`currentEnergy = MAX_ENERGY`),
> *not* a value "derived from" endurance/energy at construction. The two attributes
> act **per possession** instead: `endurance` slows the on-floor drain, `energy`
> speeds the benched recovery. Rationale: "within-game fatigue only, resets each
> game" (Decision F) means everyone should start equally fresh, and a flat start
> keeps the fatigue multiplier interpretable — `fatigueFactor(MAX_ENERGY) == ×1.0`
> is "a full tank" for *everyone*, rather than a per-player max that would make ×1.0
> mean different things for different players. This was a deliberate, user-approved
> call (2026-07) recorded here so the code and the decision don't silently disagree.

**Decision C — Substitution trigger: deterministic, between-possessions, starter-priority.** A **between-possessions** substitution check (never mid-possession), **deterministic given (energy, fouls, coach attrs, lineupRole, rotationOrder)** so it consumes **NO RNG draw** (§3.4 seed-pinned determinism only shifts because the on-floor five itself changes, not from a new draw). Each check: drain the on-floor five's energy, recover the benched, force off any fouled-out player, then swap the most-tired eligible on-floor player below a `substitutionAggressiveness`-scaled energy threshold for the freshest eligible bench player, drawing down the `rotationOrder` queue only as far as `rotationDepth` allows; rested players become eligible to return. **Star/starter retention**: a player's `lineupRole` (STARTER) + `rotationOrder` give sub priority — **starters tolerate more fatigue before being pulled and return first** — reusing existing #014 roster data (no new attribute), producing the real pattern where stars log the most minutes.

**Decision D — Coach attribute mapping: extend `CoachModifiers`, avg-10 form.** `CoachModifiers` gains `rotationDepthFactor()` / `subAggressivenessFactor()`, both the #022 avg-10 deviation form (`1 + COACH_SENSITIVITY × (attr − 10)/10`), reusing the single `COACH_SENSITIVITY` (split only if calibration needs it). `rotationDepth` → how far down the `rotationOrder` bench queue the engine goes (tight vs. deep); `substitutionAggressiveness` → how early a tired starter is pulled (scales the energy threshold). `SimConfig` gains the base substitution threshold + fatigue sensitivities.

**Decision E — Calibration: extend the harness, guard §3.4.** **Extend the existing `CalibrationHarness`** (don't build a second) to also report the **per-slot minutes distribution** and a **period-by-period FG%** so the fatigue effect is visible. Agree the §3.5 minutes targets with the user before declaring calibrated (top starter ~34–36, no one over ~42, bench scaled by `rotationDepth`), **and re-confirm the §3.4 aggregates (112/47/36/26/14) still hold with fatigue on** — fatigue must not drag them off-target.

**Decision F — Scope fence + foul-outs: within-game fatigue only (schema-free), foul-outs IN.** Fatigue is **within-game only** — nothing persists to `player_team`, no cross-game/season stamina, energy resets each game (**§3.5 is schema-free for energy**). BUT **foul-outs are pulled IN** as a **second substitution cause**:
- A player reaching **6 fouls** (`SimConfig.FOUL_OUT_LIMIT`) is **disqualified** and forced off — a forced sub independent of fatigue/priority.
- **A foul-out is NOT a stored flag — it's a derived predicate** over the existing counter: `fouledOut(p) == p.getFouls() >= FOUL_OUT_LIMIT`, evaluated fresh at each between-possession check. Because the counter only grows, the disqualification is **permanent for the rest of the game for free** — no `disqualified` boolean (which would be a second source of truth for the same fact, the #013/#015 duplicate-state trap). The rotation manager treats "fouled-out" as an **eligibility filter**, the same way "on-floor"/"benched" are derived from rotation state, not stored.
- **Timing**: the 6th foul is recorded on the *defender* mid-possession, but the foul-out check runs at the **between-possession** step — the player finishes the current possession (a dead-ball sequence, realistic) and is pulled at the next check. No mid-possession removal.
- On a foul-out (or when fatigue subs run the bench dry), replace from the **full roster** (starters + entire bench, not just the `rotationDepth` window).
- **Last resort**: if every eligible player is exhausted (whole roster fouled out), the least-fouled available player stays on — **the floor never drops below 5**, so shooter/defender/rebounder picks always have 5.

**Still out of scope**: no injuries/DNPs as sub causes (foul-out + fatigue only); no §3.6 API surface (engine + box-score population only); no mid-possession subs; no cross-game/season stamina.

**Rationale**: *(A)* Reusing possession share keeps the engine clock-free (#021) — minutes are the same kind of derived-on-write projection event time is, so no schema and no staleness. *(B)* One energy value and one multiplier keeps fatigue on the same avg-10 "thumb on the scale" discipline as coach/chemistry (#022), a small calibration surface, and composes multiplicatively with the §3.4 modifiers. A single value (not physical-vs-mental) is honest to what the sim can observe and calibrate. *(C)* Determinism is gate-critical (#021 A): a substitution *decision* that reads state rather than rolling keeps the seeded stream draw-for-draw stable, so §3.4 tests only move because the five changed — a controllable, documentable shift, not stream churn. Starter-priority reuses #014's `rotationOrder`/`lineupRole` instead of fabricating a "star" attribute (#014/#017 discipline), and reproduces the real minutes pattern for free. *(D)* The avg-10 form is the #018/#022 win carried to the rotation attributes — coach numbers compose with no lookup table, one sensitivity to start. *(E)* #022 built the observation instrument; extending it (vs. a second harness) keeps one place to watch the aggregates converge, and guarding the §3.4 numbers makes "fatigue didn't break calibration" a checkable claim. *(F)* Within-game fatigue keeps §3.5 schema-free (the H2/Postgres migration risk doesn't apply) and defers season stamina until a consumer exists. Foul-outs as a derived predicate over the existing counter avoids the #013/#015 duplicate-source trap — the fact already lives in `fouls`, so a second `disqualified` flag could only disagree with it. The never-below-5 invariant protects the "exactly 5" assumption every pick site already makes, so no short-handed handling leaks into the resolvers.  
**Trade-off**: *(A)* Minutes are a projection, not a clocked measurement — accepted; §3.2/#021 already made time derived. *(B)* A single energy value can't distinguish a winded big from a fresh-legged guard beyond their `endurance`; accepted at this fidelity. *(C)* Making the on-floor five dynamic is a real structural change to `PossessionEngine.simulate()`'s loop and re-baselines any §3.4 seed-pinned score/count assertions (structural determinism tests stay valid). *(D)* One sensitivity may not fit rotation + scheme effects equally; splittable in calibration. *(E)* The harness grows more reporting to maintain. *(F)* Foul-out-forces-a-sub adds a `FOUL_OUT_LIMIT` constant + a forced-sub path and the never-below-5 fallback the rotation manager must guarantee; the degenerate all-fouled-out path needs an explicit test.  
**Alternatives considered**: *(A)* **A ticking game clock + stored per-event minutes** — rejected: reverses #021, fabricates the deferred §3.6 time column. *(B)* **Per-skill or physical-only fatigue subsets** — rejected: more calibration surface than the sim can validate; one multiplier suffices. **A sharp fatigue cliff** — rejected: not the modest-thumb discipline. *(C)* **A substitution *roll*** (random sub timing) — rejected: consumes RNG, churns the seed-pinned stream, and subs are a coaching *decision*, not chance. **A new "star"/"minutes priority" attribute** — rejected: fabricates data #014's `rotationOrder`/`lineupRole` already encode. **Mid-possession subs** — rejected: complicates the possession loop for no fidelity gain at this scale. *(D)* **Per-effect sensitivities from the start** — rejected as premature (calibration decides). *(E)* **A second §3.5 harness** — rejected: one instrument, one place to watch the aggregates. *(F)* **A stored `disqualified` boolean** — rejected: second source of truth for a fact `fouls` already holds (#013/#015). **Cross-game/season stamina now** — rejected: no consumer; would force a persisted energy column + the migration §3.5 deliberately avoids. **Dropping below 5 when the roster is exhausted** — rejected: every pick site assumes exactly 5; the last-resort keep-least-fouled invariant protects them.  
**Status of §3.5 decisions**: A–F are all resolved by this entry (mirrors #021/#022).  
**§3.6 follow-up (carry forward)**: none new — minutes surface in the box score already; the §3.6 play-by-play/time-column follow-ups from #021/#022 still stand.

### 024 — Simulation APIs (§3.6): a shared GameResult (team-split box scores), optional-and-persisted seed, an unpaginated play-by-play list, event time deferred again (now resolved as derived-on-read), and a new 422 for the same-team guard
**Date**: 2026-07  
**Scope**: §3.6 exposes the already-persisted game engine over OpenAPI — `POST /v1/game/simulate`, `GET /v1/game/{gameId}`, `GET /v1/game/{gameId}/play-by-play`. It is **API + read-projection + entity→model mapping work, not new engine logic** (the engine is done through §3.5; everything §3.6 reads already exists in the DB, #020). The **single net schema change** is Decision B's `game.seed` column. The structural crux is the **net-new entity→model mapper** for Game/GameEvent/BoxScore (`EntityMapper` maps player/team/coach but does not touch the game entities).

**Decision A — Simulate/get response shape: a shared `GameResult` with team-split box scores.** Both `POST /simulate` and `GET /game/{gameId}` return the **same `GameResult`** model: `{ game: Game, homeBoxScore: [BoxScore], awayBoxScore: [BoxScore] }`. The `Game` header carries `id`, `homeTeamId`, `awayTeamId`, `status`, `homeScore`, `awayScore`, `periods`, and `seed` (Decision B) — **no per-period line scores** (a period line is fully derivable from the event log, #020, so it isn't stored). The box scores are **split home/away server-side**: since `box_score` has no `team_id` (#020 — derivable from game + `player_team`), the mapper resolves each row's team at map time. The **event list is NOT in `GameResult`** — play-by-play is its own endpoint (Decision D), so the simulate/get payload stays bounded (~30 stat lines, not hundreds of events).

**Decision B — Seed: optional input, and now PERSISTED (reverses #021 A's non-persist).** `POST /simulate` accepts an **optional** `seed`; omitted ⇒ the engine generates a fresh random `long` (preserving #021 A's "real games vary"), provided ⇒ a reproducible run. **The seed is persisted** on `Game` — a new `seed BIGINT` column appended to the unreleased `release.1.0.4.game.sql` (NOT a new release — the memory note; append here), written by `GameSimulator` at persist time, and **echoed back on the `Game` header** in `GameResult`. This **revises decisions.md #021 A**, which left the seed unpersisted: the "fresh seed per real game" rule is intact (random default), we now additionally *record* what was rolled so a game is **reproducible from its own record**.

**Decision C — Pace input: not exposed.** `POST /simulate` does **not** expose `possessionsPerPeriod`; the endpoint always uses `SimConfig.DEFAULT_POSSESSIONS_PER_PERIOD`. Baseline pace is a **development-time calibration concern** (the `CalibrationHarness` calls the engine directly and bakes the answer into `SimConfig`); per-game tempo variety is already produced by the coach `pace` attribute scaling the count (#022 A). A client has no legitimate use for the raw knob. Additive later if a "quick-sim/exhibition" consumer ever wants it.

**Decision D — Play-by-play: a plain ordered list, no pagination.** `GET /game/{gameId}/play-by-play` returns a **flat `[GameEvent]` in `sequence` order** (reusing `GameEventRepo.findByGameIdOrderBySequenceAsc` as-is — no `Pageable` finder, no page-envelope schema). Event volume is **~200–350 events/game** (counted: 100 base possessions × ~2 events, plus second-chance possessions) — a modest single response (well under ~100KB JSON), and a play-by-play UI wants the whole game anyway (scroll/filter/jump). The `GameEvent` model surfaces `assistPlayerId` (the §3.4 column, #022 B — its deferred-to-§3.6 API follow-up lands here). The orphaned `pageNumber`/`pageSize` params (#019) **stay orphaned** — the seam is left open; a `period` query filter is noted as the likelier first real need, both additive later.

**Decision E — Per-event time column: deferred AGAIN, now as a resolved "derived-on-read" decision (closes #021 B).** §3.6 ships **no time field and no time column** — `GameEvent` carries `period` + `sequence`, which suffices to render an ordered, period-grouped play-by-play. Any display clock is **computed on read** from `period` + `sequence` + pace. This **closes** #021 B's "for §3.6 to decide": the decision is *derived, not stored*. The explicit revisit trigger is the **Phase 7 game view** — if that UI shows a per-event clock, *it* decides scalar-vs-range and elapsed-vs-remaining and only then adds a column (if computed-on-read proves insufficient). Consequence: **Decision B's `game.seed` is the ONLY §3.6 schema change.**

**Decision F — Error contract: 404 for unknown ids, and a NEW 422 for the same-team guard.** `GET /game/{gameId}` and `GET .../play-by-play` return **404** on unknown `gameId`; `POST /simulate` returns **404** on an unknown `homeTeamId`/`awayTeamId` (the engine already throws `ResourceNotFoundException`, #021 E — free). `POST /simulate` also **validates `homeTeamId != awayTeamId`** and returns **422 Unprocessable Entity** on a same-team request — a **new `ResourceUnprocessableException` + `ApiExceptionHandler` mapping**, the first 422 in the API. Error bodies stay **empty** (matching the existing handler's `.build()` convention — a structured error payload is an all-endpoints-or-none concern, out of §3.6 scope). Only `GameStatus.FINAL` is produced (SCHEDULED/IN_PROGRESS stay for Phase 5).

**Rationale**: *(A)* A `GameResult` grouping the game header with both teams' box scores is the natural "here's your simulated game" object and exactly what the Phase 7 game view renders; sharing one model across simulate and get means a client sees the same shape whether it just simulated or is re-fetching. Excluding the event list keeps the payload bounded (the event log is the separate paginated-in-principle endpoint). Splitting home/away server-side is friendlier for the frontend than a flat list the client must regroup, and the per-row team lookup is cheap. No per-period columns because the event log is the source of truth (#020) and any line score is derivable — storing it would duplicate that fact (#013/#015 discipline). *(B)* We already persist every event (#020) precisely so the game record is authoritative; a seed column makes the record not just readable but **reproducible** — a strictly stronger property for one cheap `BIGINT`. Since `release.1.0.4.game.sql` is unreleased, it's a clean append with no new-release ceremony. Optional-with-random-default preserves #021 A's variation rule while removing its one weakness (a produced game couldn't be regenerated). *(C)/(D)/(E)* All three are the #014/#017/#020 "don't build surface/schema ahead of a consumer" discipline: the pace knob, pagination machinery, and a time column each have **no consumer that needs the distinction yet**, and each is **additive later** (a request field, a `Pageable` finder + envelope, a migration) with zero cost to defer. Counting the actual event volume (~200–350) retired the #019 "large result set" worry for this endpoint specifically — it was written before the volume was known. Deferring the time column a third time is not a punt because it is now *resolved* (derived-on-read, with a named trigger) rather than left open. *(F)* Matching `fetchTeam`'s 404 pattern reuses machinery that already works (the engine already throws on bad teams). The same-team case is **semantically** a 422, not a 400: the request is well-formed (valid JSON, both fields present, both ids resolve) but violates a cross-field business rule the server understood — the textbook RFC 9110 §15.5.21 "unprocessable entity," distinct from 400 "malformed" (no syntax error), 409 "state conflict" (no stored resource it collides with), and 404 "missing" (both teams exist). Adopting 422 is a deliberate, small forward step in the API's error vocabulary (the user prefers richer status codes to guide client branching); Spring has first-class `HttpStatus.UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY`, so the cost is one exception type + one handler line.  
**Trade-off**: *(A)* `GameResult` hydrates both full box scores on every get even for a caller who only wanted the score; trivial at ~30 rows, and a lean header-only projection is addable later if it ever matters. The team-split lookup is extra mapper work the flat-list alternative wouldn't need. *(B)* Persisting the seed is a schema change (H2/Postgres dual-compat, risks.md) and existing dev DBs need `docker compose down -v` to pick up the column (#009); the `game` table also grows a column that only a reproduce/replay consumer reads. *(C)* A client cannot request a shorter/longer game; accepted — no consumer, and the coach model already varies tempo. *(D)* An overtime marathon or a future season-log view could eventually want paging/filtering; accepted — the orphaned params + `findByGameId` seam make it a clean additive change, and a `period` filter is the likelier first ask. *(E)* Play-by-play carries no timestamp in §3.6, so a UI wanting "8:42" must compute it on read; accepted — it's display-only, cheap to derive, and the storage shape is better decided by the UI that consumes it. *(F)* Introducing 422 adds a new exception type + handler mapping for a single validation case, making `/simulate` the first endpoint with a richer code than the existing 400/404/409 vocabulary; accepted as an intentional, isolated first step (rather than the consistency-driven 400 the roster-cap violations use).  
**Alternatives considered**: *(A)* **Score-only response** (client `GET`s the box score after) — rejected: nearly every caller wants the box score immediately, forcing a second round-trip. **Full box score inline as a flat list** — rejected in favor of the grouped `GameResult` (frontend wants home/away split; regrouping client-side is busywork). **Per-period line-score columns on `Game`** — rejected: derivable from events (#020), storing duplicates the fact. *(B)* **Never accept a seed** (always internal random) — rejected: no way to reproduce a specific game for debugging/replay. **Accept a seed but don't persist it** (the #021 A status quo) — rejected: a produced game still couldn't be regenerated; persisting is one cheap column for a strictly stronger record. *(C)* **Expose `possessionsPerPeriod`** — rejected: re-does calibration the harness already owns, or overrides the coach-driven pace model with a raw number, undermining fidelity; no legitimate client use. *(D)* **Offset/limit pagination via the orphaned params + a `PlayByPlayPage` envelope + a `Pageable` finder** — rejected for §3.6: event volume is modest and a play-by-play UI wants the whole game; machinery with no payload to justify it (mirrors #019's own reasoning at the current scale). **By-`sequence`-range or by-`period` paging** — rejected as the pagination *mechanism* (a `period` *filter* is a fine additive future add). *(E)* **Store a single scalar `secondsElapsed`/`gameClock` now** — rejected: with no real game clock (#021) any value is a synthetic projection dressed as precision, and no consumer needs it yet; bake it when the UI defines the shape. **Store an explicit `startSeconds`/`endSeconds` range** — rejected: doubles columns for an already-synthetic value no consumer reads. **Defer as an open question again** — rejected in favor of *resolving* it as derived-on-read with a named trigger, so it isn't a fourth open punt. *(F)* **400 for the same-team case** (consistent with how roster-cap violations map, #016/#017) — rejected: technically slightly abuses 400 (the request isn't malformed); the user prefers the semantically-correct richer code. **409 Conflict** — rejected: no stored resource the request collides with. **A dedicated same-team validation with no distinct code** (fold into an existing exception) — rejected: loses the client-guiding distinction that motivated adopting 422.  
**Status of §3.6 decisions**: A–F are all resolved by this entry (mirrors #021/#022/#023). Net schema change: `game.seed` only (Decision B). New API-layer type: `ResourceUnprocessableException` → 422 (Decision F). Carry-forward follow-ups from #021 (event time) and #022 (`assist_player_id` in the API) are **closed here** (E resolves the first as derived-on-read; D surfaces the second). The execute-ready task sequence is todo.md's "SESSION 3 EXECUTION PLAN."  
**Implementation note (from execution)**: `GameSimulator` originally constructor-injected `GametimeService` (for team lookups), so making `GametimeServiceImp` inject `GameSimulator` for `simulateGame` formed a Spring constructor cycle. Rather than paper over it with `@Lazy`, the shared "load a team with its roster" logic was **extracted into a focused `TeamQueryService`** (`getTeam` + `toTeamWithRoster`, depending only on `TeamRepo`/`PlayerTeamRepo`/`PlayerRepo`/`EntityMapper`). Both `GametimeServiceImp` (which now delegates `getTeam`/roster-assembly to it) and `GameSimulator` (which no longer touches the top-level service) depend on `TeamQueryService`, so the dependency graph is acyclic and one-way (`GametimeServiceImp → GameSimulator → TeamQueryService`) with no `@Lazy`. This also fixed an altitude smell — a low-level engine was reaching into the god-service just for one read. The entity→model mapping (Decision A's crux) was added to the existing `EntityMapper` (`entityToGame` / `entityToGameEvent` / `entityToBoxScore` / `toGameResult`) rather than a separate `GameMapper`. Everything else shipped exactly as A–F specify; touched packages (`api` / `mapper` / `service` / `exception`) land at 99–100% line coverage.

**§3.6 follow-up (carry forward to Phase 4/7)**: play-by-play pagination + a `period` filter (Decision D seam, when a large-result-set or filtering consumer appears); a per-event time column's storage shape (Decision E — decided by the Phase 7 game view if it shows a clock); a lean header-only `GameResult` projection (Decision A, if ever needed).

---

### 025 — Blocked shots (§3.7): a block-first three-way shot outcome, a defender-vs-finisher block contest, a flat four-way loose-ball recovery, and block recorded as a field-goal outcome mirroring how a steal is a turnover outcome
**Date**: 2026-07  
**Scope**: §3.7 is the first of the possession-fidelity completion sub-phases (§3.7–§3.11, scheduled before Phase 4). It makes **blocked shots a real, first-class event** — today a block is indistinguishable from a normal miss and `BoxScore.blocks` is hardcoded `0` (`GameSimulator.setBlocks(0)`). This is **engine work** (a new outcome + resolver + stat), not API/stats work. The unifying idea: a **block is a field-goal outcome the way a steal is a turnover outcome** — a defensive event modeled as an outcome-flavor on the offensive action it interrupts, plus a separate defender-credit accumulator. **Fork shape chosen: v3** (one three-way MAKE/MISS/BLOCK draw), rejecting v1 ("relabel a miss" — blocks not a real event) and v2 (a separate block gate before the make roll). **No schema change** (`box_score.blocks` already exists; the block event reuses `PlayType.SHOT` with a new `outcome`). NOT YET BUILT — this entry is the resolved design; the execute-ready plan is todo.md's §3.7 plan.

**Decision A — three-way split construction: block-first (A1).** The MAKE/MISS/BLOCK outcome is built by carving a small `P(BLOCK)` off the top, then running the **existing, already-§3.4-calibrated** offense-vs-defense make contest on the remainder: `P(BLOCK) = small, defender-driven, shot-type-scaled`; `remainder = 1 − P(BLOCK)`; `P(MAKE) = remainder × existing make contest`; `P(MISS) = remainder − P(MAKE)`. This preserves §3.4's calibration — blocks skim a thin slice, so recalibration is "nudge base rates up to refill the points blocks removed," not "recompute FG% from scratch."

**Decision B — block probability is a defender-vs-finisher contest (B2).** `P(BLOCK)` is not a defender-only rate but a contest in the same avg-10 logistic shape as every other resolver (#021): `p = base + SENSITIVITY × (defenderBlockSkill − shooter finishing)/10`. **Defender block skill** = `rimProtection` (DRIVE/POST) / `shotContest` (PERIMETER); **shooter counter-factor** = `finishing` (a real `PlayerGameState` getter — `getShotSkill()` does not exist as a resolver-exposed skill). A great finisher gets blocked less than a scrub against the same rim protector. Offense thus touches both the block slice and the make contest — an accepted entanglement (it is *why* a rim protector is felt twice, realistically).

**Decision C — block rate by shot type + target.** Block base-rate ordering `DRIVE ≥ POST > PERIMETER ≫ THREE`; `THREE` is **very-low, not flat-zero** (real threes get closeout-blocked occasionally; flat-zero would make them unblockable — an artifact for no benefit). Aggregate target **~5 blocks/team/game**, reported by the `CalibrationHarness` next to actual (like FG%/assists). The actual constants are tuned empirically during execution, same as §3.4's `BASE_DRIVE`.

**Decision D — a flat four-way loose-ball recovery (`BlockResolver`).** After a block, a single **block-specific roll** picks one of four outcomes: (1) `RECOVERED_DEFENSE` (in-bounds, possession over), (2) `RECOVERED_OFFENSE` (in-bounds → second chance), (3) `OUT_OF_BOUNDS_DEFENSE` (OOB off the shooter/offense → defense ball), (4) `OUT_OF_BOUNDS_OFFENSE` (OOB off the blocker/defense → offense retains → second chance). Keeping OOB as two distinct outcomes preserves it as a visible play-by-play event *and* forks possession correctly. The split is **defense-leaning** (`RECOVERED_DEFENSE > RECOVERED_OFFENSE >` the two OOB slices) and **FLAT** — fixed weights identical for every block, no skill bends recovery. Where a swatted ball caroms is dominated by physics/chance, so flat is the *more accurate* model, and it keeps `BlockResolver` architecturally distinct from `ReboundResolver` (a positioning/box-out contest). **Recovery weights are placeholders — no citable NBA data for the block-recovery distribution was found**; put reasonable defense-leaning defaults in `SimConfig` and let the harness settle them against the ~5/team target.

**Decision E — offense-recovered block re-enters the second-chance loop (skip `ReboundResolver`).** Recovery outcomes 2 & 4 jump straight into the existing second-chance loop at `ShotSelector` (a fresh shooter + shot type, then Turnover → Foul → Shot), bumping the offensive-rebound counter against `MAX_OFFENSIVE_REBOUNDS_PER_POSSESSION`. It **skips** `ReboundResolver`'s draw — recovery was already decided by `BlockResolver`, so re-running it would double-decide who has the ball. (Re-entry is at the top of the possession loop, `ShotSelector`, not "top of `ShotResolver`" — the offense picks a fresh shot.)

**Decision F — attribution & event representation: a block mirrors a steal.** A steal is recorded (§3.2) as a `TURNOVER`/`STOLEN` event naming the ball-loser as `primaryPlayerId`, plus a separate `stealer.recordSteal()` credit — the stealer is not on the event. A block mirrors this exactly: **(F1)** the event is `PlayType.SHOT`, `outcome = "BLOCKED"` (carrying shot type, e.g. `BLOCKED_DRIVE`, like `MADE_*`/`MISSED_*`), `primaryPlayerId = the shooter` — **no new `PlayType.BLOCK`** (a block is a *kind of missed shot* as a steal is a *kind of turnover*; PBP filter is `SHOT WHERE outcome LIKE 'BLOCKED%'`). **(F2)** the blocker is credited `+1` via a new `blocker.recordBlock()` accumulator on `PlayerGameState` (mirrors `recordSteal()`), mapped to `BoxScore.blocks`, replacing the `setBlocks(0)` hardcode; the blocker is not on the event. **(F3)** the shooter is charged a **missed FGA** (`+1 FGA`, `+1 3PA` if a blocked THREE, `0 FGM`) — a block counts against FG%; because the event is a `SHOT`, FGA/FG% reconciliation is automatic. **(F4)** a blocked shot is never made, so it carries **no `assistPlayerId`**. Reconciliation invariant for the harness: count of `SHOT` events with `outcome LIKE 'BLOCKED%'` **==** sum of `BoxScore.blocks` (same shape steals/assists/rebounds already use, #020/#022).
