**Rationale**: *(overall)* Making a block a `SHOT` outcome + a credit accumulator reuses the engine's existing defensive-event pattern (steal = `TURNOVER` outcome + `recordSteal`), so reconciliation, FGA accounting, and play-by-play all fall out for free instead of needing special cases — and it preserves the "every shot attempt is a `SHOT` event" invariant. *(A)* Block-first keeps §3.4's hard-won calibration nearly intact (a thin slice off the top) instead of throwing the make contest into a new formula (A2) that would recalibrate FG% from scratch. *(B)* A defender-vs-finisher contest is the same logistic shape used everywhere else, so it needs no new math, and "elite finishers get blocked less" is real basketball; the offense-in-both-rolls entanglement is correct, not a bug — a rim protector genuinely both blocks shots and makes the ones he doesn't block harder. *(C)* Per-shot-type ordering matches reality (rim attempts are far more blockable than threes); very-low-not-zero avoids an "unblockable three" artifact at no cost. *(D)* A blocked ball's destination is a chaotic loose ball, not a box-out contest — flat weights are the *more* accurate model and keep `BlockResolver` from quietly becoming a reskinned `ReboundResolver`; the four-way split keeps OOB visible while forking possession correctly. *(E)* Skipping `ReboundResolver` avoids double-deciding possession; reusing the offensive-rebound second-chance loop reuses machinery that already works and already respects the second-chance cap. *(F)* Mirroring steals is the consistency win — the codebase already answered "how do we record a defensive event" and blocks should not invent a second pattern.

**Trade-off**: *(overall)* Blocks convert some would-be *makes* into blocks, so scoring drops slightly → **one recalibration pass** (nudge shot base rates up to refill; the harness reports, does not gate). *(B)* Two knobs now live in the thin block slice (defender strength + finisher counter-weight), both balanced against the ~5/team target. *(D)* The recovery weights are unsourced placeholders — accepted, calibrated empirically like every other rate. *(F1)* Overloading `SHOT`/`outcome` rather than a dedicated `PlayType.BLOCK` means a "blocks only" query is a `LIKE 'BLOCKED%'` filter rather than a play-type equality — accepted (same as filtering made threes today), and it avoids fragmenting the play-type vocabulary.

**Alternatives considered**: *(fork)* **v1 — relabel a miss as a block** (roll a block only on shots that already missed): rejected — a real block *prevents a would-be make*, so a miss-relabel is physically backwards and removes no points. **v2 — a separate block gate before the make roll** (two sequential rolls): rejected in favor of v3 because two independent skill-fed rolls create a double-count trap (the defender's skill would tax the shooter twice), which the single three-way draw (probabilities summing to 1) dissolves by construction. *(D)* **Skill-driven recovery** (rebounding skill bends the offense/defense split): rejected — re-introduces the `offenseRebound`-vs-`defenseRebound` contest `BlockResolver` exists to avoid, and the effect is a thin second-order tweak no consumer needs. **Dissolve OOB into a two-way offense/defense recovery** (OOB-3): rejected in favor of the four-way roll so `OUT_OF_BOUNDS` stays a visible play-by-play event. *(F)* **A dedicated `PlayType.BLOCK` event**: rejected — fragments the "every shot attempt is a `SHOT` event" invariant, complicates FGA reconciliation, and duplicates the steal pattern instead of reusing it. *(shot clock)* **Model elevated shot-clock pressure on block-recovered second chances now**: rejected for §3.7 — the engine has no shot clock (#021) and this belongs to §3.9's shot-clock-violation turnover sub-category (routed there, not built here).

**Status of §3.7 decisions**: A–F all resolved by this entry. Net schema change: **none** (`box_score.blocks` exists; block reuses `PlayType.SHOT`). New engine pieces: a `BLOCKED` branch in the shot resolution (`ShotResolver`/`PossessionEngine`), a `BlockResolver` (four-way flat recovery), `PlayerGameState.recordBlock()`, new `SimConfig` block base rates + recovery weights, and a `CalibrationHarness` blocks line. The execute-ready task sequence is todo.md's "§3.7 execution plan."  

**Implementation note (from execution, 2026-07).** Shipped exactly as A–F specify, with **one calibration-driven addition to Decision B**: blocks needed their **own contest sensitivity** (`SimConfig.BLOCK_SENSITIVITY = 0.12`), not the shared global `SENSITIVITY = 0.5`. B says a block is "the same avg-10 logistic shape as every other resolver" — the *shape* is identical, but reusing the global sensitivity made blocks provably unreachable-to-target: at 0.5, a good rim protector (14) vs an average finisher (10) blocks ~23% of shots (elite NBA is ~5–6%), so skilled defenders alone drove blocks to 11–13/team regardless of the thin base rate. Cutting the base could not fix it (the base was already near zero); the contest term was the driver. A gentler block-specific sensitivity keeps the defender-vs-finisher contest real (B2 — elite protectors block more, elite finishers get blocked less) while letting `BASE_BLOCK_*` stay the dominant term (A1's "thin slice off the top"), and it does **not** touch any other resolver. This was a user-approved call (2026-07). Final constants: `BASE_BLOCK_DRIVE/POST/PERIMETER/THREE = 0.056/0.048/0.023/0.005`, `BLOCK_SENSITIVITY = 0.12`, recovery weights **unchanged from the seeded defense-leaning placeholders** (0.45/0.30/0.13/0.12 — the harness settled fine without touching them). The recalibration nudged the shot base rates up (`BASE_DRIVE/PERIMETER/POST/THREE` 0.59/0.43/0.49/0.31 → 0.605/0.445/0.505/0.345) to refill the points blocks removed; landing (harness, 102 games): **blocks 4.8/team, 112.9 pts / 47.2% FG / 36.0% 3P / 27.1 ast / 13.5 TO**, §3.5 minutes distribution intact. The block-reconciliation invariant is checked by the harness (folded into its existing reconciliation counter) and by `GameSimulatorIntegrationTest`. New/changed `sim` classes land at 99.4–100% line coverage.  

**§3.7 follow-up (carry forward)**: a skilled-blocker recovery edge (Decision D — additive if a consumer ever wants it); shot-clock pressure on second-chance possessions (Decision E cross-ref → §3.9).

---

### 026 — Missed shot out of bounds (§3.8): a single skill-weighted four-way missed-shot outcome in a new MissedShotResolver that wraps ReboundResolver, with a defense-leaning OOB lean, and sail-out verified (not assumed) harness-neutral
**Date**: 2026-07  
**Scope**: §3.8 is the second possession-fidelity completion sub-phase (§3.7–§3.11, before Phase 4). It makes a **missed shot that leaves the court out of bounds a real, distinct outcome** — today *every* missed shot resolves to a rebound (`REBOUND` / `OFFENSIVE` or `DEFENSIVE`), so a ball that sails OOB untouched or is tipped OOB in a scramble is silently folded into that rebound roll and mis-credited to a rebounder. This is **engine work** (a new outcome branch + a new resolver that wraps the existing one), not API/stats work. **No schema change** (`outcome` is open-ended free text, #020; OOB reuses an existing `PlayType`). NOT YET BUILT — this entry is the resolved design; the execute-ready plan is todo.md's §3.8 plan.

**Two real events in scope (both, per the design pass).** A missed shot can leave the court two ways, and §3.8 models **both**:
- **Sail-out** — the shot itself flies OOB untouched (airball / hard brick off the side of the rim/backboard). **No contest, no rebounder.** Ball inbounded to the other team. This is the case that genuinely **changes possession flow** (see Decision D).
- **Tipped-OOB** — a rebound *contest* happens, a hand gets to the ball, and it deflects out. **A board attempt occurred**, but nobody secured it; last-touch awards the inbound. This is pure relabeling of balls that resolve as rebounds today.

**Decision A — one skill-weighted four-way outcome, not a separate OOB sub-decision (the crux).** A missed shot resolves to **exactly one of four outcomes** in a **single weighted draw**:
1. `OFFENSIVE` rebound → offense retains → second chance
2. `DEFENSIVE` rebound → defense's ball → possession over
3. OOB, offense retains → second chance
4. OOB, defense's ball → possession over

**Skill is already in this one draw** — the existing offense-vs-defense rebounding contest (`offenseRebound` vs `defenseRebound`, `ReboundResolver`) pulls the outcome toward the better rebounding team. **Whether the ball goes OOB vs. is cleanly secured is NOT a second, separately-skilled question** layered on top: once the ball is heading out, "who touched it last" is not another rebounding contest — it is a fixed **lean**, and the OOB slices **lean defensive** (real missed shots knocked out in a scramble go to the defense more often than the offense). So there is no "is it OOB?" roll and no OOB skill contest — there is one four-way result, skill-weighted for the rebound-vs-rebound balance, with the two OOB slices carved as smaller, defense-leaning weights.

**Decision B — a new `MissedShotResolver` that WRAPS `ReboundResolver` (does not replace it).** The four-way outcome is owned by a **new `sim` `@Component`, `MissedShotResolver`**, which **delegates the pure two-way board contest + rebounder selection to the existing `ReboundResolver`** (unchanged) and applies the OOB lean on top to split off outcomes 3 & 4. `ReboundResolver` stays exactly as it is — a two-way skill contest that **always credits a rebounder** on its two outcomes — and `MissedShotResolver` owns the full "a shot missed, now what?" decision, returning one of the four. `PossessionEngine`'s `// 4. Rebound` block shrinks to a single call into `MissedShotResolver`, then forks on the returned outcome using the **same retain/return shape** the §3.7 block recovery already uses (`offensiveRebounds++` + `continue` on a retained outcome, respecting `MAX_OFFENSIVE_REBOUNDS_PER_POSSESSION`; `return sequence + 1` on a possession-ending outcome).

**Decision C — the §3.7 `BlockResolver` parallel, and the one deliberate difference: skill-weighted vs. flat.** §3.8's four-way missed-shot outcome **converges in shape** with §3.7's `BlockResolver` — both are {offense/defense × retain/OOB}, defense-leaning. The **deliberate difference**: the missed-shot version is **skill-weighted** (a real board contest happened — the better rebounder should win more), while the block-recovery version is **flat** (#025 D — a swatted ball is pure chaos dominated by physics, no skill). This distinction is the whole reason they are two resolvers, not one: `MissedShotResolver` wraps a skill contest; `BlockResolver` deliberately avoids one. Recording it here so a future reader doesn't "unify" them and erase the difference.

**Decision D — sail-out is verified harness-neutral, NOT assumed (corrects the roadmap "free" label).** The roadmap filed §3.8 as *free — no recalibration* on the assumption it is pure event-log labeling. That holds for **tipped-OOB** (it relabels balls that already resolve as offensive/defensive rebounds, preserving the retain/end split, so the aggregates cannot move). It does **NOT** provably hold for **sail-out**: today *every* miss produces a rebound, so a sail-out that awards the defense the ball with **no offensive-rebound chance** slightly **reduces second-chance possessions** → a small downward nudge on offensive rebounds and possibly points. Probably within tolerance, but not zero by construction. Therefore: **add an "OOB / team / game" line to the `CalibrationHarness`** (so the rate is *visible* even without a hard target), run it after building, and **confirm the §3.4/§3.5 aggregates (112/47%/36%/26/14) and rebound totals still hold**. If sail-out drags them, treat it exactly like §3.7 — a small recalibration pass (nudge shot/rebound base rates), re-agreed with the user. §3.8 is thus **"free pending harness confirmation,"** not assumed-free.

**Decision E — attribution & reconciliation: OOB credits no rebounder; the rebound invariant must exclude OOB.** An OOB outcome (sail-out or tipped) **credits no rebound** — nobody secured the ball, so no `recordOffensiveRebound()`/`recordDefensiveRebound()`. The existing reconciliation invariant `count(REBOUND OFFENSIVE/DEFENSIVE events) == Σ BoxScore.(off+def rebounds)` (#020/#022, `GameSimulatorIntegrationTest`) **must be preserved** — which means an OOB event must **not** be counted as a rebound on either side of that equation. The exact **event representation is resolved at execution write-up** (todo.md §3.8 plan) against this invariant — the leading candidate is a distinct `outcome` on the existing `REBOUND` `PlayType` (e.g. `OUT_OF_BOUNDS_OFFENSE` / `OUT_OF_BOUNDS_DEFENSE`) crediting no rebounder, mirroring the #025 F discipline (reuse a `PlayType`, open-ended `outcome`, no schema change) and the `BlockResolver`'s own `OOB_*` naming — but the final choice (and whether sail-out vs. tipped-OOB are distinguishable in the event, or share one OOB outcome) is a small execution decision made with the invariant in front of us, not guessed now.

**Rationale**: *(A)* Modeling the missed shot as one weighted four-way draw — rather than a rebound roll with a bolted-on "did it go OOB?" second decision — is the honest structure: skill already decides the offense-vs-defense balance in the existing contest, and "the ball went out" is an *outcome*, not a modifier that re-invokes skill. A separate OOB sub-decision would either double-count rebounding skill or fabricate a second skilled contest for a ball nobody controlled. The defensive lean on the OOB slices is real basketball (loose balls in a scrum favor the defense) and is a fixed weight, not a contest. *(B)* Wrapping `ReboundResolver` (vs. absorbing it) keeps a working, well-tested two-way contest intact and pure — it still credits a rebounder on its two outcomes — and confines §3.8's new logic to the OOB lean + the four-way fork, the smallest blast radius. It also keeps single responsibility clean: `ReboundResolver` answers "who won the board," `MissedShotResolver` answers "a shot missed, now what." *(C)* The skill-weighted-vs-flat distinction from `BlockResolver` is not an inconsistency to iron out — it is the correct modeling difference between a real board contest (skill matters) and a chaotic swat recovery (chaos dominates); documenting it prevents a future "simplification" that would wrongly merge them. *(D)* Blocks (#025) taught that a "small, surely-negligible" change to the shot flow can move the aggregates more than expected; sail-out removes a rebound opportunity that exists today, so it earns the same verify-don't-assume treatment. Making the OOB rate *visible* in the harness (even without a target) is the #022 D "build the instrument" discipline — you cannot confirm neutrality for a rate you cannot see. *(E)* Events are the source of truth (#020); the rebound reconciliation is a shipped invariant, so the one hard constraint on the event representation is that OOB must not masquerade as a rebound. Deferring the exact `outcome` strings to the execution write-up (as #024 deferred a shape to its consumer) avoids guessing a vocabulary ahead of the invariant that constrains it.  
**Trade-off**: *(A)* Folding OOB into the rebound draw means the four weights (two rebound, two OOB) are tuned together, not independently; accepted — they are one decision. *(B)* `MissedShotResolver` adds a wrapping layer between `PossessionEngine` and `ReboundResolver`; accepted — it is where the miss→outcome orchestration belongs (the same place the §3.7 block fork lives). *(C)* Two structurally-similar resolvers (`BlockResolver`, `MissedShotResolver`) with a subtle skill-vs-flat difference could invite a future merge; mitigated by documenting the difference here and in code. *(D)* The harness run is one extra execution step and the OOB line one more thing to maintain; cheap. *(E)* The final event vocabulary is left open to execution; accepted — it is a small, invariant-constrained choice, not a design fork.  
**Alternatives considered**: *(A)* **A separate "is it OOB?" pre-roll or in-rebound sub-decision, skilled** (a better rebounder is more likely to tip a loose ball to his own team) — rejected: re-invokes rebounding skill a second time on the same ball, double-counting it, and fabricates a skilled contest for a ball nobody secured; the user's model (skill is already in the one draw; OOB is a fixed defensive lean) is both simpler and more honest. **A separate OOB pre-roll, flat** — rejected: still splits one decision across two places for no gain; the four-way draw expresses it in one. *(B)* **Extend `ReboundResolver` to a four-way outcome** (off/def/OOB-off/OOB-def) — rejected: breaks its single responsibility (it exists to credit a rebounder via a two-way contest) and risks the rebound reconciliation invariant by threading non-crediting OOB outcomes through a crediting resolver — the same "don't let it become a reskinned contest" smell #025 D avoided for blocks. **`MissedShotResolver` absorbs / replaces `ReboundResolver`** — rejected (user call): discards a working, tested resolver and enlarges the change for no benefit; wrapping keeps the two-way contest pure and intact. *(C)* **Unify `BlockResolver` and `MissedShotResolver` into one loose-ball resolver** — rejected: they differ in the one way that matters (skill-weighted board contest vs. flat chaos recovery); merging would force one to adopt the other's wrong model. *(D)* **Trust §3.8 is "free" and skip the harness** (per the original roadmap label) — rejected: sail-out is a genuine new outcome that removes a second-chance opportunity, exactly the kind of "surely negligible" shot-flow change §3.7 proved can move the numbers; cheap to verify, so verify. *(E)* **A new `PlayType.OUT_OF_BOUNDS`** — rejected (leaning): fragments the play-type vocabulary the way #025 F rejected a `PlayType.BLOCK`; an OOB is a *kind of missed-shot resolution*, so an `outcome` on an existing `PlayType` fits the established pattern (final call at execution). **Store which player last touched an OOB ball** — rejected: no consumer needs it, and the last-touch team is already implied by the offense/defense OOB outcome; fabricating a participant column ahead of a consumer is the #014/#017/#020 trap.  
**Status of §3.8 decisions**: A–E all resolved by this entry (mirrors #021/#022/#023/#024/#025). Net schema change: **none**. New engine pieces: a new `MissedShotResolver` (`sim` `@Component`, wraps `ReboundResolver`) owning a skill-weighted four-way missed-shot outcome with a defense-leaning OOB lean; new `SimConfig` OOB lean weights; the `// 4. Rebound` branch in `PossessionEngine` reduced to a `MissedShotResolver` call + the established retain/return fork; a `CalibrationHarness` OOB line; and (at execution) the OOB `outcome` vocabulary. Open-at-execution (small, invariant-constrained): the exact OOB `outcome` strings and whether sail-out vs. tipped-OOB are distinguishable in the event. The execute-ready task sequence is todo.md's "§3.8 execution plan."  
**Implementation note (from execution, 2026-07).** Shipped exactly as A–E specify — no design divergence. Resolved the two open-at-execution items (§3.8 plan step 1): **(1) event vocabulary** — OOB reuses `PlayType.REBOUND` with outcomes `OUT_OF_BOUNDS_OFFENSE` / `OUT_OF_BOUNDS_DEFENSE` and a **null `primaryPlayerId`** (the leading candidate; no new `PlayType`, no schema change). This is invariant-safe by construction: `GameSimulatorIntegrationTest`'s rebound reconciliation exact-matches `"OFFENSIVE"`/`"DEFENSIVE"` outcomes, so the `OUT_OF_BOUNDS_*` events are excluded from both sides automatically and credit no box-score rebound (E). **(2) sail-out vs. tipped-OOB are NOT distinguished** in the event — they share one OOB outcome each (offense/defense); nothing consumes the distinction and the last-touch team is implied by the suffix (the #014/#017/#020 no-fabricated-detail discipline). Mechanically there is no separate sail-out roll either: the single flat OOB carve (`SimConfig.OOB_TOTAL_WEIGHT = 0.07`, split `OOB_DEFENSE_WEIGHT`/`OOB_OFFENSE_WEIGHT` = 0.60/0.40) folds both cases into "OOB-offense / OOB-defense," exactly A's intent. **Carve mechanism** implemented as specified (the sharp edge): OOB-vs-clean split FIRST by the flat weights (skill-independent), `ReboundResolver`'s skill contest run ONLY on the clean-rebound remainder — guarded by a `MissedShotResolverTest` (`oobSplitIsSkillIndependent`) that fails the plausible-but-wrong "OOB inherits the board winner" implementation. **Cap tidy** taken as recommended: `capReached` is passed into `MissedShotResolver.resolve(...)`, which never returns a retained outcome when capped (forcing the possession-ending sibling, family preserved). `PossessionEngine` no longer references `ReboundResolver` directly (it wraps it via `MissedShotResolver`), so that constructor field was removed. **Landed free (Decision D verified):** harness (102 games) reads **OOB ~3.0/team/game**, aggregates held — **113.0 pts / 47.0% FG / 37.8% 3P / 27.6 ast / 13.6 TO / 5.1 blk**, §3.5 minutes + period-FG% intact — no recalibration. `OOB_TOTAL_WEIGHT`/split unchanged from the seeded placeholders. New `sim` classes (`MissedShotResolver`, `MissedShotOutcome`) at 100%, `PossessionEngine` 99.4% line coverage; full `mvn clean install` gate green (383 unit + 52 Cucumber, all coverage checks met).

**§3.8 follow-up (carry forward)**: none new. (The §3.6 time-column and §3.7 skilled-recovery/shot-clock follow-ups still stand.)

---

### 027 — Turnover sub-categories (§3.9): a post-declaration weighted-cause draw on the unchanged turnover gate, a 9-outcome taxonomy with STOLEN kept dominant, modest avg-10 leans on four causes, and a distinct LOST_BALL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS that does not collide with §3.8
**Date**: 2026-07  
**Scope**: §3.9 is the third possession-fidelity completion sub-phase (§3.7–§3.11, before Phase 4). It replaces §3.2's **two** turnover outcomes — `STOLEN` (credits a stealer) and the unforced catch-all `LOST_BALL` — with a **realistic 9-cause taxonomy**, each a new `outcome` value on the existing `TURNOVER` `GameEvent`. This is **engine work** (a richer outcome branch), not API/stats/schema work. The `outcome` field is open-ended free text (#020), so **no schema change** and **no OpenAPI change** (turnover outcome is not a surfaced enum). The unifying idea, confirmed with the user: **the turnover *gate* is untouched — a turnover happens exactly as often as today (same `BASE_TURNOVER`, same contest, same coach/fatigue scaling); only once a turnover is *declared* does a new weighted draw decide *which kind*.** That is what keeps §3.9 **free — the turnover count never moves**, so the harness aggregates (112/47/36/26/**14**) cannot be dragged by construction. NOT YET BUILT — this entry is the resolved design; the execute-ready plan is todo.md's §3.9 plan.

**Decision A — structure: the gate is unchanged; a weighted cause-draw runs only *after* a turnover is declared (the crux).** `PossessionEngine`'s `// 1. Turnover check` keeps `turnoverResolver.isTurnover(shooter, defense, defensivePressure, rng)` **exactly as-is** — same `SimConfig.BASE_TURNOVER = 0.038`, same avg-10 contest, same §3.4 `defensivePressure` and §3.5 fatigue scaling. What changes is *inside the `if (isTurnover)` block*: the binary `isStolen(rng)` (a bare `rng.nextDouble() < 0.6`) is replaced by a **single weighted categorical draw** over the 9 causes (a new `TurnoverResolver.pickCause(...)` returning a `TurnoverCause` enum). Because the draw only ever runs *given a turnover already occurred*, it **re-partitions** the existing turnovers — it can shift the *mix* but never the *count*. This is the deliberate difference from §3.7/§3.8: those carved a new event off the shot/miss flow (moving points/rebounds); §3.9 adds **zero** new events and touches **zero** other flow — it only labels an event that already fires. The "free — no recalibration" premise is therefore true **by construction**, not by luck (contrast #026 D, where sail-out genuinely removed a second-chance opportunity and had to be *verified* free; here nothing is removed or added, so freeness is structural).

**Decision B — the 9-outcome taxonomy + probability tiers (the user's set).** One weighted draw over these nine `TurnoverCause` values (base weights in `SimConfig`, tier = relative magnitude):

| `outcome` string | Tier (base weight) | Scaled by | Credited to |
|---|---|---|---|
| `STOLEN` | **high — dominant** | defender `stealing` (via existing `pickStealer`) | ball-loser on event **+ stealer** `recordSteal()` |
| `SHOT_CLOCK_VIOLATION` | high | ball-handler `acumen` (↓) + defending coach `defensiveScheme` stall (↑) | ball-loser |
| `OFFENSIVE_FOUL` | high | offense `teamOffense` (↓ — weaker offense fouls more) | ball-loser |
| `BAD_PASS` | mid | offense `teamOffense` (↓) | ball-loser |
| `TRAVELLING` | mid | flat | ball-loser |
| `LOST_BALL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS` | low | flat | ball-loser |
| `3_SECONDS_VIOLATION` | low | flat | ball-loser |
| `8_SECONDS_BACKCOURT_VIOLATION` | super-low | flat | ball-loser |
| `OVER_AND_BACK` | super-low | flat | ball-loser |

**`STOLEN` is kept dominant (~55–60% of turnovers — user call).** Real basketball: live-ball steals are the plurality of turnovers, and it preserves today's steal rate so `BoxScore.steals` (and the §3.4-calibrated steal distribution) does not drift. **The other eight causes carve out of the old ~40% `LOST_BALL` bucket** — `LOST_BALL` is thus *retired as the catch-all* and replaced by the specific eight; the residual "lost the handle / stripped, ball out" case is expressed by `LOST_BALL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS` (and `BAD_PASS`/`TRAVELLING` for the others), so no generic unforced bucket survives. **Attribution (Decision A's "same player" guarantee, user-confirmed): every cause credits the current ball-handler** (`shooter.recordTurnover()`, `primaryPlayerId = shooter`) **exactly as today** — the per-player turnover count and reconciliation are byte-unchanged; only the label differs. `STOLEN` is the sole two-sided outcome (victim on the event, stealer credited separately, unchanged). The three team-level violations (`SHOT_CLOCK_VIOLATION`, `8_SECONDS_BACKCOURT_VIOLATION`, `OVER_AND_BACK`) are, in reality, team failures rather than the ball-handler's personal fault, but they are **charged to the on-ball player anyway** — reusing the existing ball-handler pick avoids fabricating a separate "who caused the team violation" picker (the #014/#017/#020 no-fabricated-participant discipline), and the box-score turnover already lands on *a* player today regardless of cause.

**Decision C — scaling: static tier base weights + a modest avg-10 lean on four causes, normalized per-turnover.** Each cause has a **fixed base weight** in `SimConfig` set by its tier (Decision B). Four causes additionally get a **modest avg-10 deviation lean** on their weight (the #022 form the whole engine uses), the rest are flat:
- `STOLEN` — already skill-shaped by `pickStealer`'s `stealing`-weighted draw; its *share* is a tier weight (kept dominant), not itself re-scaled here.
- `SHOT_CLOCK_VIOLATION` — weight ↑ as ball-handler `acumen` falls below 10 and as the defending coach's `defensiveScheme` rises (a stalling, pressuring scheme forces more clock violations).
- `OFFENSIVE_FOUL` / `BAD_PASS` — weight ↑ as offense `teamOffense` falls below 10 (a poorly-coordinated offense charges/throws it away more).

The weights are **normalized to sum to 1.0 on *each* declared turnover** (the leans shift the *relative* shares; the draw always resolves to exactly one cause). Because normalization happens per-turnover *after* the count is fixed by the untouched gate, **no lean can change the turnover total** — a strong-`teamOffense` team simply has a *different mix* (fewer bad passes, more steals-against), not fewer turnovers. Rejected the alternative "pure skill-driven weights with no tier constants": the tiers would become emergent and uncontrollable, rare causes could swing wildly game-to-game, and calibrating the *mix* would lose its anchor — the static-base-plus-modest-lean shape is exactly how `SimConfig`'s block-recovery and OOB weights already work, so it reuses a proven, tunable pattern.

**Decision D — cross-refs: §3.7-E parked as a further seam; a DISTINCT `TURNOVER_OUT_OF_BOUNDS` that cannot collide with §3.8.**
- **§3.7-E (shot-clock pressure on block-recovered / second-chance possessions) — LEFT AS A SEAM, not picked up (user call).** #025 E routed "elevated shot-clock-violation chance on a second-chance possession that starts with less clock" here. §3.9 **does not** implement it: doing so would make a cause weight depend on *which iteration of the second-chance loop* the possession is in — a possession-context input the flat/leaned draw does not take, and which would raise `SHOT_CLOCK_VIOLATION` selectively on retained possessions. That is a live risk to the "free" premise (it does not change the turnover *count*, but it would concentrate a cause in a way that interacts with the second-chance cap and is not harness-verified) and it is scope creep on a labeling pass. It stays a numbered further seam (see below), additive later if a consumer wants that fidelity.
- **`LOST_BALL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS` vs. §3.8's OOB — deliberately distinct, no shared string.** §3.8 (#026) already owns `OUT_OF_BOUNDS_OFFENSE` / `OUT_OF_BOUNDS_DEFENSE` as outcomes on the **`REBOUND`** `PlayType` (a *missed shot* leaving the court, crediting no player). A §3.9 out-of-bounds turnover is a **completely different event** — a *live-ball handling* turnover (stepped out with the ball, dribbled it off a foot/leg out) on the **`TURNOVER`** `PlayType`, crediting the ball-loser. They must never read as the same thing. Per the user's call, §3.9 ships this as its **own explicit outcome**, `LOST_BALL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS` (chosen over a bare `OUT_OF_BOUNDS`, which would visually collide with §3.8's `OUT_OF_BOUNDS_*`): different `PlayType`, different prefix, unambiguous in a play-by-play filter (`TURNOVER WHERE outcome = 'LOST_BALL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS'` vs. `REBOUND WHERE outcome LIKE 'OUT_OF_BOUNDS%'`).

**Decision E — reconciliation holds by construction; add a per-cause harness breakdown line for visibility.** `BoxScore.turnovers` reconciles against the **count of `TURNOVER` events** (#020, `GameSimulatorIntegrationTest`). §3.9 emits **exactly one `TURNOVER` event per declared turnover, same as today** — it only changes the `outcome` string — so the reconciliation is **byte-identical and cannot break**; likewise `BoxScore.steals` reconciles against `TURNOVER WHERE outcome = 'STOLEN'` (the count of which is preserved by keeping `STOLEN` dominant and unchanged in mechanics). No new counter, no new reconciliation. For **visibility** (the #022 D / #026 D "build the instrument" discipline, even without a per-cause target), **add a per-cause turnover breakdown line to `CalibrationHarness`** (the 9 causes as a share of turnovers, printed next to the existing TO/team aggregate) so the mix is observable and a wildly-off share (e.g. `OVER_AND_BACK` at 10%) is caught. This is the one honest nuance to the "free" premise per #026 D's "verify, don't assume": the turnover *total* is free by construction (Decision A), but the *mix* is a new distribution that should be *seen* once — hence the line, not a recalibration.

**Rationale**: *(A)* Confirming the gate is untouched is the entire reason §3.9 is free: §3.7/§3.8 moved scoring/rebounds because they added events to the shot/miss flow, whereas §3.9 relabels an event that already fires — a pure partition of a fixed count. Running the cause-draw *inside* the existing `if (isTurnover)` block (rather than folding cause into the gate) keeps that separation crisp in the code and makes the freeness structural, not empirical. *(B)* The 9-cause set is the user's, chosen for realistic play-by-play texture; keeping `STOLEN` dominant preserves the one calibrated number a turnover taxonomy could disturb (`BoxScore.steals`), and retiring `LOST_BALL` as the catch-all (splitting its ~40% into the specific eight) is the actual fidelity win — the catch-all is what §3.9 exists to eliminate. Crediting every cause to the ball-handler reuses the shipped attribution with zero new machinery and keeps per-player turnover counts identical, so nothing downstream of the turnover counter changes. *(C)* Static tier weights + a modest lean is the proven `SimConfig` pattern (block-recovery, OOB) — controllable tiers, tunable mix, no wild swings — and per-turnover normalization is what mathematically guarantees the leans move only the mix, never the count. *(D)* Parking §3.7-E protects the free premise (a possession-context-dependent weight is the one way this pass could accidentally interact with the aggregates) and keeps §3.9 a labeling pass; the distinct `TURNOVER_OUT_OF_BOUNDS` name on a distinct `PlayType` is the #025 F / #026 E naming discipline applied to avoid a genuine cross-phase collision. *(E)* Reconciliation is free because event *count* is unchanged; the harness line is the cheap instrument that makes the new *mix* visible without pretending a per-cause NBA target exists.  
**Trade-off**: *(A)* None to the aggregates by construction; the only cost is the `isStolen` binary becoming a 9-way draw (one enum + one weighted-pick method). *(B)* The three team-level violations are charged to the ball-handler rather than a "team" or the true culprit — accepted (no per-cause culprit model exists, and the box-score turnover already lands on the on-ball player today). Nine outcome strings is more play-by-play vocabulary to keep coherent — accepted; they are free text with no schema/enum cost. *(C)* The four leaned causes add a few `SimConfig` sensitivities to maintain; kept modest and single-form. *(D)* §3.7-E stays unbuilt — accepted, it is additive and has no consumer. `LOST_BALL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS` is a longer string than §3.8's — accepted, the disambiguation is the point. *(E)* One more harness line to maintain — cheap; and the "free" claim now rests on a structural argument (Decision A) plus a visibility check (the line), not on an unverified assumption.  
**Alternatives considered**: *(structure)* **Fold cause into the gate** (one draw that decides turnover-or-not *and* which kind) — rejected: entangles the calibrated `BASE_TURNOVER` count with the mix, risking the free premise and making the count a function of the cause weights. **Pick it up §3.7-E now** — rejected (Decision D): possession-context weight risks the aggregates and is scope creep on a labeling pass. *(taxonomy)* **6-way / minimal 4-way** — rejected in favor of the user's fuller 9 (richer, and the extra causes are cheap free-text labels). **Keep `LOST_BALL` as a residual catch-all alongside the new causes** — rejected: the catch-all is exactly what §3.9 exists to subdivide; a surviving generic bucket would defeat the pass. *(weights)* **Pure skill-driven weights, no tier constants** — rejected (Decision C): emergent, uncontrollable tiers and wild rare-cause swings; loses the calibration anchor. *(steal share)* **Flatten STOLEN toward the other high-tier causes** — rejected (user call): drops the steal rate well below today's, changing the calibrated `BoxScore.steals` and needing a harness re-confirmation §3.9 otherwise doesn't. *(OOB naming)* **A bare `OUT_OF_BOUNDS` turnover outcome** — rejected: visually collides with §3.8's `OUT_OF_BOUNDS_*` even though the `PlayType` differs; `LOST_BALL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS` is unambiguous. **Fold OOB into `BAD_PASS`/`LOST_BALL`** — rejected (user call): the user wants it as a distinct, visible cause. *(attribution)* **A separate "team violation" attribution** (credit the team, or a picked culprit, for shot-clock/8-second/over-and-back) — rejected: fabricates a participant model no consumer needs; the ball-handler charge reuses the shipped model. **Deferred further sub-types named but not shipped**: `DOUBLE_DRIBBLE` / `CARRYING` (fold into `TRAVELLING`/`LOST_BALL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS` spirit for now), `PALMING`, `KICKED_BALL`, `LANE_VIOLATION`, `INBOUND_5_SECONDS` — real but too rare to earn a distinct weight in the first cut; recorded here so the vocabulary is future-proof (add a weight + enum value when a consumer wants the granularity, no schema cost).  
**Status of §3.9 decisions**: A–E all resolved by this entry (mirrors #021–#026). Net schema change: **none** (`outcome` free text, #020). No OpenAPI change. New engine pieces: a `TurnoverCause` enum (9 values), `TurnoverResolver.pickCause(...)` (weighted draw replacing `isStolen`), new `SimConfig` per-cause base weights + the four lean sensitivities, and a `CalibrationHarness` per-cause turnover breakdown line. Determinism: the cause-draw consumes the seeded RNG in a fixed order — `isTurnover` → `pickCause` → (`pickStealer` only when `STOLEN`) — replacing today's `isTurnover` → `isStolen` → `pickStealer`; existing seed-pinned assertions re-baseline (the stream shifts by one draw shape at each turnover), a controllable structural shift, not stream churn. The execute-ready task sequence is todo.md's "§3.9 execution plan." **Open-at-execution (small, constrained):** the exact per-cause base-weight *numbers* and the four lean sensitivities (placeholders, settled by the harness breakdown line so the mix looks plausible — no hard per-cause target), and whether `pickCause` returns the enum vs. the outcome string directly (a code-shape call).

**Implementation note (from execution, 2026-07).** Shipped exactly as A–E specify — no design divergence; freeness held **by construction** (Decision A), the turnover count did not move. Resolved the three open-at-execution items (§3.9 plan): **(1) `pickCause` returns the enum** (`TurnoverCause`), mirroring `BlockRecovery`/`MissedShotOutcome`; each constant carries its `outcome()` string and `PossessionEngine` calls `cause.outcome()` + `if (cause == STOLEN)`. **(2) digit-leading spelling** — the enum constants use the letter form (`THREE_SECONDS_VIOLATION` / `EIGHT_SECONDS_BACKCOURT_VIOLATION`) while the **`outcome` string keeps the digit form** (`"3_SECONDS_VIOLATION"` / `"8_SECONDS_BACKCOURT_VIOLATION"`) for real-PBP texture; all other constants equal their outcome string. **(3) per-cause weights + leans** — raw tier bases in `SimConfig` (`pickCause` normalizes per-turnover, the `pickStealer`/`BlockResolver` raw-plus-normalize pattern): `TO_WEIGHT_STOLEN/SHOT_CLOCK/OFFENSIVE_FOUL/BAD_PASS/TRAVELLING/LOST_BALL_OOB/THREE_SECONDS/EIGHT_SECONDS_BACKCOURT/OVER_AND_BACK = 56/10/9/8/6/4/3/2/2`, and a single `TO_CAUSE_SENSITIVITY = 0.20` driving the four avg-10 leans via a new `SimConfig.turnoverCauseLean(deviation)` helper (floored ≥ 0.05 so a lean shrinks but never zeroes a cause). `SHOT_CLOCK_VIOLATION` also multiplies by the already-computed `defensivePressure` (the defending coach's `defensiveScheme` multiplier) — **no new coach plumbing needed** (both `teamOffense` and `defensivePressure` were already in scope at the top of `resolvePossession`). **RNG order** is `isTurnover` → `pickCause` → (`pickStealer` iff `STOLEN`), replacing `isTurnover` → `isStolen` → `pickStealer`; `isStolen` removed. The pre-§3.9 `TurnoverResolverTest.isStolenReturnsBothOutcomes` was re-baselined into the `pickCause` unit tests (determinism, STOLEN-dominance, all-nine-reachable, and the weak-offense / low-acumen-plus-pressure mix-shift directions). **Weights unchanged from the seeded placeholders** — the harness settled the mix on the first pass (like §3.7/§3.8 recovery/OOB weights). **Landing (harness, 102 games):** turnover cause mix **STOLEN 56.0% / SHOT_CLOCK_VIOLATION 9.3% / OFFENSIVE_FOUL 9.2% / BAD_PASS 7.5% / TRAVELLING 6.1% / 3_SECONDS 4.2% / LOST_BALL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS 3.4% / 8_SECONDS_BACKCOURT 2.4% / OVER_AND_BACK 1.9%**; the §3.4/§3.5/§3.7/§3.8 aggregates **byte-unchanged** — **112.9 pts / 47.0% FG / 37.5% 3P / 27.7 ast / 13.5 TO / 5.0 blk / 3.0 OOB**, minutes + period-FG% intact, reconciliation (ast+blk) OK. `GameSimulatorIntegrationTest`'s turnover/steal reconciliation is green untouched (a confirm-green — count preserved by construction, E). Coverage: `TurnoverCause` 100%, `SimConfig` 100%, `PossessionEngine` 99.4%, `TurnoverResolver` 95.8% (the two uncovered lines are the cumulative-walk fallback `return`s — the same defensive edge `pickStealer` already leaves uncovered); full `mvn clean install` gate green (JDK 21).

**§3.9 follow-up (carry forward)**:
- **§3.7-E, still parked** — elevated `SHOT_CLOCK_VIOLATION` weight on block-recovered / second-chance possessions (a possession-context-dependent weight); additive if a consumer wants it, deliberately not built here (Decision D).
- **Finer turnover sub-types** — `DOUBLE_DRIBBLE`/`CARRYING`/`PALMING`/`KICKED_BALL`/`LANE_VIOLATION`/`INBOUND_5_SECONDS`; add a weight + enum value when granularity is wanted (Decision E alternatives).
- (The §3.6 time-column and §3.7 skilled-recovery follow-ups still stand.)

### 028 — Loose-ball / rebounding fouls + the shared team-foul/bonus substrate (§3.10): a rebound-phase foul roll carved off the top, a two-sided (offense/defense) foul with a new `committing_team_id` column, penalty status DERIVED from the FOUL event log, and bonus free throws reusing the existing FT machinery
**Date**: 2026-07  
**Scope**: §3.10 is the fourth possession-fidelity completion sub-phase (§3.7–§3.11, before Phase 4). It makes a **non-shooting foul during the rebound phase** (box-out push, over-the-back) a real event — today fouls fire *only* on drive/post shot attempts (`FoulResolver.isFoul`, gated by `shotType.isContactType()`), so the rebound contest is entirely foul-free. It also builds the **team-foul / bonus (penalty) substrate** the roadmap flags as **shared with §3.11** (a team's period foul count → bonus free throws), designed and built once here. This is **engine work** (a new foul branch + the penalty model), not API/stats work. Per the user's scope call, §3.10 covers **the substrate + rebounding fouls only**; §3.11 (and-1) is left to its own later design+execution so the two recalibration sources are not tuned at once. **Net schema change: ONE additive nullable column** — `game_event.committing_team_id` (Decision D, user call) — a deliberate, conscious reversal of the schema-free stance §3.7–§3.9 held, taken because §3.10 models **two-sided** rebounding fouls (Decision A2) whose committing team must be attributable, and the column has a real day-one consumer (the penalty derivation), so it is *not* a field fabricated ahead of a consumer (#014/#017/#020). NOT YET BUILT — this entry is the resolved design; the execute-ready plan is todo.md's §3.10 plan.

**Decision A1 — the penalty is DERIVED from the FOUL event log, not a stored team-foul counter (the crux, user call).** "Team T is in the bonus this period" is computed on demand as `count(FOUL events committed by team T in period P) >= SimConfig.BONUS_FOULS_PER_PERIOD` — **no `teamFouls` field, no reset logic.** This is the exact discipline #023 F applied to foul-*outs* (a derived predicate over the `fouls` counter, explicitly *not* a stored `disqualified` flag, to avoid the #013/#015 duplicate-source-of-truth trap) carried to the team level: the fact already lives in the `FOUL` events (#020, events are the source of truth), so a stored counter could only ever *disagree* with them. **Threshold**: the modern-NBA **5th team foul in a period** puts the other team in the bonus (`BONUS_FOULS_PER_PERIOD = 5`, a `SimConfig` placeholder tunable via the harness). **Both** `SHOOTING_FOUL` and the new rebounding foul count toward the period tally (all fouls count in reality) — one unified derivation over all `FOUL` events, not split per foul type. **The predicate is evaluated AFTER counting the current foul (user call):** the FOUL event is emitted, then the derivation includes it, so the *Nth* foul (the one that reaches the threshold) **itself awards the bonus FTs** — "in the bonus" means the count *has reached* the limit. (Emit-then-check, not check-then-emit.)

**Decision A2 — rebounding fouls are TWO-SIDED (offense OR defense can commit), so the committing team must be explicit (user call).** A rebounding foul is not always on the defense: a defensive **box-out push** fouls the offensive rebounder (defense commits → offense to the line / retains), but an offensive **over-the-back** fouls the defender (offense commits → defense's ball). §3.10 models **both**, **defense-leaning** (box-out contact dominates; over-the-back is the minority — a `SimConfig` placeholder split, e.g. defense ~70–80%, harness-tunable). Because either team can commit, the committing team is **not** recoverable from the possession's `offense/defense` orientation alone the way a `SHOOTING_FOUL` (always the defender) is — hence Decision D's explicit column. This is the deliberate difference from a defense-only model (rejected below): it is more realistic and forks possession *both* directions, and it is what makes the `committing_team_id` column earn its keep.

**Decision B — reward: possession-retention under the bonus, bonus free throws once IN the penalty; the possession fork depends on WHO fouled (user requirement).** On a rebounding foul, the **fouled** team (the non-committing side) is the beneficiary:
- **Defense commits** (box-out) → the **offense** is fouled → offense retains for a second chance (under the bonus) OR shoots bonus FTs (in the penalty). Under the bonus this is another **offense-retention** path (like the offensive rebound / §3.7 recovery / §3.8 OOB-offense), respecting `MAX_OFFENSIVE_REBOUNDS_PER_POSSESSION`.
- **Offense commits** (over-the-back) → the **defense** is fouled → **possession ends** for the offense, defense gets the ball (under the bonus) OR the defense shoots bonus FTs (in the penalty; the possession still ends for the offense).

Once the **committing** team is in the penalty for the period (A1's derived predicate), the foul goes **straight to free throws** for the fouled team — the user's explicit bonus requirement. The bonus FTs **reuse the existing FT-award block verbatim** (`PossessionEngine` lines ~136–144: the `for` over `SimConfig.FREE_THROWS_PER_FOUL`, `recordFreeThrowAttempt()`/`recordFreeThrowMade()`, `MADE`/`MISSED` `FREE_THROW` events, `addScore`) — no new FT machinery, FGA/FT reconciliation automatic. The FT shooter is a player on the fouled team.

**Decision C — the foul roll is layered on the rebound phase, BEFORE the board contest, mirroring the §3.7 block carve.** In the miss-resolution flow (owned by `MissedShotResolver`, #026), a **rebound-foul roll runs first**; on a foul it **short-circuits** the four-way board draw and resolves per Decision B (the board contest never runs — the whistle stopped play). This is the same "carve a slice off the top, then run the existing contest on the remainder" shape §3.7 used for `P(BLOCK)` (#025 A1) — it keeps the foul probability **independent of the rebound weights** (so the rebound-foul rate is tuned on its own, not entangled with `offenseRebound`/`defenseRebound`), which the alternative (a fifth outcome inside the four-way draw) could not do. The foul probability is a small base scaled by the same discipline/pressure skills the shooting foul already uses (`foulProne` / `defensivePressure`, the coach.md pressure/breakdown trade-off) — the exact skill wiring is an execution detail, constrained to the avg-10 form (#021 C / #022).

**Decision D — attribution & event vocabulary: a `FOUL` event, a new `committing_team_id` column, two-sided `outcome`, committer as `primary_player` (user call — add the column).** The rebounding foul is a `PlayType.FOUL` event, `primary_player_id` = the committing player, `outcome` distinguishing side (leading candidates `REBOUNDING_FOUL_DEFENSE` / `REBOUNDING_FOUL_OFFENSE`, mirroring §3.8's `OUT_OF_BOUNDS_OFFENSE`/`_DEFENSE` suffix discipline; final spelling at execution) — **no new `PlayType`** (a rebounding foul is a *kind of foul*, the #025 F / #026 E reuse discipline). It **increments the committing player's `fouls`** (feeds foul-outs, #023 F) exactly as the shooting foul does. **A new nullable `game_event.committing_team_id` column** carries the committing team on every `FOUL` event (populated for `SHOOTING_FOUL` = the defender's team too, so the penalty derivation reads one uniform field). This is the one schema change (Scope): it mirrors the existing `assist_player_id` column exactly — a nullable `VARCHAR` threaded through `GameEventEntity`, `GameData.EventRecord`, `addEvent(...)` (an overload, as `assistPlayerId` already has), `GameSimulator`, and `EntityMapper`, appended to `release.1.0.4.game.sql` (the unreleased game DDL — no new release), a plain column add with no `dbms` gate (H2 + Postgres both fine). **Why the column over the alternatives:** with two-sided fouls (A2), the committing team is no longer implied by `defense_team_id` (as it is for a shooting foul), so it must be either encoded in the `outcome` suffix (decode-at-count) or resolved from `primary_player_id` → roster (a lookup coupling the derivation to roster state). The column answers "which team committed this event" **uniformly for every play type, queryably, forever** — a durable win for Phase 4 stats (fouls-by-team, fouls-drawn) and §3.11, with a real day-one consumer (the penalty count), so it is not a fabricated-ahead-of-consumer field. The `FREE_THROW` events in the bonus case are the existing `MADE`/`MISSED` vocabulary — a bonus FT is indistinguishable from a shooting-foul FT except by its preceding `FOUL` (accepted; nothing consumes the distinction).

**Decision E — recalibration is EXPECTED (small), and the instrument is added first.** Unlike §3.8/§3.9 (free/free-by-construction), §3.10 **adds free-throw volume that does not exist today** (bonus FTs) → it lifts points, so it earns a **recalibration pass** like §3.7 (nudge `BASE_FOUL` / the new rebound-foul base down, or accept the lift, re-agreed via the harness). Per the #026 D "build the instrument before you claim neutrality" discipline, **add a `CalibrationHarness` line for team fouls/period + the bonus-FT rate** (next to the existing aggregates) so the new FT source is *visible* and "the FT rate still holds / points re-centered" is a checkable claim, not an assumption. The existing FT/points aggregates plus this line bound the recalibration.

**Rationale**: *(A1)* Deriving the penalty from events is the same win #023 F took for foul-outs and #020 established for the whole engine — one source of truth, no reset bugs, exactly the mechanism the user reasoned toward (log the foul + committer, compute bonus-closeness from the log). A stored counter would be a second copy of a fact the events already hold, the #013/#015 trap. Emit-then-count (the Nth foul awards the bonus) is the simpler ordering and a defensible reading of "in the bonus." *(A2)* Two-sided fouls are more realistic (over-the-back is a real offensive foul) and fork possession correctly both ways; they are the reason the committing team can't be inferred from orientation, which motivates the column honestly rather than as speculation. *(B)* Possession-retention-then-bonus-FTs is real basketball and the user's requirement; reusing the shipped FT block means zero new FT/scoring machinery and automatic reconciliation; forking on who-fouled reuses the established retain-vs-end shape (§3.7/§3.8). *(C)* Carving the foul off the top (vs. a fifth draw outcome) keeps the rebound-foul rate independently tunable and reuses the proven §3.7 block-carve shape, so the rebound contest stays the pure two-way skill draw #026 built. *(D)* The `committing_team_id` column answers "which team committed this" uniformly and queryably for every play type — a durable Phase-4/§3.11 asset with a real day-one consumer (the penalty count), so it is not fabricated ahead of a consumer; it mirrors `assist_player_id` exactly, so the add is mechanical and low-risk. Reusing `PlayType.FOUL` + a suffixed `outcome` keeps the play-type vocabulary from fragmenting (#025 F/#026 E). *(E)* §3.10 genuinely adds points (bonus FTs), so — unlike the last two passes — it is *not* free; the honest move is to expect the recalibration and build the instrument that makes it checkable (#026 D).  
**Trade-off**: *(A1)* Deriving the penalty means a scan of the current period's `FOUL` events at each rebound-foul check inside the possession loop — cheap (one period, a handful of events) and the same derive-over-events cost #023 F accepted, but a real (if tiny) per-check cost vs. an O(1) counter read. Accepted. *(A2)* Two-sided fouls add a possession-fork branch (offense-commits ends the possession, defense-commits retains) and a side-selection roll. *(B)* Bonus FTs add scoring → the recalibration of (E). *(D)* **This reverses the schema-free stance §3.7–§3.9 held** — §3.10 is no longer "no schema change." Accepted as a conscious, user-approved call: the column is small, mirrors a shipped one, appends to the *unreleased* game DDL, and buys a uniform committing-team answer with real consumers. The alternative (schema-free) would have pushed that answer into an `outcome` suffix decode or a roster lookup at count time. *(E)* One more harness line + a recalibration pass to run and re-agree.  
**Alternatives considered**: *(A1)* **A transient within-game team-foul counter** (reset per period) — rejected (user call): reintroduces a second source of truth for a fact the `FOUL` events already hold, the #013/#015 duplicate-state smell #023 F explicitly avoided. **A persisted per-period team-foul *count* column** — rejected: *that* would be storing derived state (the #020 "no in-game state columns" stance); the `committing_team_id` column (D) is different — it stores a raw *fact* about the event (who committed it), not a running total. **Predicate evaluated BEFORE the current foul** (only the (N+1)th foul draws FTs) — rejected (user call): emit-then-count is simpler and the Nth-foul-awards reading is fine. *(A2)* **Defense-only rebounding fouls** (committing team always `defense_team_id`, no column needed) — rejected (user call): less realistic (no over-the-back), and though it would keep §3.10 schema-free, the user chose the richer two-sided model + the durable column. **Roughly-even off/def split** — rejected: over-the-back is genuinely rarer than defensive box-out contact; defense-leaning is more accurate. *(B)* **Always award bonus FTs on any rebounding foul** (ignore the threshold) — rejected: unrealistic, largest scoring lift. **Never award bonus FTs in §3.10** — rejected: builds the derivation without exercising it and drops the user's bonus requirement. *(C)* **A fifth `REBOUND_FOUL` outcome inside the four-way missed-shot draw** — rejected: couples the foul probability to the rebound weights; the top-carve keeps them independently tunable (the §3.7 shape). *(D)* **Encode the committing side in the `outcome` suffix only, no column** (`REBOUNDING_FOUL_OFFENSE`/`_DEFENSE` + off/def team ids, decode at count) — rejected (user call): keeps §3.10 schema-free but the "which team" answer lives in vocabulary, not a queryable field, and every consumer must decode it. **Resolve `primary_player_id` → team at count time** — rejected: couples the penalty derivation to roster state and adds a per-foul lookup. **A dedicated `PlayType.REBOUNDING_FOUL`** — rejected: fragments the play-type vocabulary (#025 F/#026 E). *(scope)* **Design §3.11 (and-1) in this pass too** — rejected (user call): §3.11 restructures the possession branching (foul *alongside* the shot) and is the biggest recalibration; coupling them would tune two scoring sources at once.  
**Status of §3.10 decisions**: A1/A2/B/C/D/E all resolved by this entry (mirrors #021–#027). Net schema change: **ONE additive nullable column** — `game_event.committing_team_id` (D), appended to the unreleased `release.1.0.4.game.sql`, mirroring `assist_player_id`; the penalty itself is derived, not stored (A1). New engine pieces: a rebound-phase foul roll (in/around `MissedShotResolver` + a `FoulResolver` extension or sibling) with a two-sided (defense-leaning) side draw, a **derived penalty predicate** over the `FOUL` event log (committing team + period, emit-then-count), a `SimConfig` rebound-foul base rate + off/def split + `BONUS_FOULS_PER_PERIOD`, the two-sided possession fork + bonus-FT path reusing the existing FT block, two new `REBOUNDING_FOUL_*` `outcome` strings, the `committing_team_id` plumbing (`GameEventEntity`/`EventRecord`/`addEvent`/`GameSimulator`/`EntityMapper` + the DDL), and a `CalibrationHarness` team-fouls/bonus-FT line. Determinism: the rebound-foul roll (+ its side draw) consumes the seeded RNG at a fixed point in the miss flow (before the board draw) — existing seed-pinned rebound assertions re-baseline (a controlled structural shift, as §3.7/§3.9 did). The execute-ready task sequence is todo.md's "§3.10 execution plan." **Open-at-execution (small, constrained):** the exact `REBOUNDING_FOUL_*` outcome spelling; the rebound-foul base rate + the off/def split + `BONUS_FOULS_PER_PERIOD` numbers (placeholders, settled by the harness line); whether the foul roll lives in an extended `FoulResolver` method or a small sibling resolver; and the size/direction of the recalibration (E), re-agreed with the user against the harness.

**Implementation note (from execution, 2026-07).** Shipped as A1/A2/B/C/D/E specify — no design divergence, plus **one unplanned addition forced by execution** (the `PROB_FLOOR` trap, below). Resolved the four open-at-execution items: **(1) outcome spellings** — the leading candidates unchanged, `REBOUNDING_FOUL_DEFENSE` / `REBOUNDING_FOUL_OFFENSE`, carried on a `ReboundFoul.Side` enum whose constants own their `outcome()` string (the #027 `TurnoverCause` shape). **(2) Resolver placement** — an **extended `FoulResolver`** (`resolveReboundFoul(offense, defense, defensivePressure, rng)` returning a nullable `ReboundFoul` record), not a sibling resolver: `FoulResolver` already owns the `foulProne`/`foulDrawing`/`defensivePressure` wiring and the FT machinery, so a sibling would have duplicated all of it for no separation gain. The committer is picked by a `foulProne`-weighted draw over the committing five (the `ReboundResolver.pickWeighted` pattern). **(3) The constants** — `REBOUND_FOUL_BASE = 0.055`, `REBOUND_FOUL_DEFENSE_WEIGHT`/`REBOUND_FOUL_OFFENSE_WEIGHT = 0.75/0.25`, `BONUS_FOULS_PER_PERIOD = 5` (the modern-NBA rule, kept as designed). **(4) The recalibration (E)** — see below.

**The unplanned addition — `PROB_FLOOR` made the new knob one-directional.** `SimConfig.clampProbability` floors every probability at `PROB_FLOOR = 0.02`, which exists so a skill mismatch can never make a *normal* outcome impossible. Applied to a deliberately-rare carve with a base near 0.03, it instead acts as a **floor the base cannot go below** — the knob became tunable only *upward*, and the first "turn the rebound-foul rate down" recalibration attempt **silently did nothing** (halving `REBOUND_FOUL_BASE` from 0.030 to 0.015 moved points by 0.1). This is the same class of problem `BLOCK_SENSITIVITY` solved for §3.7 (a global constant tuned for common events being wrong for a rare one), so it took the same shape: a new `SimConfig.rareEventProbability(base, drivingSkill, opposingSkill, sensitivity)` that keeps the avg-10 contest but clamps at **[0, PROB_CEILING]** with no floor, plus a rebound-foul-specific `REBOUND_FOUL_SENSITIVITY = 0.10` (the global 0.5 would swamp a 0.055 base exactly as it swamped the block bases). Guarded by a regression test (`reboundFoulBaseIsTunableDownward`) asserting a sub-floor base is not floored up and a zero base yields zero — i.e. the feature can still be switched off, which is what makes E's "nudge the rebound-foul base down" a real option for §3.11.

**Recalibration (E) — the lift was real but NOT mostly where the design expected.** §3.10 landed **+2.7 pts/team** before tuning (112.9 → 115.6). Decomposed on the harness: only **~1.1 pts is bonus free throws** — the larger share is **retained possessions**, because a defensive box-out foul under the bonus hands the offense a second chance where a defensive rebound would have ended the possession. #028 E anticipated the FT lift; the possession-retention channel is the bigger one and was not called out in the design. Two tuning findings worth recording: **`BASE_FOUL` is the wrong lever and moves points the wrong way** — trimming it 0.15 → 0.138 *raised* points to 116.1, because a shooting foul *ends* a possession after 2 FTs, so removing one converts it back into a live shot attempt worth more than ~1.5 expected FT points at this FG%. And **`REBOUND_FOUL_BASE` alone cannot absorb the lift** (~1 pt across its useful range). The recalibration therefore used the **§3.7 lever run in reverse** — the shot `BASE_*` rates trimmed ~1.2% (`BASE_DRIVE` 0.605→0.5975, `BASE_PERIMETER` 0.445→0.4375, `BASE_THREE` 0.345→0.3375, `BASE_POST` 0.505→0.4975), exactly the knob §3.7 nudged *up* to refill points blocks removed. **Landing agreed with the user at 113.8** rather than chasing 112.9 exactly: trimming further pulls FG% below its calibrated target, and trading a calibrated number for an uncalibrated one is a bad swap. **Landing (harness, 102 games): 113.8 pts / 46.9% FG / 37.6% 3P / 27.7 ast / 14.2 TO / 5.0 blk / 2.8 OOB**, §3.5 minutes + period-FG% intact, reconciliation (ast+blk) OK. New §3.10 line: **fouls 3.95/team/period** (bonus at 5), **37.1% of team-periods reach the penalty**, **rebounding fouls 1.75 def + 0.48 off per team/game**, **bonus FTA 0.8/team/game (3.2% of all FTs, 0.7 pts)**. The **turnovers 13.5 → 14.2** move was investigated rather than waved through: flow-entries barely rose (116.2 → 117.2) while turnovers rose 5%, so the per-check rate genuinely rose 11.6% → 12.1% — the cause is `isTurnover`'s `fatigueFactor()` dependency (more glass scrambles → more possessions played → more fatigue → worse `ballSecurity`), a correct §3.5 emergent effect, and 14.2 is *closer* to the ~14 target than the baseline. **Observed off/def rebounding-foul split is 78/22, not the configured 75/25** — defensive fouls compound (each retained possession generates another miss and another roll); left as emergent rather than back-solving the weights.

**Divergences from the design, in full:** (1) the `rareEventProbability` + `REBOUND_FOUL_SENSITIVITY` addition above (C stated "the exact skill wiring is an execution detail, constrained to the avg-10 form" — this stays in that form, but needed its own clamp). (2) **`EntityMapper` needed no change**: the todo.md plan anticipated threading the column through the read path, but the OpenAPI `GameEvent` model has no `committingTeamId` and §3.10 forbids an OpenAPI change, so the column is engine/persistence-only for now — it is queryable in the DB (its Phase-4 stats consumer) but not yet surfaced on the play-by-play API. Adding it there is a one-line additive spec change whenever a consumer wants it. (3) Two pre-§3.10 `PossessionEngineTest` invariants were **re-baselined, not deleted**, because §3.10 genuinely invalidates their premises: `everyMissedShotIsFollowedByARebound` → `...OrAReboundingFoul` (a foul short-circuits the board contest, C), and `foulEventsFollowedByTwoFreeThrows` → `shootingFoulEventsFollowedByTwoFreeThrows` (a rebounding foul under the bonus awards possession, not FTs, B). **Coverage:** `FoulResolver`, `ReboundFoul`, `ReboundFoul.Side`, `GameData`, `SimConfig`, `MissedShotResolver` all **100%**; `PossessionEngine` **99.5%** (the one uncovered line is its pre-existing 99.4% edge). Full `mvn clean install` gate green — **421 unit + 52 Cucumber**, all coverage checks met (JDK 21).

**§3.10 follow-up (carry forward)**:
- **§3.11 (and-1 / shooting foul on a made basket)** builds on this substrate — the team-foul/bonus derivation (A) and the bonus-FT path (B) are reused; §3.11 adds the foul-*alongside*-the-shot restructuring and its own (larger) recalibration. Note for that pass: the **possession-retention channel, not the FT channel, is where a foul pass leaks points** (see the implementation note) — budget the recalibration against retained possessions first.
- **`committing_team_id` is not yet on the API** — the column is populated and queryable but absent from the OpenAPI `GameEvent` (see divergence 2). Additive whenever a play-by-play or Phase-4 stats consumer wants "which team committed this".
- **The observed off/def rebounding-foul split (78/22) drifts from the configured 75/25** — emergent from defensive fouls compounding through retained possessions. Back-solve the weights only if a consumer ever needs the *observed* split to hit a target.
- (The §3.7-E shot-clock-pressure, §3.9 finer turnover sub-types, and §3.6 time-column follow-ups still stand.)

---

### 029 — And-1 / shooting foul on a made basket (§3.11): a second post-make foul roll carved beside the assist, a per-situation free-throw count (and-1 = 1), a self-describing `FREE_THROW` source type retiring §3.10's bonus/shooting-foul FT ambiguity, and a rare-event and-1 rate with its own base + sensitivity
**Date**: 2026-08  
**Scope**: §3.11 is the fifth possession-fidelity completion sub-phase (§3.7–§3.13, before Phase 4). It is **not** the last — this design pass split the previously-planned §3.11 end-state into two follow-on sub-phases (see Decision A2 and the follow-up list): **§3.12** (all-shot-type contact fouls + graduated rate + fouled-three = 3 FTs) and **§3.13** (flagrant/technical fouls). §3.11 makes an **and-1** — a defensive foul on a shot that still goes in — a real event: a made FG **plus one** free throw, where today a foul fires *instead of* the shot (`PossessionEngine`'s pre-shot foul branch returns before the make/miss roll, so a foul and a made basket are mutually exclusive). This is **engine work** restructuring the post-make block, not API/stats work. It reuses the §3.10 substrate wholesale — the team-foul/bonus derivation (`GameData.isInBonus`, #028 A1) and the extracted `awardFreeThrows` block (#028 B) — and adds no new substrate. **Deliberately kept narrow (Decision A2, user call):** §3.11 draws and-1s **only on made DRIVE/POST** (`made && shotType.isContactType()`), staying inside today's foul calibration; widening contact so **all** shot types (perimeter, three) can draw a foul — and the 3-point shooting foul = 3 FTs — is **split out to the new §3.12**, and flagrant/technical fouls to the new **§3.13**, so §3.11 tunes exactly one new scoring source. **Net schema change: NONE** — the and-1 is a `PlayType.FOUL` event (`AND_ONE` outcome) with `committing_team_id = defTeamId` (the simple one-sided case, reusing #028 D's column), and the new free-throw source type is an `outcome` string on the existing `PlayType.FREE_THROW` (#020, free-text outcome). **One deferred-debt fix rides along (Decision D):** §3.10 accepted that bonus and shooting-foul FTs are indistinguishable except by the preceding `FOUL`; at **three** FT sources that is retired here by tagging each `FREE_THROW` with its source. **SHIPPED 2026-08** — see the implementation note below.

**Decision A1 — the and-1 is a SECOND, post-make foul roll, not a restructured single foul roll (the crux, user call).** The existing pre-shot foul branch (`PossessionEngine` `// 2. Foul check`) is **kept exactly as-is** — it remains the "fouled, shot stopped, 2-FT trip" case, and `BASE_FOUL = 0.15` keeps meaning precisely "P(a contact foul stops the shot)". The and-1 is a **new, independent roll placed after the make is recorded** — inside the `if (made)` block, immediately after the assist roll (see A3), before the return. On a hit: award **one** FT (D/B) via the reused `awardFreeThrows`, emit an `AND_ONE` `FOUL` event, charge the defender's foul, and count it toward the period tally (§3.10 A1) like any other foul; the points from the made FG are already recorded, so the and-1 only *adds* the FT. This is the **§3.7 block-carve / §3.10 rebound-foul-carve shape a third time**: a separate roll layered on an existing outcome, never entangled with the outcome it rides — which is what keeps its rate independently tunable and `BASE_FOUL`'s §3.4 calibration legible.

**Decision A2 — §3.11 draws and-1s on made DRIVE/POST only; all-shot-type contact + 3pt-fouls = 3 FTs is split to §3.12 (user call).** The and-1 roll is gated `made && shotType.isContactType()`, i.e. only on a made `DRIVE` or `POST` (`ShotType.isContactType()`, unchanged — `PERIMETER`/`THREE` are not contact types). This is the **conscious, temporary** limitation: today's whole foul model already gates on `isContactType`, so an and-1 on a perimeter/three shot has no path anyway, and opening that mid-phase would reopen the "can a jump shot be fouled, and at what graduated rate?" question **and** activate the latent fouled-three bug (below) — two recalibrations at once, the exact coupling §3.10's scope call avoided. **The user's stated end-state is that ALL shot types can draw an and-1** (a closeout on a three is a foul), at a **graduated** rate (post frequent → three rare), **not** the current binary. That, plus the **fouled-three = 3 FTs** fix, is **§3.12** (both share one root cause: the binary `isContactType`). The parameterized FT count (D) is the seam §3.12 reuses to pass `3`.

**Decision A3 — the and-1 roll sits BESIDE the assist roll in the post-make block; the two coexist and do not interact.** A made and-1 is still eligible for an assist (real basketball), and the assist odds are unaffected by whether a foul was drawn. In `possession-flow.puml` this is an **`And1` partition immediately after the `Assist` partition**, both inside `SHOT — MADE`. In code the assist is rolled first (its `assistPlayerId` is stamped on the `SHOT` event), then the and-1 roll runs and — on a hit — emits its own `FOUL` + `FREE_THROW` events **after** the `SHOT` event. The only correctness obligation is that a `FOUL`/`FREE_THROW` landing between the `SHOT` and the next possession must not disturb assist reconciliation (the assist is already on the `SHOT` event, so it does not) — guarded by a test, not a design change.

**Decision B — reward: made FG points (already recorded) + exactly ONE free throw via the reused `awardFreeThrows`.** The and-1 does **not** re-roll or re-score the field goal — the `if (made)` block has already recorded the 2 points, the FGM, and (possibly) the assist. The and-1 adds a single FT for the shooter through the **same** extracted `awardFreeThrows` block §3.10 reused (`recordFreeThrowAttempt`/`recordFreeThrowMade`, `MADE`/`MISSED` `FREE_THROW` events, `addScore` +1), so FGA/FT/points reconciliation stays automatic. Unlike a §3.10 rebounding foul, the and-1 **never forks the possession** — a made basket already ends the offense's possession; the FT is a bonus tacked on before the ball changes hands. The and-1 foul still counts toward the committing (defense) team's period foul tally (§3.10 A1); it does **not** itself consult the bonus (an and-1 is always exactly 1 FT regardless of penalty status — that is the rule).

**Decision C — the and-1 rate is a RARE-EVENT probability with its own base + sensitivity (`AND_ONE_BASE` + `AND_ONE_SENSITIVITY`), reusing `rareEventProbability`.** The and-1 roll reuses the **method** `SimConfig.rareEventProbability(base, drivingSkill, opposingSkill, sensitivity)` (#028's floor-free clamp to `[0, PROB_CEILING]`), fed a new `AND_ONE_BASE` (thin — an and-1 is a minority of made contact shots) and a gentle `AND_ONE_SENSITIVITY` **of its own**. It does **not** reuse `contestProbability` (its `PROB_FLOOR = 0.02` would trap a thin base tunable-only-upward — the #028 `PROB_FLOOR` trap, discovered on rebounding fouls), and it does **not** reuse the global `SENSITIVITY = 0.5` (which swamps a thin base — the §3.7 blocks + §3.10 rebound-foul finding, twice over). The skill inputs are the shooter's `foulDrawing` against the defender's effective discipline (`foulProne` inverted + fatigue), the exact avg-10 wiring `FoulResolver.isFoul` already uses. Base/sensitivity are `SimConfig` placeholders settled by the harness (E). **This is the "reuse the machine, give it its own two dials" call the user reasoned toward** — no new formula, but its own base and sensitivity so a thin rate stays independently tunable in both directions.

**Decision D — free throws become SELF-DESCRIBING: a source type on the `FREE_THROW` event, retiring §3.10's accepted bonus/shooting-foul FT ambiguity (user call — fix it now).** §3.10 (#028 D) accepted that a bonus FT is indistinguishable from a shooting-foul FT except by looking backward at the preceding `FOUL` event. §3.11 makes a **third** FT source (and-1), so that backward-join ambiguity worsens and is retired here: every `FREE_THROW` event carries its **source** — the leading candidates `SHOOTING` / `BONUS` / `AND_ONE` — so an FT is self-describing and a Phase-4 stats consumer can split FT% by source without replaying the event sequence. Mechanically this is a **per-situation FT count *and* source threaded through `awardFreeThrows`**: today the block hard-loops `SimConfig.FREE_THROWS_PER_FOUL = 2`; it gains a **count** parameter (a new `AND_ONE_FREE_THROWS = 1`; shooting foul + bonus keep `FREE_THROWS_PER_FOUL = 2`) and a **source** parameter stamped onto each emitted `FREE_THROW` event's `outcome`. All **three** existing/new call sites pass their count + source (shooting foul, §3.10 bonus, new and-1). **No schema change** — the source is an `outcome` string (#020, free text); the exact spelling (e.g. `MADE_AND_ONE` vs. a separate source field on the event) is deferred to execution, constrained to not collide with the existing `MADE`/`MISSED` and to keep made/missed still readable. The and-1 `FOUL` event itself is `outcome = AND_ONE`, `committing_team_id = defTeamId` (one-sided — reuses #028 D's column with no new plumbing).

**Decision E — recalibration is EXPECTED and is a PURE-ADDITIVE points lift; build the and-1 + FT-source harness line first, tune the shot `BASE_*` lever, and steer by MULTIPLE runs.** Unlike §3.10 (where most of the lift turned out to be retained possessions), §3.11 adds FTs **on top of shots that already scored** with **no offsetting removal** — a pure additive scoring source, so expect a **larger, cleaner** points lift than §3.10's. Per #026 D "build the instrument before you claim a number," **add a `CalibrationHarness` line for the and-1 rate + the FT-source split** (extending §3.10's fouls/bonus-FT line) *before* tuning. Two findings carried from #028's implementation note are load-bearing here: **`BASE_FOUL` is the wrong lever and moves points the wrong way** (trimming it converts a possession-ending 2-FT trip back into a live shot worth more), so the real knob is the **shot `BASE_*` lever run in reverse** (trim to re-center), exactly §3.10's fix. And **steer by multiple harness runs (different seeds), tuning to the mean**, not a single run — a lone run carries per-seed noise that can bait an over-correction (the user's explicit guard against low/high randomness).

**Rationale**: *(A1)* A second post-make roll keeps `BASE_FOUL` meaning exactly what it means today ("P(foul stops the shot)"); the single-restructured-roll alternative forces `BASE_FOUL` to be re-decomposed into P(contact) × P(shot-survives-contact), reopening a §3.4-calibrated lever and destroying the legibility the phase is supposed to protect. It is also the same carve-a-slice-off-the-top shape §3.7/§3.10 proved, so its rate stays independently tunable (C). *(A2)* Gating on the existing `isContactType` keeps §3.11 inside today's foul calibration and tunes exactly one new source; the all-shot-type widening is genuinely a *different* change (a graduated per-shot-type foul rate) that also carries the fouled-three FT-count fix, so it is honestly its own phase (§3.12), not scope creep folded in. *(A3)* And-1 and assist are independent facts about the same made basket; placing the and-1 roll beside the assist mirrors how the puml already reads and reuses the `if (made)` seam with no branching change. *(B)* Adding one FT to an already-scored make (no re-roll, no possession fork) is the minimal honest model of an and-1 and reuses the shipped FT block, so reconciliation is automatic; not consulting the bonus is the rule (an and-1 is always 1 FT). *(C)* The §3.7 and §3.10 passes each independently discovered that a rare foul-type needs its own base + sensitivity and a floor-free clamp; doing that from the start here avoids rediscovering it a third time, while reusing `rareEventProbability` adds no new formula. *(D)* Three FT sources make the backward-join ambiguity real; a self-describing FT is the derive-nothing-at-read-time win (#020), has a real Phase-4 consumer (per-source FT%), and the `awardFreeThrows` choke point makes the change small (one count + one source parameter, three call sites). *(E)* §3.11 is genuinely additive with no offset, so it is *not* free; the honest move is to expect the recalibration, build the instrument, reuse §3.10's proven shot-`BASE_*` lever (not the wrong-way `BASE_FOUL`), and guard against seed noise with multiple runs.  
**Trade-off**: *(A1)* A second foul roll adds one RNG draw per made contact shot at a fixed point in the possession loop (existing seed-pinned assertions re-baseline, as every §3.7–§3.10 pass did). *(A2)* §3.11 knowingly ships an incomplete contact model (no perimeter/three and-1s) until §3.12 — the first and-1 leaderboards will under-count fouls drawn on jump shooters. Accepted as the scope fence that keeps the recalibration single-source. *(A3)* None beyond one guard test. *(B)* Adds FT volume → the recalibration of (E). *(C)* Two more `SimConfig` constants. *(D)* `awardFreeThrows` gains two parameters and all three call sites change; a small, mechanical widening of §3.10's extracted block, and it touches the shooting-foul + bonus call sites that were previously untagged (their FTs now carry `SHOOTING`/`BONUS`) — a re-baseline of any test asserting bare `MADE`/`MISSED` FT outcomes. *(E)* One more harness line, a recalibration pass, and multiple-run discipline to run and re-agree.  
**Alternatives considered**: *(A1)* **One restructured foul roll whose consequence depends on make/miss** (foul → if shot would have gone in, and-1; else 2-FT trip) — rejected (user call): forces `BASE_FOUL` to be re-decomposed into contact × survives-contact, reopening a §3.4-calibrated number and muddying the very legibility the phase protects; the second-roll carve keeps `BASE_FOUL` untouched. *(A2)* **Draw and-1s on ALL shot types in §3.11** (perimeter + three, graduated rate, 3pt = 3 FTs) — rejected (user call): that is a graduated per-shot-type foul rate + the fouled-three FT fix, a second recalibration and a second latent-bug fix folded into the and-1 pass; split to §3.12 so each lands against a known baseline (the §3.10 scope-split discipline). **Leave the binary `isContactType` as the permanent and-1 gate** — rejected (user call): the user's end-state is that all shot types can draw an and-1; the binary is temporary, not final. *(B)* **Re-roll the field goal as part of the and-1** — rejected: the make is already decided and scored; re-rolling would double-count. **Fork the possession on an and-1** — rejected: a made basket already ends the offense's possession;
