there is nothing to fork. **Consult the bonus for the and-1 FT count** — rejected: an and-1 is always exactly 1 FT by rule, independent of penalty status. *(C)* **Reuse `contestProbability` / the global `SENSITIVITY`** — rejected: the `PROB_FLOOR` trap (#028) makes a thin base tunable-only-upward, and the global 0.5 swamps a thin base (§3.7/§3.10, twice) — a rare event needs the floor-free `rareEventProbability` + its own gentle sensitivity. **A single shared rare-foul sensitivity across rebound-fouls and and-1s** — rejected: different events with different feel; §3.10 already has its own `REBOUND_FOUL_SENSITIVITY`, and and-1s get their own to match. *(D)* **Keep FTs undifferentiated** (accept §3.10's bonus/shooting ambiguity at three sources, distinguish only by the preceding `FOUL`) — rejected (user call): at three FT sources the backward-join is fragile and a Phase-4 consumer cannot get per-source FT% without replaying the sequence; the self-describing tag is cheap at the `awardFreeThrows` choke point. **A new `PlayType.AND_ONE`** — rejected: an and-1 is a *kind of foul*; reuse `PlayType.FOUL` + an `AND_ONE` outcome (the #025 F/#026 E/#028 D vocabulary-reuse discipline). *(scope)* **Fold §3.12 (all-shot-type contact) and §3.13 (flagrants) into §3.11** — rejected (user call): each is a distinct fidelity gap with its own recalibration (§3.12) or its own sub-system (§3.13, shooter selection + possession retention + ejections); they are new numbered sub-phases pushing Phase 4 back by two, not §3.11 scope.  
**Status of §3.11 decisions**: A1/A2/A3/B/C/D/E all resolved by this entry (mirrors #021–#028). Net schema change: **NONE** — the and-1 `FOUL` (`AND_ONE` outcome, `committing_team_id = defTeamId`) reuses #028 D's column; the FT source type is an `outcome` string on the existing `PlayType.FREE_THROW` (#020). New engine pieces: a post-make and-1 roll in `PossessionEngine`'s `if (made)` block (gated `made && isContactType`), a `FoulResolver` and-1 probability method (reusing `rareEventProbability` with `AND_ONE_BASE` + `AND_ONE_SENSITIVITY`), a **per-situation count + source** threaded through `awardFreeThrows` (`AND_ONE_FREE_THROWS = 1`; `FREE_THROWS_PER_FOUL = 2` unchanged for shooting/bonus), the self-describing `FREE_THROW` source `outcome`s, and a `CalibrationHarness` and-1-rate + FT-source-split line. Determinism: the and-1 roll consumes the seeded RNG at a fixed point in the made-shot flow (after the assist roll, before the return) — existing seed-pinned made-shot assertions re-baseline (a controlled structural shift, as §3.7–§3.10 did). The execute-ready task sequence is todo.md's "§3.11 execution plan." **Open-at-execution (small, constrained):** the exact `AND_ONE` + FT-source `outcome` spellings (constrained: no collision with `SHOOTING_FOUL`/`REBOUNDING_FOUL_*`/`MADE`/`MISSED`, and made/missed still readable); the `AND_ONE_BASE` + `AND_ONE_SENSITIVITY` numbers (placeholders, settled by the harness line); whether the FT source is a suffix on the existing `outcome` or a small structured field; and the size/direction of the recalibration (E), re-agreed with the user against multiple harness runs.

**Implementation note (from execution, 2026-08).** Shipped as A1/A2/A3/B/C/D/E specify — **no design divergence**, and (unlike §3.10) no unplanned addition: #029 C's decision to reuse `rareEventProbability` with its own two dials *from the start* meant the `PROB_FLOOR` trap never had to be rediscovered a third time, which is exactly what that decision was for. Resolved the four open-at-execution items: **(1) outcome spellings** — the `FOUL` is `AND_ONE` (the leading candidate, unchanged); the FT source is a **suffix on the existing `outcome`**, `MADE_<SOURCE>` / `MISSED_<SOURCE>` (`MADE_SHOOTING`, `MISSED_BONUS`, `MADE_AND_ONE`, …). **(2) Suffix vs. structured field** — the **suffix**, decided by the constraint itself: leading with the `MADE`/`MISSED` prefix keeps every existing `startsWith("MADE")` read working (the box-score reconciliation, the harness period-FG% line), so the tag is additive to consumers rather than a breaking re-shape, and it needs no schema change (#020 free-text `outcome`). Carried on a `FreeThrowSource` enum whose constants own their string (the #027 `TurnoverCause` / #028 `ReboundFoul.Side` shape). **(3) The constants** — `AND_ONE_BASE = 0.055`, `AND_ONE_SENSITIVITY = 0.10` (matching `REBOUND_FOUL_SENSITIVITY`), `AND_ONE_FREE_THROWS = 1`. **(4) The recalibration (E)** — see below. `awardFreeThrows` gained its `count` + `source` parameters and all three call sites pass their pair, as designed; `EntityMapper` again needed no change (the §3.10 divergence-2 situation is unchanged — the FT source rides an `outcome` string that the API already surfaces).

**Recalibration (E) — the pure-additive prediction held exactly, and the lever choice was the whole call.** §3.11 landed **+2.4 pts/team** untuned at the `AND_ONE_BASE = 0.11` placeholder (114.0 → 116.4, mean of 3 seeds). Decomposed on the new harness line, **essentially all of it is the FT channel** (+2.35 pts of and-1 FTs ≈ the +2.4 total) — the exact opposite of §3.10, where the possession-retention channel dominated and the FT channel was the minority. #029 E predicted this correctly: an and-1 never forks the possession, so there is no retention channel to leak through. **The placeholder was also wrong on its own merits, independent of points**: 0.11 put and-1s at **11.4% of made contact FG** (vs. a realistic ~4–6%) and pushed team-periods-in-the-penalty from 37% to 48%. That gave §3.11 a lever §3.10 did not have — the and-1 base itself is both the scoring source *and* an uncalibrated realism number, so trimming it fixes two things at once. **Trimming `AND_ONE_BASE` 0.11 → 0.055** landed and-1s at **6.3% of made contact FG** and removed ~0.9 of the lift. **The user's call (option A, re-agreed against a 3-config × 5-seed sweep) was to stop there and take NO shot-`BASE_*` trim**, accepting points at 115.9: the measured exchange rate on that lever is **~0.6% FG% per 1.0 point removed** (much worse than §3.10's, precisely *because* §3.11's lift is free throws, which cost no FG%), so paying ~1% of a calibrated FG% to hide points that §3.12 will force a re-centering of anyway would burn the same number twice. `BASE_FOUL` was **not** touched (the #028 wrong-way-lever finding, respected). **Landing (harness, ~102 games × 5 seeds, tuned to the MEAN per #029 E's multiple-run discipline): 115.9 pts / 46.8% FG / 37.6% 3P / 27.5 ast / 13.9 TO / 4.9 blk / 2.8 OOB** — FG% and 3P% sit **on** their §3.4 targets (~47 / ~36), which is what the no-trim call bought. New §3.11 line: **and-1s 1.67/team/game (6.4% of made contact FG)**; **FT-source split SHOOTING 90.6% / AND_ONE 5.8% / BONUS 3.6%** (25.9 / 1.67 / 1.02 FTA per team/game), and-1 FTs worth **1.35 pts/team**. Fouls rose 3.95 → **4.41/team/period** and team-periods in the penalty 37.1% → **43.4%** (the and-1 foul counts toward the tally like any other, as designed). Seed 1000's and-1-disabled baseline reproduced §3.10's shipped landing **exactly** (113.8 / 46.9 / 37.6 / 27.7 / 14.2 / 5.0 / 2.8), which is what validated the harness before any number was read off it.

**One measured finding worth recording — a zero base does NOT switch a rare event off.** The §3.10 regression test asserts `rareEventProbability(0, …) == 0`, which is true only at an **even** contest: the formula is `base + sensitivity × (driving − opposing)/10`, so whenever the shooter's fatigue-scaled `foulDrawing` exceeds the defender's fatigue-scaled discipline, the **skill term alone** keeps the rate positive. Zeroing `AND_ONE_BASE` still produced ~0.44 and-1s/team/game — rare, but not off. (Found by running a baseline that was supposed to be a clean zero and wasn't.) This applies equally to §3.10's `REBOUND_FOUL_BASE` and to any future rare-event knob: **the base is a floor-setter, not an off-switch; only the caller's gate (here `isContactType`, #029 A2) truly disables the feature.** Guarded by a new test (`aZeroBaseStillLeavesASkillDrivenAndOneTail`) that pins the real behavior rather than the assumed one. A second test invariant was corrected during the pass for the same class of reason: an assertion bounding the skill swing as a *multiple* of the base spuriously failed once the base was tuned down (the additive skill term is base-independent, so a thinner base makes the same swing a larger multiple) — re-expressed as the absolute bound that actually matters.

**Divergences from the design:** none of substance. Two test re-baselines, as A2/D's trade-off predicted: the two points-reconciliation helpers (`PossessionEngineTest.pointsFromEvent`, `GameSimulatorIntegrationTest.pointsFromEntity`) read FT makes by **exact match** `"MADE".equals(outcome)` and became prefix reads — the one place the suffix choice was not transparent, and the reason D's "made/missed still readable" constraint was written. The `CalibrationHarness` FT tally **dropped its backward join entirely** (it used to attribute an FT to whichever `FOUL` most recently preceded it) and now reads the source off the event, which is the point of D — with an `UNKNOWN` bucket so a missing tag would be visible rather than silently mis-attributed (it never appeared). The harness also gained a `-DcalibrationSeed` override so E's multiple-run discipline is runnable. **Coverage:** `FoulResolver`, `FreeThrowSource`, `SimConfig`, `GameData` all **100%**; `PossessionEngine` **99.5%** (the same single pre-existing uncovered line as §3.10 — no new gap); `sim` package **99.1%**. Full `mvn clean install` gate green — **441 unit + 52 Cucumber**, all coverage checks met (JDK 21).

**§3.11 follow-up (carry forward)**:
- **Points sit at 115.9, ~3.9 above the ~112 §3.4 target** — a deliberate, user-agreed deferral, not drift (see the recalibration note). §3.12 adds *more* foul volume on top of this, so it should re-center points **once**, against the shot `BASE_*` lever, rather than §3.11 paying FG% now and §3.12 paying again. Treat 115.9 as the §3.12 baseline.
- **§3.12 (all-shot-type contact fouls + graduated foul rate + fouled-three = 3 FTs)** — the user's end-state where **every** shot type can draw a foul/and-1 at a graduated rate (post frequent → three rare), replacing the binary `isContactType` gate, and a shooting foul on a `THREE` awards **3** FTs (the latent bug: `awardFreeThrows` loops a flat count, and today no fouled three ever reaches it because `THREE` is not a contact type — the parameterized count from D is the seam that fixes it). Its own design pass + recalibration.
- **§3.13 (flagrant / technical fouls)** — a distinct foul class that changes **who shoots** (a technical is shot by a chosen shooter, not the fouled player), **possession retention** (a flagrant awards FTs *and* returns the ball to the offense — a retention path no current model has), and adds **ejections**. Its own sub-system + design pass.
- **B's "the and-1 never forks the possession" is SUPERSEDED for the FLAGRANT case only** (#034 C, §3.14b, 2026-08). The ordinary and-1 is completely unchanged — still a made basket plus exactly one free throw, still no fork. But when that same and-1 foul turns flagrant, the basket counts **and** two free throws are awarded **and** the offense keeps the ball: **three scoring channels on one possession**, which no other path in this engine produces. It is the rule, it looks exactly like a double-count bug in a play-by-play, and the flagrant's 2 FTs **replace** B's one rather than adding to it. Read B's claim as scoped to the ordinary and-1.
- **`committing_team_id` still not on the API** (carried from #028) — the and-1 `FOUL` populates it (`defTeamId`) but the OpenAPI `GameEvent` still omits it; additive whenever a play-by-play/Phase-4 consumer wants it.
- (The §3.7-E shot-clock-pressure, §3.9 finer turnover sub-types, §3.10 observed-split, and §3.6 time-column follow-ups still stand.)

### 030 — All-shot-type contact fouls (§3.12): the binary `isContactType` deleted for a per-shot-type foul-multiplier table anchored on an untouched (but renamed) `BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL`, one shared curve driving both the pre-shot and the and-1 roll, the latent fouled-three bug fixed through #029 D's parameterized count, and a SINGLE re-centering — spending the new uncalibrated multipliers before any calibrated FG% — that also absorbs §3.11's deferred 3.9-point debt
**Date**: 2026-08  
**Scope**: §3.12 is the sixth possession-fidelity completion sub-phase (§3.7–§3.13, before Phase 4) and the **second half of the split #029 A2 made**. Today the **entire foul model** gates on the binary `ShotType.isContactType()` (`DRIVE || POST`): a perimeter or three-point attempt cannot draw a shooting foul, and cannot draw an and-1, at any rate. §3.12 replaces that binary with a **graduated per-shot-type foul rate** so *every* shot type can be fouled — post/drive frequent, perimeter uncommon, three rare (a closeout on a three-point shooter is a real foul). The same root cause carries the **latent fouled-three bug**: the pre-shot foul branch passes a flat `FREE_THROWS_PER_FOUL = 2`, so a fouled `THREE` would award 2 FTs instead of 3 — it never fires today *only* because `THREE` isn't a contact type, so widening contact **activates** the bug and must fix it in the same pass. This is **engine work** in the `sim` package: no schema change, no OpenAPI change, no new `PlayType`, no new `FreeThrowSource` (the fouled three is a `SHOOTING` FT that happens to come in a set of three — Decision C). **§3.13 (flagrant/technical fouls) is NOT this pass.** §3.12 also **owns closing §3.11's deliberately deferred re-centering** (Decision E, the live risks.md failure mode): points sit at **115.9** vs. the ~112 §3.4 target because §3.11 took no shot-`BASE_*` trim, and §3.12 re-centers **once**, from that baseline, absorbing both its own lift and the inherited debt. NOT YET BUILT — this entry is the resolved design; the execute-ready plan is todo.md's §3.12 execution plan.

**Decision A1 — `ShotType.isContactType()` is DELETED, replaced by a per-shot-type foul-multiplier table in `SimConfig` (the crux, user call).** There is no surviving binary predicate. A new table — the `BASE_BLOCK_*` shape §3.7 established (#025 C), where a rare event carries a per-shot-type rate rather than a per-shot-type *gate* — supplies a multiplier for every one of the four types, and **all three of today's `isContactType` call sites read the table instead**: `FoulResolver.isFoul` multiplies its probability by the shot type's factor (rather than returning `false` early for non-contact types), `PossessionEngine`'s and-1 gate in the `if (made)` block **disappears entirely** (every made shot now rolls the and-1, whose own rate carries the graduation), and `ShotType` loses the method. Keeping the predicate as a "does this type foul at a non-trivial rate" convenience was **rejected (user call)**: once every type has a non-zero rate the predicate has no true meaning, and a leftover binary sitting beside a graduated table is precisely the second-source-of-truth smell #013/#015 exist to prevent — the harness and the tests would eventually disagree about which one is authoritative. **One caveat makes the deletion a deliberate act rather than an incidental one:** #029's measured finding that *a zero base does NOT switch a rare event off* (the skill term alone keeps the rate positive off an even contest) established that **the caller's gate was the real off-switch**. Removing that gate is therefore a real behavioral commitment, and it is the intended one — every shot type is *meant* to foul now. A type that should be near-foul-free is expressed as a **small multiplier**, not as a gate, and a multiplier of exactly `0.0` is the one honest off-switch left (it multiplies the whole probability, skill term included — unlike a zero *base*, which does not).

**Decision A2 — the multiplier is ANCHORED on an untouched `BASE_FOUL` (renamed `BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL` by F); it is NOT re-derived as a new league-wide base (user call).** The constant stays at **0.15** and keeps meaning exactly what §3.11 A1 protected it to mean: **"P(a contact foul stops the shot)" on a drive** — the §3.4-calibrated number. (F renames it for clarity; the value and meaning are untouched, and the two decisions are independent — A2 would hold under either name.) The table is a set of **relative** multipliers anchored at `DRIVE = 1.0`, with `POST` at or near 1.0 (post contact is at least as frequent as drive contact) and `PERIMETER` / `THREE` well below it (placeholders: `PERIMETER ≈ 0.35`, `THREE ≈ 0.133`, settled by the harness per E — the `THREE` value derived with the user in G). The consequence that makes this the right call: **drive and post foul rates come out numerically identical to today**, so §3.12's entire delta is isolated to the two types that previously could not foul at all — a decomposable, attributable change on the harness. The alternative — a new league-wide "P(contact stops an average shot)" base with *every* type including drive/post scaled off it — is conceptually cleaner (no privileged anchor type) but **reopens a calibrated number**: it forces a re-solve of `BASE_FOUL` such that drive/post lands back where it already is, *while simultaneously* adding two new foul sources, so a small error in the re-solve is indistinguishable from the new sources' lift. That is the same trap #029 A1 refused when it declined to re-decompose `BASE_FOUL` into P(contact) × P(shot survives contact). **Trade-off accepted (see below):** `DRIVE = 1.0` is a slightly arbitrary privileged anchor, and the table reads as relative rather than absolute rates.

**Decision B — the pre-shot foul roll and the and-1 roll graduate on the SAME shared curve — ONE table, both call sites (user call).** The two rolls keep their separate bases (`BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL` vs. `AND_ONE_BASE`) and their separate sensitivities, exactly as #029 C established — this decision changes neither. What is shared is the **per-shot-type multiplier**: one table, read by `FoulResolver.isFoul` and by `FoulResolver.isAndOne` alike, so a shot type's *relative* propensity to draw contact is one number with one owner. Two independent tables (one graduating the pre-shot rate, one graduating the and-1 rate) was rejected: it doubles the knobs for a distinction with no physical meaning — the contact frequency on a post-up versus a spot-up three is a property of *the shot*, not of which of the two rolls is asking. **The observation that an and-1 on a three is rarer than a foul on a three needs no extra modeling and must not be modeled:** it already falls out of the two rolls being independent and multiplicative — the and-1 requires the shot to *also* go in, so its realized frequency is roughly `P(make) × P(and-1 | make)`, and a three both makes less often and fouls less often. Adding a second, steeper table for and-1s would double-count that effect. `isAndOne` gains a `ShotType` parameter (it currently takes none, because its caller did the gating); that is the mechanical shape of this decision.

**One shared table does NOT mean one shot can be fouled twice — the two rolls are MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE by control flow, and this is the thing most likely to be misread.** The pre-shot foul branch **returns** (`PossessionEngine:143`), so a shot that draws a stopped-shot foul never reaches the make/miss roll and therefore never reaches the and-1 roll. The and-1 is reachable only on the *surviving* branch, and only on a make — it is conditioned **twice**. Worked at §3.11's shipped numbers on a drive: P(stopped) = 0.15; P(and-1) = 0.85 × ~0.55 × 0.055 ≈ **0.026**; combined ≈ **17.6%**, not the 20.5% naive addition suggests, and nowhere near double. **This double conditioning is precisely the mechanism B relies on** when it says the and-1-on-a-three rarity falls out for free: a three both survives-to-the-make less often *and* carries a smaller multiplier, so the compounding is automatic — which is why a second, steeper and-1 table would double-count it. **Where the two rolls genuinely DO compound is the period foul tally** (both emit a `FOUL`, both count toward `BONUS_FOULS_PER_PERIOD`), measured in §3.11: adding and-1s alone moved fouls 3.95 → 4.41/team/period and the penalty rate 37.1% → 43.4%. §3.12 widens **that** channel, but by much less than the change in *reach* suggests, and the distinction matters for where the builder should expect the lift.

**Reach roughly doubles; the and-1 RATE rises only ~20%.** Deleting the gate takes the and-1 roll from ~51% of attempts (drive/post) to 100%, but a perimeter/three and-1 is far rarer than a drive one — **and the shared multiplier appears TWICE in the and-1 path**, scaling both the stopped-shot roll the shot must survive and the and-1 roll itself. Per attempt, at the placeholder multipliers: **DRIVE ~2.79%, POST ~2.33%, PERIMETER ~0.80%, THREE ~0.36%** — a perimeter and-1 is ~3.5× rarer than a drive's and a three's ~8× rarer, because the smaller multiplier, the lower make rate, and the double application compound. Aggregated over an estimated shot mix that lands **~1.35% → ~1.62% of possessions, a ~1.2× rise — not the ~2× reach implies, and nowhere near 4×.** **The consequence for E: §3.12's and-1 channel is close to a non-event, and the lift will be dominated by the STOPPED-SHOT channel** (where a fouled three pays ~2.25 points against the ~1.01 3PA it removes). A builder who budgets the recalibration against and-1s will be looking in the wrong place. *(These per-type figures are an analytic estimate over an assumed shot mix — `PlayerGameState.shotTypeWeight` weights `DRIVE` as `drive + finishing` while the other three are single skills, so drive is structurally over-weighted — and they ignore the coach shot-mix lean. Directionally reliable, not measured: **the harness settles them, per E.**)* Two rates widening their domain at once, both feeding one tally, is still what D's penalty-rate line is watching for.

**Decision C — the free-throw count is decided by a method on `ShotType` (`freeThrowsIfFouled()`), and ONLY the stopped-shot count graduates.** The count is a property of the shot type, so it lives on the type: `ShotType.freeThrowsIfFouled()` returns **3** for `THREE` and **2** for `DRIVE`/`POST`/`PERIMETER`, and the pre-shot foul branch passes it into `awardFreeThrows` where it passes the constant `FREE_THROWS_PER_FOUL` today. **This is a call-site change, not new machinery** — #029 D already parameterized `awardFreeThrows` with a per-situation `count` precisely so §3.12 could pass `3`; that seam is the fix. Chosen over a `switch` on `ShotType` at the call site (which puts a rule about shot types somewhere other than `ShotType`, and would need duplicating if a second consumer ever asks) and over a `SimConfig` lookup (rejected on a sharper principle: **`SimConfig` holds tunable numbers, and "a fouled three shoots three free throws" is not tunable — it is a rule of basketball**, and putting it in the tuning surface invites someone to tune it). `FREE_THROWS_PER_FOUL = 2` survives as the non-shooting/bonus count (§3.10's bonus FTs are still 2 and are not shot-type-scoped). **The rule this encodes, in full:**

| Situation | FTs | Where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| Foul stops a `DRIVE`/`POST`/`PERIMETER` | 2 | `ShotType.freeThrowsIfFouled()` |
| Foul stops a `THREE` | **3** | `ShotType.freeThrowsIfFouled()` ← the latent bug this pass activates and fixes |
| Foul on a **made** shot (and-1), **any** type incl. a made three | **1** | `AND_ONE_FREE_THROWS`, unchanged |
| §3.10 bonus (non-shooting) foul | 2 | `FREE_THROWS_PER_FOUL`, unchanged |

**`AND_ONE_FREE_THROWS` stays 1 for every shot type and must NOT be made to graduate in parallel** — a made three plus a foul is 3 points and **one** free throw, not three. This is the easy wrong turn in the pass (the `count` parameter makes it a one-character mistake) and it would silently inflate scoring on top of §3.12's real lift; it is guarded by an explicit test, not just by this sentence. **No new `FreeThrowSource`:** a fouled three's FTs are `SHOOTING` — the source describes *why* the FT was awarded, and three-FT-ness is carried by there being three of them, not by a new tag. (A Phase-4 consumer wanting "3-FT trips" counts `SHOOTING` FTs per preceding `FOUL`, or reads the `SHOT`-type context; nothing today asks, so nothing is fabricated ahead of a consumer — #014/#017/#020.)

**Decision D — `BONUS_FOULS_PER_PERIOD` stays at 5; the penalty rate is an OBSERVATION to report, not a knob to turn (user call).** 5 team fouls per period is **the real NBA rule**, and the standing principle the user set is that a real rule is always honored — it is not a calibration lever and §3.12 does not touch it. §3.12 will push fouls per team-period up from §3.11's 4.41 and team-periods-in-the-penalty up from 43.4%, possibly substantially (perimeter + three are the *majority* of the shot mix, so widening contact to them is not a marginal addition). **The harness already reports both lines** (§3.10 added them, #028 E) — confirmed against `CalibrationHarness:388–392`, so **no new instrument is needed for this question**; the existing fouls/team/period + penalty-% lines answer it. **One existing line does need repair, though, and it is the §3.11 line rather than a §3.10 one:** the and-1 rate is printed as a share of "made contact FG", whose denominator is hardcoded `endsWith("DRIVE") || endsWith("POST")` (`CalibrationHarness:223–227`). A1 makes the **numerator** count and-1s on all four shot types while that denominator still counts two, so the percentage silently inflates and §3.11's 6.4% stops being a comparable figure. **The denominator must be widened to all made FG before any §3.12 number is read off the harness** — an instrument that is wrong in the direction of the change being measured is worse than no instrument (the #026 D discipline applied to a line that already exists rather than one being added). Cross-phase continuity should be judged on **and-1s per team per game** (1.67), which is denominator-independent. If the penalty rate lands implausibly high, the response is to **trim the `PERIMETER`/`THREE` multipliers** (A2's table — the uncalibrated new numbers) or, if fouls are high league-wide, to note it for a future pass — **never** to move the rule constant, and **never** to reach for `BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL` (E). Plausibility is judged against the harness output with the user, not asserted here.

**Decision E — ONE re-centering, from the 115.9 baseline, spending the levers CHEAPEST-FIRST (the new multipliers before shot `BASE_*`, never `BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL`), steered by multiple seeds to the mean — and the lift is DECOMPOSED by channel before any trim is chosen.** §3.12 owns closing §3.11's deferred debt (risks.md's live failure mode is "§3.12 forgets to absorb it"): the target is to land points back at **~112**, re-centering *once* from **115.9** so no calibrated number is paid twice. **The lever ORDER is itself a decision, and §3.12 has one §3.10/§3.11 did not:**
1. **The new `PERIMETER`/`THREE` foul multipliers (A2) — spend these FIRST.** They are **uncalibrated numbers this pass invents**, so trimming them costs **nothing calibrated**: it only asserts that jump-shooters draw fouls somewhat less often, and no measured target contradicts that. §3.10 and §3.11 went straight to shot `BASE_*` because neither had a free lever; §3.12 does, and using it first is what can make this re-centering nearly costless.
2. **The shot `BASE_*` rates — only for the remainder.** The proven lever (§3.10/§3.11, §3.7's nudge-up in reverse), but it **spends a calibrated number**: ~0.6% FG% per 1.0 point at #029's measurement, so naively closing ~4 points would drag FG% from 46.8% toward ~44.4%, well off the ~47 §3.4 target. This is exactly the arithmetic that made §3.11 decline the trim.
3. **`BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL` — never.** It is a *wrong-way* lever, and the mechanism is worth stating plainly because the sign is counterintuitive: **it does not control scoring, it controls a swap.** When it fires, a live shot is replaced by a 2-FT trip worth ~1.5 expected points; when it doesn't, the possession retains a live shot worth *more* than 1.5 once its offensive-rebound, and-1, and rebounding-foul continuations are counted. So trimming it converts FT trips back into the more valuable live shots and points go **up** — measured in #028: 0.15 → 0.138 moved points *up* to 116.1. This is doubly load-bearing in §3.12, because the wrong-way effect now applies to jump shots too.

Two further constraints, both inherited findings rather than fresh calls: **steering is by multiple `-DcalibrationSeed` runs tuned to the MEAN** (#029 E), never a single run — per-seed noise is ±1.5 points, enough to bait an over-correction; and **the FG%-per-point exchange rate is re-measured on §3.12's own numbers** before any FG% is traded, since #029's ~0.6% was measured on a pure-FT lift and §3.12's is mixed. **The decomposition is mandatory and comes BEFORE the trim**, because the headline number has hidden the mechanism in both prior foul passes (§3.10's lift was mostly retained possessions, not FTs; §3.11's was almost entirely FTs). §3.12's channels:
- **Stopped-shot channel (mixed, and this is the subtle one).** A foul that *stops* a shot removes a live attempt and replaces it with FTs. On a `PERIMETER`: removes ~0.44 × 2 ≈ 0.88 expected points, awards 2 FTs at ~75% ≈ 1.5 → **net up ~0.6**. On a `THREE`: removes ~0.34 × 3 ≈ 1.01, awards **3** FTs at ~75% ≈ 2.25 → **net up ~1.2, the largest per-event gain in the whole model**. **todo.md's framing of this channel was wrong and is corrected here:** it warned the widening "may move points LESS than expected, or even down in places" on the theory that a stopped jump shot is roughly a wash. It is not a wash — at the engine's own FG%/3P%/FT% numbers **both** new stopped-shot cases are net *positive*, and the fouled three is the biggest single-event gain §3.12 introduces. Expect the lift to be **larger** than a naive FT-volume count suggests, not smaller. (The correct residue of todo.md's warning is that this channel does not scale like §3.11's pure-additive one — it is additive *net of a removal*, so its per-foul yield is ~0.6–1.2 points rather than a full FT trip.)
- **And-1 channel (up, purely additive).** Perimeter and three makes can now draw an and-1 — a made FG plus 1 FT with no offsetting removal, the §3.11 shape (#029 E) extended to the two types that were excluded.
- **Possession-ending channel (down, secondary).** A stopped perimeter/three attempt also removes that attempt from the shot mix, which slightly reduces offensive-rebound opportunities. Small, but it is the reason FG% and 3P% can drift **even though no individual shot probability changed** — fouls now remove perimeter/three attempts from the denominator, not just drive/post ones. Note the mechanic precisely: the pre-shot foul branch returns **before** `recordFieldGoalAttempt()`, so a stopped shot charges **no FGA** and removes points without moving FG% directly — but it changes the *mix* FG% is computed over. **Watch 3P% and FG% on the harness rather than assuming they hold**, and **re-measure the FG%-per-point exchange rate on §3.12's own numbers** before trading any FG% away — §3.11's ~0.6% figure was measured on a pure-FT lift and does not carry over unexamined.

**Decision F — `BASE_FOUL` is RENAMED to `BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL` (value unchanged, user call).** The constant keeps its value (**0.15**) and its meaning; only the label changes. **The name was actively misleading and demonstrably caused harm:** `BASE_FOUL` reads as "the foul rate," which is what invites tuning it to move fouls or points — and it is not that. It is P(a foul **stopped the shot**), the possession-ending branch, which is exactly why trimming it moves points the *wrong* way (E) and why that had to be recorded as a finding in #028 and re-warned in #029. A name that produces a recurring, documented misuse is a defect in the name. **§3.12 is the right moment, and not merely for tidiness:** until now the ambiguity was contained by the *gate* — `BASE_FOUL` obviously meant drive/post, because nothing else could foul. A1 deletes that gate, so from this pass forward there are **two** foul rates that both apply to **all four shot types** (the stopped-shot base, and `AND_ONE_BASE` for the foul the shot survives), and a pair where one name describes its outcome and the other does not is a genuine trap. `BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL` is basketball's own vocabulary ("foul, no basket") and pairs cleanly against `AND_ONE_BASE` — no basket vs. basket-plus-one. **Explicitly rejected: `BASE_SHOOTING_FOUL`** — tempting because it matches the existing `SHOOTING_FOUL` `outcome` string, but an and-1 *is* a shooting foul in basketball, so that name would re-create the very ambiguity this decision removes. **The close-out must state unambiguously that the VALUE never moved** — this renames a §3.4-calibrated number, and a rename that reads as a re-tune would undo A2's whole attributability argument. The `SHOOTING_FOUL` `outcome` string is **not** renamed (it is persisted event vocabulary; §3.12 changes no outcome strings). **To be unambiguous about the end state: after §3.12 executes, the identifier `BASE_FOUL` no longer exists anywhere in the codebase** — this is a rename, not an alias or a deprecation, so there is no transition period and no second constant to keep in sync. Every reference to it in this entry and in the older #028/#029 entries is *historical*, describing the constant under its pre-§3.12 name. Confirmed scope at design time: 5 references across `SimConfig.java` (×4) and `FoulResolver.java` (×1), **zero in tests**.

**Decision G — a FOUL-OUT harness line is added (a genuinely new instrument), and three plausibility benchmarks are recorded so "realistic" is checkable rather than intuited (user question).** D confirmed the fouls/period and penalty-% lines already exist, but a direct question — *how many players foul out, and is that realistic?* — exposed that **nothing has ever measured it**. The mechanism is shipped and live (`SimConfig.FOUL_OUT_LIMIT = 6`, `PlayerGameState.hasFouledOut()`, §3.5 forces the player off the floor and substitutes), but its **rate has never been observed in any calibration run** — so the honest answer today is "unknown", and §3.12 is precisely the pass that moves it, since widening contact to all shot types raises fouls across all five defenders. Per #026 D ("build the instrument before you claim a number"), §3.12 **adds** a foul-out line: foul-outs per team per game, plus the **distribution of end-of-game per-player fouls** (how many finish at 4 / 5 / 6) — the distribution matters more than the count, because it shows pressure building *below* the threshold before it becomes visible above it. **Three ballpark benchmarks are recorded to judge plausibility against** — real-NBA figures, deliberately framed as *plausibility ranges, not new calibration targets* (the §3.4 five remain the targets, and #017's "don't fabricate a constraint ahead of a consumer" applies to targets too): **fouls ~19–20/team/game** (measured at **16.8** on seed 1000 — meaningfully low, so §3.12 has real room to rise, but not past ~20); **foul-outs ~0.11/team/game**, about one every nine games (if §3.12 lands above ~0.3, the multipliers are too hot); and **fouled-three trips ~0.7/team/game** (today structurally 0 — impossible). **That last benchmark is the one the `THREE` multiplier is set from, and it was worked through with the user (2026-08) rather than left to feel.** ~0.7 trips against ~35 3PA/team/game implies **~2% of 3PA** stopped-and-fouled, or **~3%** counting and-1 threes as well — and **2% is the agreed rate**, encoded as `FOUL_MULT_THREE ≈ 0.133` (`0.15 × 0.133 = 0.02`). **The choice between those two measures is the substantive call, and it went to the stricter one deliberately:** this multiplier's most consequential output is the **3-FT trip** — the highest-yield event in the model (~2.25 points against the ~1.01 3PA it replaces) — so anchoring on the *stopped-three* rate (~2%) is more faithful than anchoring on all-fouls-on-threes (~3%), which would inflate the trip count by half. The and-1 threes the multiplier also governs ride on top of that. A **5%** rate (multiplier ~0.33) was considered and set aside — well above the real rate, and it would add roughly **+0.6 pts/team/game** on a pass already 3.9 over target, spending §3.12's cheapest lever on realism that must then be repurchased with calibrated FG%. Expected landing at 0.133: **~0.40 3-FT trips/team/game** — and the gap to the real-league ~0.7 is **expected, not an error**, because this engine shoots a **measured 20.3 3PA/team/game against the NBA's ~35** (seed 1000, 2026-08). At an identical *rate*, lower 3PA volume necessarily yields fewer trips. **Anchor the multiplier on the RATE (2% of 3PA), never on the trip count**, or a builder will "correct" 0.40 up to 0.7 and silently undo this decision. (The sub-NBA 3PA volume is a separate pre-existing observation, not §3.12's to fix.)

**A units hazard this exchange exposed, worth carrying into execution:** a `FOUL_MULT_*` value **is not a probability**, and it reads like one. `FOUL_MULT_THREE = 0.133` means "a three fouls at **13.3% of the drive rate**" (⇒ 2%), not "13.3% of threes are fouled" — and these constants sit in `SimConfig` directly beside `BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL = 0.15`, where every value looks like a probability. The misreading happened once during this design pass; the javadoc and constant naming must make the units explicit so it does not happen again at the tuning bench, where it would send someone to the wrong knob. **Note also the two distinct measures** the benchmarks use: ~2% of 3PA = *missed and fouled* (the 3-FT trips); ~3% = **any** foul on a 3PA, and-1s included. **`PERIMETER = 0.35` (⇒ ~5.25%) is the least-grounded of the four** — `DRIVE`/`POST` are pinned at 1.0 to preserve §3.11 behavior and `THREE` is benchmark-anchored, but perimeter was chosen only to sit between them; ~0.30 (⇒ ~4.5%) is an equally defensible alternative. Its stakes are lower (a fouled mid-range awards 2 FTs ≈ 1.5 pts against the ~0.88 it removes — about half a fouled three's yield), so it is left to the per-shot-type harness line to settle. **The fouled-three benchmark is the most operationally useful of the three**, and G directs that the `THREE` multiplier be tuned against it *directly* rather than against the points aggregate alone: a fouled three is the **highest-yield event in the model** (~2.25 expected points vs. the ~1.01 3PA it replaces), so an inflated `THREE` multiplier is a **realism** error that re-centering cannot repair — E's levers can always drag points back to ~112, but they would do it by making *everything else* wrong to compensate for one bad number.

**Rationale**: *(A1)* A graduated rate makes the *rate* carry the information the gate used to carry, so there is one mechanism instead of a gate plus a rate; deleting the predicate rather than retiring it in place avoids the #013/#015 duplicate-source trap, and the table is the shape §3.7 already proved for a per-shot-type rare event (`BASE_BLOCK_*`). Making the deletion explicit about #029's zero-base finding is what keeps it a decision rather than an accident — the gate *was* the off-switch, and we are knowingly removing it. *(A2)* Anchoring on an untouched `BASE_FOUL` keeps §3.4's calibration legible and, more importantly, makes §3.12's delta **attributable**: drive/post behavior is bit-identical to §3.11, so anything the harness moves came from perimeter/three. Re-deriving a league-wide base would entangle a calibrated-number re-solve with two new scoring sources in the same measurement — the #029 A1 trap. *(B)* One shared table matches the physics (contact propensity is a property of the shot type) and halves the knobs; the and-1-on-a-three rarity is already produced by the two independent rolls compounding, so a second table would double-count it. *(C)* Putting the count on `ShotType` puts a fact about shot types where shot types live, and keeps a **rule** out of the **tuning** surface — `SimConfig` is for numbers someone is invited to change, and this is not one. The count parameter already exists (#029 D), which is exactly why this phase is a call-site change and not a plumbing change. *(D)* A real NBA rule is honored, not tuned (the user's standing principle); the penalty rate is a *result* to look at, and the instrument already exists, so the honest move is to confirm the instrument (#026 D) rather than build a new one or assert a number now. *(E)* Both prior foul passes proved the headline number hides the mechanism and each found a *different* dominant channel, so decomposing first is the only way to know which knob is the right one; the wrong-way `BASE_FOUL` finding and the multiple-seed discipline are inherited measurements, not fresh opinions, and re-centering once from 115.9 is what stops a calibrated number being paid twice for two adjacent foul phases. The cheapest-first lever order is the genuinely new element: §3.10 and §3.11 reached for shot `BASE_*` because it was the only lever they had, whereas §3.12 introduces two *uncalibrated* numbers (the perimeter/three multipliers) that can absorb lift at **zero** cost to a calibrated target — spending a free lever before a costly one is simply the right order, and it may make this re-centering nearly free where §3.11's would not have been. *(F)* A constant whose name has produced a documented, repeated misuse (a #028 finding plus a #029 re-warning, both about reaching for it as a points lever) has a defective name, not merely an imperfect one; renaming it in the pass that removes the gate which had been silently disambiguating it is the moment the confusion would otherwise become permanent, since A1 leaves two all-shot-type foul rates side by side. *(G)* A capability that has shipped but has never been measured is indistinguishable from one that is broken, and §3.12 is the pass that puts real pressure on it — adding the instrument now is the same #026 D discipline the arc has followed since §3.8, applied to the one foul-model output nobody had thought to look at. Recording benchmarks as *ranges* rather than targets keeps them a plausibility check without fabricating new calibration constraints (#017).  
**Trade-off**: *(A1)* Every shot type now consumes an RNG draw in the foul branch and (on a make) the and-1 branch, where perimeter/three previously short-circuited — **every seed-pinned assertion in the shot flow re-baselines**, a larger structural RNG shift than §3.7–§3.11's, because it changes draw counts on the *majority* of possessions rather than a minority. *(A2)* `DRIVE = 1.0` is a privileged, faintly arbitrary anchor, and the table reads as relative multipliers rather than absolute per-type rates — a reader must hold "× `BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL`" in their head. Accepted for the attributability it buys. *(B)* `isAndOne` gains a `ShotType` parameter and the and-1's per-type rate is not independently tunable from the pre-shot foul's — deliberate, and reversible into two tables later if a real need appears. *(C)* `ShotType` acquires a rules method alongside its `points` data — a mild widening of a previously-inert enum. *(D)* §3.12 may well land the penalty rate somewhere uncomfortable and the only sanctioned responses are trimming the new multipliers or accepting it; the rule constant is off the table by construction. *(E)* The largest recalibration of the arc — two new scoring channels plus an inherited 3.9-point debt, all absorbed in one pass — and the FG%/3P% drift from shot-mix change must be watched rather than assumed, so this is a multi-run, decompose-then-trim exercise, not a single knob turn. *(F)* Rename churn (`SimConfig`, `FoulResolver`, javadoc, tests) lands in the same commit as a behavior change, making the §3.12 diff noisier to read; and renaming a §3.4-calibrated constant carries a real risk of being *misread* as a re-tune, which is why the close-out must state the value never moved. *(G)* One more harness line to write and read; and the three benchmarks, being ballparks rather than measured targets, could be mistaken for hard requirements by a later pass — hence the explicit framing that the §3.4 five remain the only targets. There is also a real chance the foul-out line reveals a **pre-existing** §3.5 problem that predates §3.12, which would have to be triaged on its own rather than treated as §3.12 lift.  
**Alternatives considered**: *(A1)* **Keep `isContactType()` as a convenience predicate** ("does this type foul at a non-trivial rate") — rejected (user call): two overlapping definitions of "can this be fouled", authoritative in neither place, the #013/#015 trap. **Keep the gate and give only DRIVE/POST a graduated rate** — rejected: that is §3.11's model with extra ceremony; it does not deliver the user's end-state (a closeout on a three-point shooter is a real foul). **Express a foul-free type as a zero *base*** — rejected on the #029 measured finding: a zero base leaves a skill-driven tail; only a zero *multiplier* (which scales the whole probability) truly switches a type off. *(A2)* **Re-derive a new league-wide `BASE_FOUL` with all four types scaled off it** — rejected (user call): cleaner in the abstract, but it reopens a §3.4-calibrated number in the same pass that adds two scoring sources, so a re-solve error and the new lift are indistinguishable on the harness — the #029 A1 trap. **Anchor on `POST` instead of `DRIVE`** — considered and left to execution as a spelling-level choice; either preserves today's drive/post behavior provided the pair keeps its current relative rate (both are 1.0 today, since both simply passed the same gate). *(B)* **Two independent tables, one per roll** — rejected: doubles the knobs for a distinction with no physical meaning, and invites double-counting the and-1-on-a-three rarity that the two independent rolls already produce. **Model the and-1-on-a-three rarity explicitly with a steeper and-1 curve** — rejected: it *is* already modeled, multiplicatively; adding it again would compound it twice. *(C)* **A `switch` on `ShotType` at the call site** — rejected: puts a rule about shot types outside `ShotType`, and duplicates on the second consumer. **A `SimConfig` FT-count lookup** — rejected: `SimConfig` is the tunable surface and "a fouled three shoots 3" is a rule, not a tunable; housing it there invites tuning a rule. **Make `AND_ONE_FREE_THROWS` graduate by shot type in parallel** — rejected outright: a made three plus a foul is 1 FT by rule; the parallel-graduation mistake is a one-character error the `count` parameter makes easy, so it is guarded by a test. **A new `FreeThrowSource` for the 3-FT trip** — rejected: the source records *why* the FT was awarded (it is a `SHOOTING` foul); three-FT-ness is carried by the count, and no consumer asks for the distinction (#014/#017/#020). *(D)* **Raise `BONUS_FOULS_PER_PERIOD` to hold the penalty rate down** — rejected (user call): 5 is the real NBA rule and real rules are honored, never used as calibration levers. **Add a new harness line for the penalty rate** — rejected as redundant: §3.10's fouls/period + penalty-% lines already report it (#028 E) — confirmed, not assumed. *(E)* **Re-center only §3.12's own lift and leave §3.11's 3.9 points** — rejected: that is exactly risks.md's named failure mode, and it would leave the ~4-point gap permanently ownerless. **Trim `BASE_FOUL` to remove points** — rejected on measurement: it is a wrong-way lever (#028), and §3.12 makes it wrong-way on jump shots too. **Go straight to the shot `BASE_*` lever as §3.10/§3.11 did** — rejected: those passes had no alternative, but §3.12's own new perimeter/three multipliers are uncalibrated and can absorb lift for free, so spending a calibrated FG% before an uncalibrated multiplier would be paying for something available at no cost. **Trust §3.11's ~0.6% FG%-per-point exchange rate** — rejected: it was measured on a pure-FT lift; §3.12's is mixed, so it is re-measured before any FG% is traded. **Tune from a single harness run** — rejected: ±1.5 points of per-seed noise (#029 E). *(G)* **Assume foul-outs are fine because §3.5 shipped and nothing looked wrong** — rejected: nothing has ever *looked*, since no line reports it; that is an assumption, not an observation (#026 D). **Make the three benchmarks hard calibration targets alongside the §3.4 five** — rejected: they are unsourced ballparks, and promoting them would fabricate constraints ahead of any consumer (#017) and over-constrain a five-target calibration that is already hard to satisfy. **Defer the foul-out line to Phase 4 stats** — rejected: §3.12 is the pass that moves the number, so measuring it afterward would mean tuning §3.12 blind and discovering the drift a phase late. *(F)* **`BASE_SHOOTING_FOUL`** — rejected: matches the `SHOOTING_FOUL` outcome string, but an and-1 is *also* a shooting foul, so it re-creates the ambiguity being removed. **`BASE_FOUL_STOPS_SHOT` / `BASE_CONTACT_STOPS_SHOT`** — accurate and unambiguous, but sentence-shaped where `SimConfig`'s other rates are `BASE_<noun>` (`BASE_TURNOVER`, `BASE_ASSIST`, `BASE_BLOCK_*`). **`BASE_STOPPED_SHOT_FOUL`** — the closest rival; lost to the basketball-native "no basket" phrasing, which pairs better against `AND_ONE_BASE`. **`BASE_BLOCKING_FOUL`** — rejected outright: collides with §3.7 blocks. **Keep `BASE_FOUL` and document it harder** — rejected (user call): javadoc has not stopped the misuse across two passes. **Defer the rename to its own post-§3.13 cleanup pass** — rejected (user call): it would persist the confusing name through precisely the pass where A1 makes it most confusing.  
**Status of §3.12 decisions**: A1/A2/B/C/D/E/F/G all resolved by this entry (mirrors #021–#029). Net schema change: **NONE** — no new column, no OpenAPI change, no new `PlayType`, no new `FreeThrowSource`, no new `outcome` string (a fouled three emits the existing `SHOOTING_FOUL` `FOUL` and three existing `MADE_SHOOTING`/`MISSED_SHOOTING` `FREE_THROW` events). New engine pieces: a per-shot-type foul-multiplier table in `SimConfig` (`FOUL_MULT_DRIVE`/`_POST`/`_PERIMETER`/`_THREE`, or an equivalent lookup — spelling at execution), `ShotType.isContactType()` **deleted** and `ShotType.freeThrowsIfFouled()` added, `BASE_FOUL` **renamed** `BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL` at its unchanged 0.15 (F), `FoulResolver.isFoul` multiplying by the table instead of early-returning, `FoulResolver.isAndOne` gaining a `ShotType` parameter and multiplying by the same table, `PossessionEngine`'s and-1 gate removed and its pre-shot foul call site passing `shotType.freeThrowsIfFouled()`, two `CalibrationHarness` changes (the and-1 **denominator repair** — `madeContactShots` widened from DRIVE/POST to all made FG, D — and a **new foul-out line** with the end-of-game per-player foul distribution, G), and the §3.12 recalibration. Determinism: **the RNG shift is the largest of the arc** — perimeter/three possessions now consume a foul draw (and, on a make, an and-1 draw) where they previously short-circuited, so seed-pinned assertions across the *shot* flow re-baseline, not just the foul flow (a controlled structural shift, as §3.7–§3.11 each took, but broader). The execute-ready task sequence is todo.md's "§3.12 execution plan." **Open-at-execution (small, constrained):** the multiplier constants' exact names/shape (four constants vs. a switch on `ShotType` vs. a field on the enum — constrained to keep `SimConfig` the tuning surface, C); the `POST` multiplier's exact value relative to the `DRIVE = 1.0` anchor (must preserve today's drive/post behavior, A2); the `PERIMETER`/`THREE` placeholder values (settled by the harness, E); and how much of the re-centering each lever carries plus the final agreed landing (E), re-agreed with the user against multiple seeds. The `BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL` rename (F) is **not** open — the name is decided; only whether it lands as a separate mechanical commit ahead of the behavior change is the builder's call. G's benchmarks are **not** open either — they are plausibility ranges to judge against, not numbers to negotiate; what *is* open is what to do if the foul-out line reveals a pre-existing §3.5 problem (triage it separately, do not absorb it into §3.12's recalibration).

**Implementation note (from execution, 2026-08).** Shipped as A1/A2/B/C/D/E/F/G specify — **no design divergence**, plus **two unplanned fixes forced by execution** (both below). Resolved the open-at-execution items: **(1) the table's shape** — four `SimConfig` constants (`FOUL_MULT_DRIVE`/`_POST`/`_PERIMETER`/`_THREE`) plus a `foulMultiplier(ShotType)` switch, the leading candidate unchanged; the numbers stayed in `SimConfig` (the tuning surface) and the *rule* went on the enum (`ShotType.freeThrowsIfFouled()`), exactly as C splits them. **(2) `POST` = 1.0**, as A2 required — the harness never argued otherwise. **(3) `PERIMETER` = 0.30**, not the 0.35 placeholder: set on **realism, not points** per the user's explicit instruction, since the whole 0.25–0.40 band moves points by only ~0.3 (measured) and is therefore not a points lever at all. 0.30 puts a mid-range foul at **2.25× a three's rate** — inside the defensible 2–3× window — and lands perimeter and-1s at 0.16/team/game against a drive's 1.21. `THREE` = **0.133** unchanged (the user-agreed 2%-of-3PA rate), landing **0.59 3-FT trips/team/game at 2.9% of 3PA** — inside G's expected ~0.3–0.6 band, so the multiplier was not moved. **(4) The recalibration (E)** — see below; **no shot-`BASE_*` trim was taken**.

**The two unplanned fixes.** *(1) `PROB_FLOOR` again, in a new form — the #028 trap's §3.12 shape.* `contestProbability` ends in `clampProbability`, which floors at `PROB_FLOOR = 0.02`; multiplying its result and re-clamping would have floored a thin type back up. `FOUL_MULT_THREE` targets **exactly 2%** — sitting *on* the floor — so `isFoul` would have silently ignored any downward tuning of the `THREE` multiplier **and** floored a `0.0` multiplier up to 2%, destroying the off-switch A1 explicitly relies on. `isFoul` therefore multiplies the contested probability and clamps with `Math.min(PROB_CEILING, …)` only — floor-free, the same shape `rareEventProbability` took in #028. The un-multiplied DRIVE/POST path is unaffected (at mult 1.0 the value is far above the floor), so A2's bit-identical guarantee holds. *(2) A latent points-reconciliation bug in a TEST helper, exposed (not caused) by §3.12's RNG shift.* `PossessionEngineTest`'s reconciliation buckets event points by `offTeamId`, which is wrong for exactly one event: a §3.10 **BONUS** free throw awarded when the **offense** committed the rebounding foul — there the *defense* shoots and scores while the event still carries the possession's orientation. The engine has always scored these correctly (`awardFreeThrows` takes an explicit `shootingTeamId`); the *helper* could not tell, and no seed had ever reached the offense-commits-in-penalty case until §3.12's draw shift moved it. Fixed by threading the preceding `FOUL`'s `committingTeamId` (#028 D) into the attribution. **Latent since §3.10** — worth noting as evidence that the RNG-shift re-baselining each foul pass performs is doing real work, not just churn.

**Recalibration (E) — the lift was HALF what the design sized, and the re-centering was NOT taken.** §3.12 landed **+1.1 pts/team** untuned (115.9 → **117.0**, mean of 5 seeds), against a design expectation nearer +2.5. Decomposed on the new per-shot-type lines, **all three channels behaved as #030 E predicted directionally**, but the possession-ending drag was larger than expected: **stopped-shot +1.6** (fouled threes 0.59 × ~+1.2 ≈ **+0.71**; extra stopped twos +1.49 × ~+0.6 ≈ **+0.89**), **and-1 +0.15** (1.67 → 1.87 — B's "close to a non-event" was exactly right, and a builder budgeting the recalibration against this channel would have been looking in the wrong place), **possession-ending ≈ −0.6** (stopped attempts remove offensive-rebound opportunities). **The cheapest-first lever order (E) turned out to have almost nothing to spend:** the new `PERIMETER`/`THREE` multipliers are worth only **~0.3 points across their entire defensible range** — measured, by sweeping 0.25/0.30/0.35 — so §3.12's supposedly free lever is nearly weightless, and the "this re-centering may be nearly costless" hope in E did not survive contact. **The shot-`BASE_*` exchange rate was re-measured on §3.12's own numbers as E requires: ~0.50% FG% per 1.0 point** (better than #029's 0.6% because §3.12's lift is mixed rather than pure-FT, but still expensive). **Reaching ~112 would have cost ~2.5 points of FG%, landing FG% near 43.9% against a ~47% target** — so E's own stop condition fired ("if ~112 cannot be reached without pulling FG% off target: STOP and bring the user the trade"), and **no trim was taken**. **Landing (harness, ~102 games × 5 seeds, tuned to the MEAN): 117.0 pts / 46.4% FG / 36.7% 3P / 26.8 ast / 13.5 TO / 4.8 blk / 2.8 OOB.** New §3.12 lines: fouls **19.1**/team/game (16.8 → 19.1, inside G's ~19–20 ballpark), fouls **4.87**/team/period, **52.3%** of team-periods in the penalty (43.4% → 52.3%), and-1s **1.87**/team/game, stopped shots **14.9** (14.3 two-pt + **0.59** three-pt), FTA **33.4**/team/game.

**The re-centering was not deferred — the TARGET was contested, which is a different outcome and needed recording.** E framed §3.12 as owning §3.11's 3.9-point debt, with risks.md's live failure mode being "§3.12 forgets to absorb it." §3.12 did not forget; it found the premise unsound. The user checked the §3.4 targets against current figures mid-execution: **points may be ~114–117 and FG% ~47–48**, both *above* the §3.4 numbers (~112 / ~47), which were themselves set from unsourced estimates in #022 D and never revisited. Against a ~115 target, §3.11's "3.9-point debt" was largely an artifact of a stale anchor. **And the two contested targets cannot be reconciled by this pass's lever at all**: the shot `BASE_*` rates move points and FG% **the same direction**, so points-too-high wants a trim while FG%-too-low wants a raise. That is not a re-centering problem; it is a target problem needing a lever that separates **efficiency from volume**. Recorded in a **new `docs/calibration.md`** — created this pass at the user's direction as **the source of truth for targets**, superseding #022 D (which now carries a forward pointer), with both contested rows flagged and the harness's own `(target ~N)` strings updated to match. Promoted to **§3.16**, the last Phase-3 sub-phase, sequenced after §3.13/§3.14 because both move scoring. **Three consecutive passes have now declined this same trim** (§3.10 stopped at 113.8, §3.11 took none, §3.12 took none), each because it cost more calibrated FG% than the points miss was worth — which is evidence about the target, not the passes.

**G's foul-out instrument found a real problem, and it PREDATES §3.12.** The line was added as designed (foul-outs per team per game plus the end-of-game 4/5/6 distribution) and immediately earned its keep: **0.60 foul-outs/team/game against G's ~0.11 ballpark** — and against a user-checked ~0.15–0.25, still **~2.4× high**. G anticipated exactly this possibility ("a real chance the foul-out line reveals a **pre-existing** §3.5 problem, which would have to be triaged on its own"), so it was triaged rather than absorbed: re-running with **both new multipliers zeroed** (reproducing §3.11's exact foul reach) across 5 seeds gives **0.425** — so **~70% of the overshoot predates this pass** and §3.12 contributes only +0.18. Promoted to **§3.13**, moved *ahead* of flagrants by user call ("it seems more core to the game"), with the note that the obvious fix is a trap: trimming the foul rate means `BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL`, the wrong-way lever (#028). The likely real fix is **behavioral** — nothing in `RotationState` reacts to foul count today; a player is forced off only at the hard `FOUL_OUT_LIMIT = 6`. **One correction to G's own text, found while writing the §3.13 brief:** the predicate is `PlayerGameState.isFouledOut()`, **not** `hasFouledOut()` as G names it, and its only consumers are `RotationState.replaceFouledOut()` and `eligible(...)`. `runFatigueSubs()` — the *only* other substitution driver — considers **energy alone and never reads `fouls`**, which is precisely the gap §3.13 exists to close.

**Divergences from the design:** none of substance in A1–G. Beyond the two unplanned fixes above, three test re-baselines, all predicted by A1's trade-off note ("every seed-pinned assertion in the shot flow re-baselines"): `FoulResolverTest.foulNeverTriggeredOnPerimeter`/`foulNeverTriggeredOnThree` **inverted** into `perimeterFoulsAreUncommonButPossible`/`threeFoulsAreRareButPossible` (their premise is false by design now), and `pressureStillNeverFoulsOnJumpShots` → `pressureScalesJumpShotFoulsToo` (the surviving invariant: `defensivePressure` scales a jump-shot foul the same way it scales a drive's). `ShotTypeTest`'s four `isContactType` assertions were **replaced, not deleted**, by the `freeThrowsIfFouled()` rule per the plan. **Coverage:** `FoulResolver`, `ShotType`, `SimConfig` all **100%**; `PossessionEngine` **99.5%** (the same single pre-existing uncovered line as §3.10/§3.11 — no new gap); `sim` package **99.1%**. Full `mvn clean install` gate green — **455 unit + 52 Cucumber**, all coverage checks met (JDK 21).

**§3.12 follow-up (carry forward)**:
- **§3.13 (foul trouble & foul-outs)** — **NEW, and moved ahead of flagrants** (user call). G's instrument measured foul-outs for the first time at **0.60/team/game** vs. a ~0.1–0.25 ballpark, **~70% of it pre-existing** (0.425 at §3.11's foul reach). Needs its own design pass in §3.5/rotation territory: at what foul count does a coach bench a player, does the threshold vary by period, does it lean on `rotationDepth`, and is it a derived predicate (#023 F / #028 A1) rather than new state. **Do not reach for `BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL`** — wrong-way lever (#028).
- **§3.14 (flagrant / technical fouls)** — as previously scoped (was §3.13), still needing its own design pass: it changes **who shoots** (a technical is shot by a chosen shooter, not the fouled player), adds a **possession-retention** path no current model has (a flagrant awards FTs *and* returns the ball), and adds **ejections**. Should build on §3.13's foul-trouble handling — an ejection is a *second* way a player leaves early, and two parallel removal mechanisms would be the #013/#015 smell.
- **§3.15 (`SimConfig` profiles)** — **promoted from a backlog chore to a numbered phase** (user call). Swappable named configs so tuning becomes comparable experiments (`-DcalibrationProfile` beside `-DcalibrationSeed`), which §3.12 wanted badly: its multiplier sweep and `BASE_*` exchange-rate measurement were run **by hand**, editing constants and rebuilding between every reading — the third pass in a row to do so. Scope-fenced to the **developer-facing substrate** (engine + harness, no schema/OpenAPI); the player-facing side (selectable eras, difficulty, custom rules) is Phase 4+. Full reasoning + the open full-replacement-vs-override question in backlog.md.
- **§3.16 (recalibration against verified targets)** — **NEW**, the last Phase-3 sub-phase and a different *kind* of pass (it adds no mechanic; it re-solves numbers — a second §3.4). Owns the contested points/FG% targets and the efficiency-vs-volume lever question. **Prerequisite, and it is not engine work**: verify the benchmarks with sources (a backlog chore) — every number on both sides of this argument is currently unsourced.
- **The `docs/calibration.md` table is now the source of truth for targets** — update it *and* the `CalibrationHarness` `(target ~N)` strings together whenever a target moves. The per-phase landing notes in decisions.md are history; do not retro-edit them.
- **Two independent foul curves** (one for the pre-shot roll, one for the and-1) — B chose one shared table; splitting it is additive if a real need ever appears (e.g. and-1 rates that must be tuned per type independently of stopped-shot rates). No consumer today.
- **A "3-FT trip" statistic** — C deliberately adds no `FreeThrowSource` for it; if a Phase-4 stats consumer wants fouled-three trips as a first-class number, that is the moment to decide whether it is a new source tag or a derived count.
- **The foul-out rate is measured for the first time in §3.12 (G)** — if the line reveals a rate that was already off *before* §3.12 (a §3.5 substitution/fatigue interaction rather than a §3.12 foul-volume effect), that is **not** §3.12's to absorb: triage it as its own item rather than tuning §3.12's multipliers to hide a pre-existing problem. Record the observed rate in the implementation note either way — it is the first-ever observation of a shipped-but-unmeasured mechanism.
- **The always-on calibration tolerance-band test** — risks.md notes this becomes more attractive at §3.12's close-out, since re-centering to ~112 removes the known intentional gap that made an expected value awkward to set. Reconsider it when this pass lands (backlog.md).
- **`committing_team_id` still not on the API** (carried from #028/#029) — populated and queryable, absent from the OpenAPI `GameEvent`; additive whenever a consumer wants it.
- (The §3.7-E shot-clock-pressure, §3.9 finer turnover sub-types, §3.10 observed-split, and §3.6 time-column follow-ups still stand.)

---

### 031 — Foul trouble & foul-outs (§3.13): a probabilistic foul-trouble bench rule scaled by `substitutionAggressiveness` AND the player's value to the team, a rest-then-return cycle rather than a season-ending bench, the over-dispersion root cause found by decomposition (defender selection, not foul volume), and a deliberate refusal to touch the foul rate
**Date**: 2026-08  
**Scope**: §3.13 is the seventh possession-fidelity completion sub-phase (§3.7–§3.16, before Phase 4) and the **first one that is not foul-model work at all** — it is **§3.5/rotation work** (#023 C/D/F territory), triggered by an instrument §3.12 added (#030 G). Today **nothing in the engine reacts to foul *trouble*, only to foul-*out*:** `RotationState.runFatigueSubs()` — the only substitution driver besides the hard `replaceFouledOut()` — reads **energy alone and never reads `fouls`**. A player accumulates fouls with zero consequence until the 6th. §3.13 closes that gap with a **foul-trouble bench rule**. This is **engine work in the `sim` package**: **no schema change, no OpenAPI change, no new `PlayType`, no new `outcome` string, no new coach attribute** (#018's five stand), consistent with every sub-phase since §3.10. **§3.14 (flagrants/technicals) is NOT this pass**, but its ejection path must build on the removal machinery established here rather than adding a parallel one (Decision H). NOT YET BUILT — this entry is the resolved design; the execute-ready plan is todo.md's §3.13 execution plan.

**Decision A — the decomposition came FIRST, and it OVERTURNS the brief's leading hypothesis: the dominant cause is neither foul volume nor missing substitutions, it is that fouls are radically OVER-DISPERSED by defender selection (measured, this pass).** todo.md framed the phase around a single hypothesis (no benching behavior) and asked whether the rate was also implicated. #030 E's decompose-before-you-trim discipline was applied before any lever was chosen, and it found a **third mechanism neither option named**. Measured on seed 1000 (204 team-games, a temporary diagnostic since deleted):

| Quantity | Measured | Read |
|---|---|---|
| League fouls per 36 min | **2.85** | vs. real-NBA ~2.2–2.4 for rotation players — modestly high |
| Foul-outs, **actual** | **0.593** | the headline |
| Foul-outs, **predicted by a flat-rate Poisson** at the engine's own minutes + 2.85/36 | **0.262** | **the load-bearing number** |
| Foul-outs at the engine's minutes but a real-NBA 2.25/36 | 0.094 | |
| Foul-outs at 2.85/36 but no player over 30 min | 0.198 | |

**The actual rate is 2.3× what a flat per-minute foul rate over the engine's own minutes distribution predicts.** That gap is not explainable by volume (the rate is in the ballpark, 19.0/team/game) and not by minutes (they are on their §3.5 targets). Fouls are **concentrated on specific players** far beyond what independent per-minute arrivals would produce — and foul-outs, being a **tail** event at the 6th foul, are hypersensitive to exactly that concentration. **The mechanism is `ShotSelector.pickDefender`, compounded with `FoulResolver.isFoul`:** the defender on a shot is drawn **weighted by `individualDefense`**, and that same defender's foul probability is then scaled by their own `foulProne`. Measured spread over the 359 seeded players: `individualDefense` **mean 10.37, sd 4.11** (p10 5.2 → p90 16.4, a **3.2× spread**), while `foulProne` is nearly flat (**mean 9.66, sd 1.22**). So the best perimeter defenders are drawn as the defender on a hugely disproportionate share of possessions, and every one of those carries foul risk. Modelling the measured `individualDefense` spread into the Poisson gives **0.403 foul-outs/team-game against the 0.425 pre-§3.12 baseline** — the mechanism is essentially fully explanatory. **The per-slot diagnostic confirms it independently:** fouls-per-36 is **flat across every minutes rank** (2.56–3.05 from slot 1 to slot 10), which is the signature of concentration *within* a slot rather than *across* slots — the starters aren't fouling more per minute than the bench, but a subset of players within every tier are.

**What A means for the phase, and it is the most important sentence in this entry: the foul RATE is not the lever, and neither is the defender-selection weighting.** Answering the user's Decision-6 question directly: the user's instinct that "some players tending to foul out is a good thing" is **correct, and it is what the engine is already producing** — the concentration is a *feature* (good defenders guard more and foul more; that is real basketball), not a bug. **What is missing is the counter-pressure real basketball applies to exactly that player: the coach sits him.** The engine has the realistic cause and none of the realistic response. So §3.13 stays **purely behavioral** — it does **not** touch `pickDefender`'s weighting (which would flatten a correct realism), does **not** touch `foulProne`, and does **not** touch any foul base (`BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL` is a wrong-way lever, #028, and `FOUL_MULT_*` is settled on realism grounds, #030 G). **Answering Decision 7 explicitly** (the user asked what decision it even poses): the decision it forced was *which lever this phase is allowed to touch*, and A resolves it — **the lever is `RotationState`, and only `RotationState`.** Had the decomposition instead shown a flat-rate Poisson already reproducing 0.59, the honest conclusion would have been that the foul rate itself was too high and §3.13 would have been a very different (and much more constrained) pass. It did not, so it isn't.

**Decision B — the bench rule is PROBABILISTIC, not a hard threshold, and the probability is driven by TWO factors: the coach's `substitutionAggressiveness` and the player's VALUE to his team (user call).** A player at foul count *f* is a **bench candidate**; whether he actually sits is a per-check probability, not a switch. The crux shape, resolved with the user:
- **A hard threshold was rejected.** "Bench at exactly 4" makes every coach in the league behave identically at the same foul count and produces a visible cliff in the minutes distribution. A probability lets the same rule express "usually sits a 4-foul star early, rarely sits a 4-foul bench big" without branching.
- **`substitutionAggressiveness` sets the base probability at each count**, using the established avg-10 deviation form (#022 / `SimConfig.rotationModifier`) — the same factor `CoachModifiers.subAggressivenessFactor()` already exposes and `runFatigueSubs` already consumes. The user's framing, adopted directly: *a high-aggressiveness coach starts thinking about sitting at 3, a medium one at 4, a low one at 5.* This is expressed **not** as three different thresholds but as **one probability curve per foul count, scaled by the coach factor** — at 3 fouls the base probability is small and only a high-aggressiveness coach's multiplier lifts it to something that fires often; at 5 it is high for everyone.
- **The player's value to the team scales it further, and in the direction the user specified: the BEST players are managed MORE tightly.** A star at 4 fouls is likelier to sit than a 9th man at 4 fouls, because the coach is protecting an asset he needs in the fourth quarter. This is the *opposite* of §3.5's fatigue rule (where starters tolerate **more** fatigue via `STARTER_SUB_THRESHOLD_BONUS` before being pulled), and that inversion is deliberate and correct: **you ride your star when he's tired, you protect him when he's in foul trouble.** Recording it explicitly because it looks like an inconsistency and is not.
- **"Value" is DERIVED — from a defense-leaning SKILL composite, COMBINED with (not replacing) the roster signal (user call).** No new attribute and no new column (#014/#017: don't fabricate a field ahead of a consumer); the inputs are skills `PlayerGameState` already holds. **The formula, set by the user:**

  ```
  value = (individualDefense + rimProtection + defenseRebound + offense + offense) / 5
  where offense = mean(drive, finishing, perimeter, post, longRange)
  ```

  Offense is **deliberately double-weighted**, putting the composite at roughly **60/40 defense-leaning** — the right lean for a *foul-trouble* rule (fouls concentrate on defenders and bigs, per A) while still valuing scorers. **Measured over the 359 seeded players before adoption** (temporary diagnostic, since deleted): **mean 10.06, sd 3.10, p10 6.18 → p90 14.47** — a ~2.3× spread, ample for the factor to differentiate a star from a role player. For contrast, `foulProne` (sd 1.22) could never carry this.

  **The composite is COMBINED with the `lineupRole`/`rotationOrder` signal, not substituted for it (user call).** Measured: starters average **11.95** against the bench's **8.36**, and in **0 of 34 seeded teams** is the highest-value player a non-starter — so the two signals overwhelmingly agree, and combining them is safe rather than contradictory. Where they *do* disagree is precisely where the combination earns its keep: in one sampled team a `rotationOrder`-1 bench player (value 8.90) outranks two starters (6.74, 6.31), so he gets real protection while the roster signal still keeps the weaker starters from being treated as scrubs.

  **A correction to this entry's own first draft, recorded because it was wrong in a way worth not repeating:** the design initially derived value from **the player's index in the `squad` list**, on the belief that `GameSimulator.buildRotation` ordered it by rotation priority throughout. **It does not.** Verified against the code: starters carry a **null `rotationOrder`** (#014) and are appended in whatever order `team.getPlayers()` returns, so **squad indices 0–4 are unordered among themselves** — only indices 5+ (the bench, sorted by `rotationOrder`) carry real priority. Squad index would therefore have made "the most protected player" a database-ordering accident. **The accompanying rejection of a skill aggregate as a #013/#015 duplicate-source violation was also overreaching and is withdrawn:** #014 makes `lineupRole`/`rotationOrder` authoritative for *who plays and in what order*, which is a **different question** from *who is most valuable*. A talent composite is not a competing answer to #014's question, so no duplicate source of truth arises.

**Decision C — the threshold does NOT vary by period or time remaining (user call: keep it simple).** A period-aware rule ("5 fouls in Q2 is a crisis, 5 in the last two minutes of Q4 is played through") is more realistic and was the explicit content of open question 2 — and it is **deliberately not built**. The user's call was to avoid the added dimension in this pass. **This is a real fidelity cost and is recorded as such**, not waved away: the rule will bench a 5-foul star with 90 seconds left in a close game, which no real coach does. **Two things make that cost tolerable rather than wrong.** First, Decision D's return cycle means the player is not gone — he is resting and re-entering, so the late-game absence is partial rather than absolute. Second, and more importantly, the *aggregate* this pass is judged on (foul-outs/team/game) is nearly insensitive to end-of-game behavior, because a player who reaches 5 fouls in the final two minutes has very little exposure left in which to reach 6 — the tail is populated by players who hit 5 **early**, which is exactly where the rule is realistic. **Carried forward as a §3.13 follow-up** (a `gameProgress`-aware relaxation), and flagged for §3.14: an ejection is unconditional and time-blind by nature, so it does not inherit this simplification.

**Decision D — a benched player RETURNS through the ORDINARY freshness path; the sit is made sticky by the energy state that already exists, with no timer and no competing foul-trouble roll (user call, and the answer that makes B safe).** The user asked how long a player stays down and whether `substitutionAggressiveness` should drive it too. **Resolved: it should NOT be a separate duration knob.** Modelling "sits for N possessions" would introduce the first **timer** in a rotation system that is deliberately state-derived and clock-free (#021, #023 A) — a per-player countdown is stored state of exactly the kind #023 F refused.

**The mechanism, stated precisely — the first draft of this decision was vague here and the vagueness hid a real defect:** once the foul-trouble rule benches a player, **he becomes an ordinary bench player**. There is no "benched for fouls" status, no exclusion that must later be lifted, and — the load-bearing part — **no foul-trouble roll competing to bring him back**. Re-entry runs **only** through the existing `runFatigueSubs` freshness path. Because he keeps accumulating no fouls while sitting but *does* recover energy (`ENERGY_RECOVERY_PER_POSSESSION = 5.5` against a `2.6` drain — recovery is ~2× drain, §3.5 B), he becomes a progressively better `freshestEligibleBench` candidate over several possessions. **That gradient is the return timer**, and it costs nothing to build.

**This resolves a genuine oscillation risk the first draft only pinned with a test.** Substitution is re-decided **once per possession per team — ~100 decision points per team per game** (`PossessionEngine.simulate()` calls `advancePossession()` for both teams every possession, ~one per 29 seconds of game time). A rule that re-rolled *both* directions at that cadence could plausibly flip a 4-foul player on and off across consecutive possessions, which would be visibly unrealistic. Making the sit sticky and the return gradual means the **only** thing that can put him back is accumulated rest, which imposes a natural multi-possession floor **without a countdown**. Two existing guards help but were not sufficient alone: `runFatigueSubs` swaps only if the bench player is genuinely fresher, and it makes **at most one swap per call**.

`substitutionAggressiveness` is therefore used **once**, for the sit decision, and is **not** re-applied to the return — a second application would double-count one coach trait and make an aggressive coach both quick to sit *and* slow to return, which is not what the attribute means.
