**Decision E — DERIVED predicate over the existing `fouls` counter; no stored `inFoulTrouble` flag (user lean, and the house discipline).** Confirmed as open question 4 asked. The foul-trouble state is `p.getFouls() >= someCount`, evaluated fresh at each between-possession check, exactly as `isFouledOut()` is (#023 F) and exactly as the §3.10 penalty status is derived from the event log (#028 A1). No boolean is stored. **The one thing that makes this non-trivial here, and it must be stated because it is the difference between E working and E being naive:** unlike `isFouledOut()`, foul trouble is **not monotonic in its effect** — a player can be benched, return, and be benched again, so the *predicate* is stable but the *response* to it is re-decided every check. That is fine and is precisely what D relies on, but it means the predicate must be a **pure question about the player** (`foulTroubleLevel(p)` → how deep in trouble is he) with the *decision* living in `RotationState`, not a `shouldBench()` predicate on `PlayerGameState` that would need to know about the coach. **Keeping the probability out of `PlayerGameState` also keeps `PlayerGameState` RNG-free**, which it is today and should remain.

**Decision F — when the bench is too thin, the SOFT rule yields; the foul-out bar is absolute and is not what F trades against (open question 5, wording corrected after a user challenge).** **State the invariant first, because F's first draft was worded in a way that implied otherwise: a player who has fouled out NEVER returns to that game. Full stop.** That is not a §3.13 decision, a preference, or something the soft rule can weigh — it is already absolute in the shipped code, and §3.13 does not touch it. Verified: `PlayerGameState.isFouledOut()` (`fouls >= FOUL_OUT_LIMIT`) is enforced in **two** places — `replaceFouledOut()` forces him off, and **`eligible(...)` filters him out of every candidate pool**, which `freshestEligibleBench` is the sole consumer of. So no code path can select him. Being **derived** rather than a stored flag makes this *stronger*, not weaker: `fouls` only ever increases, so the predicate is monotonic and cannot flip back, whereas a stored boolean could in principle be cleared by a bug. **The single exception is #023 F's never-below-5 last resort** — if literally every player is fouled out, a fouled-out player stays on rather than the floor dropping below five, because every pick site assumes exactly 5. That is a degenerate-roster guard, not a return path, and it should never fire in practice.

**What F actually trades is the NEW soft rule against the bench's depth**, and nothing else. §3.13's rule is strictly **softer** than a foul-out and must never be allowed to weaken a harder constraint. The ordering:
1. `replaceFouledOut()` — the **hard** rule (6 fouls), unchanged, still first, still allowed to reach the **full** bench.
2. The **foul-trouble** sub — the **soft** rule, new. It fires only if a genuinely eligible replacement exists (`eligible(...)`, so a fouled-out player is never a candidate). **If no eligible replacement is available, the rule simply does not fire and the foul-troubled player keeps playing.** A soft preference never overrides a hard invariant, and it never reaches for an ineligible player to satisfy itself.
3. `runFatigueSubs()` — unchanged, energy-only, still last, and (per D) the **only** path by which a foul-trouble-benched player returns.

**The foul-trouble sub draws from the `rotationDepth` window, not the full bench** — matching `runFatigueSubs`, not `replaceFouledOut`. A coach managing foul trouble is making a *rotation* decision (he goes to his bench), not an emergency one; reaching past his rotation for a 4-foul situation would make `rotationDepth` meaningless in precisely the case a tight-rotation coach would ride the player instead. **A tight rotation therefore naturally yields more foul-outs than a deep one, with no extra modelling** — a genuine, free, and correct emergent behavior worth watching for on the harness. **Answering the user's late-game question in open question 5 ("does a coach re-insert a 5-foul star?"):** yes, but through F's yielding and D's return cycle rather than a special late-game clause — C declined the time-awareness that a purpose-built rule would need.

**Decision G — the target is the 4/5/6 DISTRIBUTION, not the headline count, and the minutes cost is budgeted UP FRONT (the user's second explicit ask).** calibration.md already directs that §3.13 be judged by the distribution, because a benching rule works by moving players out of the 5-foul bucket before they reach 6. Modelled cost of the rule at three candidate aggressiveness levels (same over-dispersed Poisson model as A, so directional not exact — **the harness settles it**):

| Rule | Foul-outs | Minutes lost, slot 1 | slot 2 | slot 3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| none (today) | 0.39 *(model)* / **0.59** *(measured)* | — | — | — |
| bench from 5 | 0.27 | −1.1 | −0.8 | −0.3 |
| bench from 4 | **0.125** | **−2.4** | −1.8 | −1.0 |

**The honest read: reaching the ~0.1–0.25 ballpark costs roughly 1–2.5 minutes off the top starter, which lands slot 1 at ~34.2–35.5 against the §3.5 target of ~34–36 — inside the target, but consuming most of its headroom** (it currently sits at 36.6, i.e. at the top of the band). This is the trade the user asked to see, and it is **acceptable but not free**: §3.13 spends slack in a calibrated §3.5 target to fix a ballpark miss. **The explicit stop condition, in the #030 E shape:** if landing foul-outs inside ~0.1–0.25 pushes **slot 1 below ~34** or moves any §3.4 aggregate materially, **STOP and bring the user the trade** rather than pushing through — a calibrated target is worth more than a ballpark. **A partial landing (~0.3) that preserves minutes is an acceptable outcome** and is likely the right one, given the ballpark's own numbers are unsourced (#030 G's 0.11 and a searched 0.15–0.25). **Points will also move and must NOT be chased** — benching starters lowers scoring, and points/FG% re-centering is **§3.16**, whose targets are contested. Report the aggregates; do not re-center (the todo.md scope fence).

**Decision H — foul-outs are PROMOTED from ballpark to TARGET at close-out, and §3.14's ejection path must reuse this removal machinery.** calibration.md flags the promotion as a §3.13 close-out decision ("once a phase has deliberately tuned against it, someone owns it"). §3.13 is that phase, so **the close-out updates calibration.md's Foul-outs row from `ballpark` to `TARGET`** with the landed value, **and** updates the `CalibrationHarness` `(target ~N)` string in the same change (the calibration.md rule). **The number itself stays soft** — it is promoted because it now has an owner, not because it became sourced; verifying it belongs with §3.16's benchmark-verification prerequisite. **For §3.14** (the todo.md scope fence's explicit interaction): an ejection is a **second way a player leaves early**, and two parallel removal mechanisms would be the #013/#015 smell. §3.13 leaves `RotationState` with a clean three-tier structure — **hard/forced** (`replaceFouledOut`), **soft/preference** (foul trouble), **fatigue** — and a flagrant ejection is unambiguously the **hard/forced** tier. It should extend the eligibility filter `eligible(...)` uses rather than adding a fourth path. **The one thing §3.14 cannot reuse is E's derivation**: an ejection is *not* derivable from a counter the way `fouls >= 6` is, so it will need real state — the first genuine exception to the #023 F discipline, and one §3.14 must argue explicitly rather than inherit.

**Rationale**: *(A)* Both prior foul passes proved the headline hides the mechanism and each found a *different* dominant channel (#030 E), so decomposing before choosing a lever is now house discipline — and it paid, overturning the brief's own hypothesis and identifying a cause (over-dispersion via defender selection) that neither of the two options on the table named. Establishing that the rate is *not* the problem is what licenses the rest of the pass to stay behavioral, and it is the difference between fixing the mechanism and flattening a correct realism. *(B)* A probability rather than a threshold keeps the whole league from behaving identically at the same foul count and avoids a cliff in the minutes distribution; scaling by `substitutionAggressiveness` reuses the attribute that already means "how readily does this coach pull a player" (no 6th coach attribute, #018's five stand). The value composite is derived from skills the engine already holds rather than a fabricated "star" field (the #014/#017 refusal #023 C also made), is defense-leaning because that is where foul trouble actually bites (A), and was **measured before adoption** rather than assumed — its sd of 3.10 is what makes it capable of differentiating at all, and the starters-11.95-vs-bench-8.36 separation is what makes combining it with the roster signal safe rather than contradictory. The star-protection inversion relative to fatigue is real coaching behavior, and recording it prevents a later reader "fixing" the apparent inconsistency. *(C)* The user's simplicity call, with the fidelity cost stated honestly and the reason it is tolerable made concrete (the foul-out tail is populated early, not late). *(D)* Returning through the ordinary freshness path beats both a duration timer and a competing foul-trouble roll: it adds no state to a deliberately state-derived system (#021/#023 A/F), and the ~2× recovery-over-drain gradient supplies a natural multi-possession return floor for free. The stickiness is the part that matters — at ~100 substitution decision points per team per game, a rule that re-rolled in **both** directions would visibly oscillate, so making only the *sit* probabilistic and letting the *return* be earned by accumulated rest is what keeps the behavior realistic without a countdown. Using `substitutionAggressiveness` once rather than twice keeps one attribute from silently governing two opposed behaviors. *(E)* The #013/#015 duplicate-source trap, applied exactly as #023 F and #028 A1 applied it; splitting "how deep in trouble" (player) from "does he sit" (rotation + coach + RNG) keeps `PlayerGameState` RNG-free and keeps coach knowledge out of it. *(F)* A soft preference must never break a hard invariant, and the never-below-5 rule already exists to protect the exactly-5 assumption every pick site makes — reusing it beats a second fallback with its own edge cases; drawing from the `rotationDepth` window rather than the full bench keeps `rotationDepth` meaningful and yields the tight-rotation-fouls-out-more behavior for free. *(G)* calibration.md already directs judging by the distribution, and pricing the minutes cost before building is what turns "benching stars will move minutes" from a discovered surprise into a budgeted trade with a stop condition — the #030 E stop-condition pattern, which fired correctly in §3.12 and should be armed here too. *(H)* A number a phase has deliberately tuned against needs an owner (calibration.md's own rule), and naming §3.14's reuse now prevents the parallel-mechanism smell before it is written rather than after.  
**Trade-off**: *(A)* The decomposition consumed real design-session time and two temporary diagnostics (since deleted, so the finding is recorded here but **not reproducible from the tree** — anyone re-checking it must rebuild them; the numbers in A's table are the durable artifact). *(B)* A probabilistic rule **consumes RNG**, which is a genuine break from #023 C's "substitution decisions consume NO RNG draw" — see the determinism note in the status block; it is the single most consequential mechanical consequence of this pass. It also introduces two new tuning surfaces (the per-count base probabilities and the value scaling) into a rotation system that had two. *(C)* The engine will bench a 5-foul star in the final minute of a close game — unrealistic, accepted, and carried forward. *(D)* The return timing is emergent rather than controlled, so "how long does a foul-troubled player actually sit" is an *output* to observe on the harness, not an input to set; if it lands implausibly (never returns, or returns instantly) the fix is the sit probability or the energy constants, **not** a new duration knob. Note also the coupling this creates: the return gradient is governed by `ENERGY_RECOVERY_PER_POSSESSION`, a §3.5-calibrated fatigue constant — so retuning fatigue in a later pass silently retunes foul-trouble return timing too. *(E)* The predicate is stable but the sit response is re-decided every check (~100 times per team per game), so the rule is evaluated far more often than a one-shot removal — cheap, and D's sticky-sit/earned-return shape is what keeps that cadence from producing oscillation, but the behavior is emergent rather than bounded by construction and still warrants an explicit test. *(F)* Foul-trouble benching silently does nothing for a coach with a very tight `rotationDepth` and a thin bench — correct behavior, but it means the rule's effectiveness varies by team in a way the league-average harness number will hide. *(G)* §3.13 spends headroom in a calibrated §3.5 minutes target to close a ballpark miss, and it moves points in a phase explicitly forbidden from re-centering them — so it hands §3.16 a moved baseline. *(H)* Promoting foul-outs to a TARGET commits future passes to a number that is still unsourced; mitigated by keeping it soft and pointing at §3.16's verification prerequisite.  
**Alternatives considered**: *(A)* **Take the brief's hypothesis (missing benching) at face value and skip the decomposition** — rejected: it is the exact discipline #030 E imposed, and it would have missed the over-dispersion finding entirely, leaving a builder to tune a benching rule against a cause he had mis-modelled. **Trim `BASE_NO_BASKET_FOUL`** — rejected outright, twice over: it is a measured **wrong-way** lever (#028 — trimming it *raises* points), and A shows the rate is not the problem anyway. **Re-open §3.12's `FOUL_MULT_*`** — rejected: settled on realism grounds with the user (#030 G), and off the table by the todo.md scope fence. **Flatten `pickDefender`'s `individualDefense` weighting to reduce concentration** — rejected, and it is the most tempting wrong answer A exposes: it would "fix" the number by deleting a *correct* piece of realism (good defenders do guard more), and it would silently move blocks, steals, and shot contests, which are calibrated. **Lower the league foul rate to ~2.25/36** — rejected: it reaches 0.094 on the model, but 19.0 fouls/team/game is inside its own ~19–20 ballpark, so this trades a passing number for a failing one. *(B)* **A hard threshold at 4 or 5** — rejected (user call): identical league-wide behavior and a visible minutes cliff. **A 6th coach attribute for foul-trouble management** — rejected (already settled, user call 2026-08): `substitutionAggressiveness` already means the right thing, and inventing a sibling field ahead of its consumer is the #014/#017 trap. **Derive value from the player's squad index** — rejected on inspection (see B): starters carry a null `rotationOrder` and are appended in `team.getPlayers()` order, so indices 0–4 are unordered and value would have been a database accident. **Derive value from `offensiveWeight()`** — rejected: offense-only, so it rates a defensive anchor as low-value, which is backwards for a foul-trouble rule (fouls concentrate on defenders and bigs, A). **A flat average of all 23 skills** — rejected: dilutes the defensive signal that matters here with skills irrelevant to foul trouble. **Value REPLACING the `lineupRole`/`rotationOrder` signal** — rejected (user call): the measurement showed the two agree at the top in all 34 teams, so replacement buys nothing, while combining preserves the coach's own lineup intent where the two diverge mid-roster. **Protect the star LESS rather than more** (mirroring the fatigue rule) — rejected: backwards as coaching, and the user specified the opposite. *(C)* **A period- or time-remaining-aware threshold** — rejected for this pass (user call: complexity), carried forward; it is the single largest fidelity gap §3.13 knowingly leaves. *(D)* **A "sits for N possessions" duration timer** — rejected: the first stored timer in a deliberately clock-free, state-derived rotation system (#021/#023 A/F), and unnecessary once energy recovery supplies the gradient. **A competing foul-trouble roll to bring the player BACK** — rejected, and this was the first draft's actual defect: re-rolling in both directions at ~100 checks per team per game is what would make a 4-foul player flicker on and off across consecutive possessions. The sit is probabilistic; the return is earned by rest. **Re-apply `substitutionAggressiveness` to the return decision** — rejected: double-counts one attribute across two opposed behaviors. *(E)* **A stored `inFoulTrouble` boolean** — rejected: a second source of truth for a fact `fouls` already holds (#013/#015, #023 F). **A `shouldBench()` predicate on `PlayerGameState`** — rejected: it would need the coach factor and an RNG source inside a class that has neither and should keep neither. *(F)* **A dedicated foul-trouble fallback path** — rejected: `replaceFouledOut`'s never-below-5 invariant already covers it; a second fallback is a second set of edge cases. **Let foul-trouble subs reach the full bench like foul-outs do** — rejected: it makes `rotationDepth` meaningless exactly where a tight-rotation coach would ride the player, and it loses the free tight-rotation-fouls-out-more emergent behavior. *(G)* **Chase the ~0.11 end of the ballpark** — rejected: it costs ~2.4 minutes off the top starter and would push slot 1 out of its calibrated band to fix an unsourced number. **Re-center the points drop this pass causes** — rejected: §3.16 owns it and the targets are contested (calibration.md, and the todo.md fence). *(H)* **Leave foul-outs a ballpark** — rejected: calibration.md's own rule is that the phase which tunes against a number owns it. **Have §3.14 add its own ejection-removal path** — rejected pre-emptively: two parallel removal mechanisms is the #013/#015 smell; it extends the hard/forced tier.  
**Status of §3.13 decisions**: A–H all resolved by this entry (mirrors #021–#030). Net schema change: **NONE** — no new column, no OpenAPI change, no new `PlayType`, no new `outcome` string, no new `FreeThrowSource`, **and no new coach attribute** (#018's five stand). New engine pieces: foul-trouble constants in `SimConfig` (the per-foul-count sit probabilities + the value-scaling sensitivity + their helper — spelling at execution), a **derived** foul-trouble level and a **derived value composite** on `PlayerGameState` (both pure questions about the player — no stored flag, no RNG, no new field — E/B), and a new **soft** substitution step in `RotationState` sequenced **between** `replaceFouledOut()` and `runFatigueSubs()` (F), reading `CoachModifiers.subAggressivenessFactor()` — **already threaded via `TeamContext`, so no new plumbing** — combined with the value composite and the existing `isStarter()`/`rotationOrder` roster signal (B). `CalibrationHarness` needs **no new instrument**: #030 G's foul-out line and 4/5/6 distribution are exactly the right ones, and the §3.5 per-slot minutes line is the cost gauge (G). **Determinism — the significant mechanical consequence, and it REVISES #023 C:** that decision made substitution deterministic and **RNG-free** on purpose ("a substitution *roll* — rejected: consumes RNG, churns the seed-pinned stream, and subs are a coaching *decision*, not chance"). **B's probabilistic rule reverses that**, and the reversal is argued rather than incidental: the thing being modelled here is genuinely **not** a deterministic policy — two coaches facing an identical 4-foul situation make different calls, and #023 C's own reasoning (subs are a decision, not chance) applies to *fatigue* subs, where the trigger is a measurable state, far better than to foul trouble, where the trigger is a judgement. The cost is real and must be planned for: **`RotationState.advancePossession()` will need a `RandomGenerator`**, which it does not take today, and **every seed-pinned assertion re-baselines** — a shift comparable to §3.12's. **The draw must be taken unconditionally at a fixed point** (not only when a candidate exists), or the stream will fork on rotation state and make the shift unreproducible. The execute-ready task sequence is todo.md's "§3.13 execution plan." **Open-at-execution (small, constrained):** the exact per-foul-count base probabilities and the value-scaling **sensitivity** (constrained by B's user-specified intent — high aggressiveness thinks about it at 3, medium 4, low 5, and better players managed more tightly — and settled against the harness per G); **how the value composite and the roster signal are combined** (B fixes that they *are* combined and not substituted, and fixes the composite's formula; the exact blend is a tuning shape); where exactly the RNG draw is taken inside `advancePossession()` (constrained: fixed point, unconditional); and the final landing plus how much minutes cost is accepted (G's stop condition, re-agreed with the user against **multiple seeds tuned to the mean**, #029 E). **Not open:** the lever (A — `RotationState` only, no foul-rate and no `pickDefender` change), the no-new-coach-attribute call (B), **the value formula itself** (B, user-set), **combine-not-replace** (B, user call), **sticky sit / return only via the ordinary freshness path** (D, user call), the derived-not-stored discipline (E), and the absolute never-returns bar on a fouled-out player (F).

**Implementation note (from execution, 2026-08).** Shipped as A–H specify, with **one addition the design did not anticipate** and **one landing that misses the ballpark on purpose**. New pieces exactly as the status block called for: `SimConfig.FOUL_TROUBLE_SIT_PROBABILITY` + `FOUL_TROUBLE_VALUE_SENSITIVITY` + the roster-signal constants + `foulTroubleSitProbability(...)` / `rosterProtectionFactor(...)`; **derived** `PlayerGameState.foulTroubleLevel()` and `valueComposite()` (no field, no flag, no RNG, no coach knowledge); `RotationState.runFoulTroubleSub(...)` sequenced between `replaceFouledOut()` and `runFatigueSubs()`, drawing from `benchWithinDepth()`; and the RNG threaded as a **method parameter** on `advancePossession(RandomGenerator)`, leaving all 8 constructor call sites untouched as planned. The draw is unconditional and fixed-point. **No seed-pinned assertion actually re-baselined** — the engine tests assert structural properties rather than pinned values, so the anticipated churn did not materialize; `determinismHoldsWithSubstitutionOn` still passes and its stale "subs are deterministic" comment was corrected. `CalibrationHarness` needed no new instrument, as predicted.

**The divergence — a fifth factor, `FOUL_TROUBLE_FRESHNESS_MARGIN`, that D's mechanism turned out to require.** D argues the sit is made sticky "for free" by energy recovery, with the return running only through `runFatigueSubs`. **Measured during execution, that is not sufficient on its own.** A player benched for foul trouble rests to full, returns via the freshness path as the freshest man on the floor — and is then re-benched by the *sit* rule on the very next possession, because a bench player at 100 energy now out-rests him at 97.4. The result was exactly the flicker D set out to prevent: observed 1-possession stints, on and off across consecutive possessions. **D's asymmetry (only the sit is probabilistic) prevents a competing *return* roll; it does not prevent an immediate *re-sit*.** The fix stays inside D's constraints — no stored flag, no status, no timer: the rule now requires the replacement to be fresher **by a margin**, mirroring the `>` comparison `runFatigueSubs` already makes. This is derived from energy state that already exists. Pinned by `RotationStateTest`'s average-sit-length assertion (≥3 possessions) rather than by the constant's size, deliberately, so the guard survives retuning.

**Counter-intuitive tuning result worth recording: a LARGER margin makes foul-outs WORSE.** Sweep at a fixed curve — margin 12 → 0.53 (it vetoed ~69% of fired rolls), 8 → 0.46, 4 → 0.51, 3 → 0.45, 2 → 0.40, **1 → 0.377**, 0 → 0.40. Both ends are bad: too large blocks legitimate sits, zero lets the rule swap for a replacement fresher by a rounding error (churn without removing exposure). **1.0 is the optimum**, and it sits *below* one possession's drain (2.6) — the anti-oscillation guarantee comes from the margin combined with a returning player entering at a full tank, not from the constant being large.

**Final constants**: sit curve `{0, 0, 0, 0.120, 0.500, 0.900, 0}` (indices = foul count), `FOUL_TROUBLE_VALUE_SENSITIVITY = 0.55`, `FOUL_TROUBLE_STARTER_BONUS = 0.15`, `FOUL_TROUBLE_BENCH_DISCOUNT_PER_SLOT = 0.05`, `FOUL_TROUBLE_MIN_ROSTER_FACTOR = 0.70`, `FOUL_TROUBLE_FRESHNESS_MARGIN = 1.0`. Resolving the open-at-execution items: the **blend** of composite and roster signal is multiplicative (a starter bonus, a per-slot bench discount floored so a deep reserve is protected less but never exempt); the **draw site** is immediately after energy drain/recovery and before `replaceFouledOut()`.

**Landing (harness, 102 games × 5 seeds, tuned to the mean per #029 E):**
<pre>
  Points/team 117.0 | FG% 46.6% | 3P% 36.6% | Assists 26.7 | Turnovers 13.8
  Blocks/team 5.0   | Fouls 19.0/team/game
  Foul-outs 0.388 (from 0.616)   |  Players at 4/5/6: 1.00 / 0.52 / 0.39
  Minutes: 36.1 / 33.2 / 31.1 / 28.7 / 26.8 / 24.6 / 22.6 / 20.2 / 16.7
</pre>
**G's stop condition did NOT fire, and the reason matters: the minutes cost was ~0.5, not the budgeted 1–2.5.** The top starter went 36.6 → 36.1 — *further into* the calibrated ~34–36 band, not out of it. The §3.4 aggregates are unmoved (points 117.0 → 117.0, FG% 46.4% → 46.6%). The model in G over-predicted the cost because it assumed a benched player stays benched; D's return cycle gives most of the minutes back.

**The landing misses the ~0.1–0.25 ballpark, and that is the honest answer rather than a shortfall — the lever is SATURATED.** Measured directly: raising the curve to `{0.25, 0.75, 0.95}` gives 0.407 and `{0.40, 0.90, 0.98}` gives 0.382 — i.e. **~60% more substitutions move the number by nothing.** The binding constraint is not how readily the coach sits the player; it is that **D's earned return sends him back into the same over-dispersed defender draw (A) that gave him the fouls**. Benching harder returns him sooner. Reaching ~0.25 requires a lever this phase does not own, and per G a *calibrated* minutes target outranks an *unsourced* ballpark — so §3.13 stopped at the saturation point rather than spending §3.5's headroom on a number that would not have moved anyway. **~0.39 is promoted to a soft TARGET (H)** in calibration.md, with the harness string updated in the same change.

**Coverage**: `mvn clean install` green — *All coverage checks have been met.* 487 tests. New tests: 15 in `RotationStateTest` (sit/yield/precedence/sticky-sit/oscillation/star-inversion/coach-scaling/depth-window/unconditional-draw), 5 in `PlayerGameStateTest` (the derived level, the cap, and the value formula asserted **exactly** against the user-set composite), 9 in `SimConfigTest`, 1 in `PossessionEngineTest` (the stream does not fork on foul state). `TestPlayerFactory` gained a value-varying rotation-player overload.

**§3.13 follow-up (carry forward)**:
- **Getting foul-outs below ~0.38 needs a lever §3.13 does not own** (the saturation finding above). Three candidates, none of them this phase's: **(1)** relax D's earned return — a period- or possession-aware minimum sit, which is C's deferred time-awareness and would need the `gameProgress` notion the rotation step lacks; **(2)** reduce the over-dispersion at source (A's `pickDefender` weighting) — explicitly forbidden here because it would delete correct realism and silently move calibrated blocks/steals/contests, but it *is* where the 2.3× actually lives; **(3)** accept ~0.39 as correct for this engine and re-verify the benchmark instead (§3.16's prerequisite). **Do not simply raise the sit curve — that has been measured and does nothing.**
- **`FOUL_TROUBLE_FRESHNESS_MARGIN` couples the foul-trouble rule to the §3.5 energy constants** in a second place (D already noted the return gradient does). Retuning `ENERGY_DRAIN_PER_POSSESSION` or `ENERGY_RECOVERY_PER_POSSESSION` silently retunes both the return timing *and* how often the soft rule can fire at all. §3.15's profiles should keep these together.
- **Period- / time-remaining-aware foul trouble** (C, deliberately deferred) — the largest fidelity gap this pass knowingly leaves: the engine will bench a 5-foul star in the final minute. Needs a `gameProgress` notion the rotation step does not have today (the possession loop knows `period`, but `RotationState.advancePossession()` is not passed it — a small, additive plumbing change whenever this is picked up).
- **`substitutionAggressiveness` now governs TWO behaviors** (fatigue subs *and* foul-trouble subs) that need not correlate in reality — a coach can be quick with tired legs and stubborn about foul trouble. **An accepted cost of the reuse call (B), not an oversight.** Splitting it into a second attribute is purely additive if a consumer ever wants the distinction.
- **The over-dispersion finding itself (A) outlives this phase.** Fouls concentrate because `pickDefender` weights by `individualDefense` (sd 4.11 against `foulProne`'s 1.22). §3.13 deliberately does not touch it, but the same concentration mechanism applies to **blocks, steals, and shot contests** — any future pass finding one of those over-dispersed should look here first rather than re-deriving it. **The diagnostics that measured it were temporary and are deleted**; A's table is the durable record.
- **Foul-outs promoted to a TARGET at close-out** (H) — update calibration.md's row **and** the `CalibrationHarness` `(target ~N)` string in the same change. The number stays soft until §3.16's benchmark-verification prerequisite sources it.
- **§3.14 inherits the three-tier removal structure** (H) — hard/forced, soft/preference, fatigue. An ejection is the **hard** tier and must extend `eligible(...)`, not add a fourth path. **That half held through both halves of §3.14** (#032 F, #034 F): `isDisqualified(...)` now carries three causes behind one `eligible(...)` filter, and no removal path was ever added. ~~It will need **real stored state** (an ejection is not derivable from a counter), the first genuine exception to #023 F's derive-don't-store discipline~~ — **WRONG, and disproved twice** (#032 F for the two-technical case, #034 F for the flagrant-2). An ejection *is* derivable from a counter whenever the underlying fact is **absorbing**: `technicalFouls >= 2` and `flagrantTwos >= 1` are monotonic exactly as `fouls >= 6` is, and the limit being 1 changes nothing about the shape. **The prediction is retired.** The test to apply before predicting the exception again is #031 E's: it holds only for a fact that is genuinely **non**-monotonic (the way `inFoulTrouble` is — sit, recover, return, sit again), and no disqualification in this model is.
- **§3.16 inherits a moved baseline** — §3.13 lowers points by benching starters and is forbidden from re-centering (G). Whatever it lands becomes §3.16's starting point.
- **§3.13's rule is a TEMPLATE for strategic substitution, not a one-off — and it is the engine's FIRST one** (user observation during the design pass). Until now the engine had exactly **one elective** sub reason (fatigue) plus two rule-forced ones (foul-out, and §3.14's ejection); everything a coach actually decides — matchup subs, going small, riding a hot hand, closing lineups, score-aware rotation — is absent. Fatigue was built first because it was **available** (§3.5 already had a continuous `currentEnergy`), not because it ranked highest. **All the remaining strategic subs are blocked on one shared prerequisite: the rotation step has no game-situation awareness** — `advancePossession()` is not passed the `period`, let alone the score margin. **That is where C's deferred time-awareness and the declined score-aware reinsertion actually belong**; both were framed here as foul-trouble limitations but are really symptoms of that single absence. A later strategic sub should extend #031's shape (probabilistic, coach-scaled, value-weighted, sticky-sit/earned-return) rather than invent a parallel one — the same discipline H imposes on §3.14. Parked in [ideas.md](ideas.md) with the full category list; **not planned work**.
- **The rotation system's tuning surface has grown** — `SimConfig` now carries fatigue thresholds, rotation depth, *and* foul-trouble probabilities, all steered by two coach attributes. If a third rotation behavior wants a knob, that is the signal to reconsider whether `substitutionAggressiveness` should split (see above) and a natural fit for §3.15's profiles.
- (The §3.7-E shot-clock-pressure, §3.9 finer turnover sub-types, §3.10 observed-split, §3.12 3PA-volume/clamp-helper, and §3.6 time-column follow-ups still stand.)

### 032 — Technical fouls (§3.14a): the phase SPLIT in two on the finding that technicals and flagrants share nothing but the word "foul", a rate rolled OUTSIDE the possession flow with no causal model at all, a separate counter that does not feed the 6-foul limit, an ejection that stays DERIVED (deferring #023 F's exception to §3.14b), a deterministic best-shooter FT rule beside the existing weighted draw, and the clamp-helper consolidation folded in at its fourth site
**Date**: 2026-08  
**Scope**: §3.14 is the eighth possession-fidelity completion sub-phase (§3.7–§3.16, before Phase 4) and the **last new mechanic** before §3.15 (profiles) and §3.16 (recalibration). **This entry splits it in two and resolves only the first half (Decision A).** §3.14a is **technical fouls**: a non-contact, behavioral penalty the engine has no path for — today *every* foul in the model is a by-product of a contest (`ShotSelector.pickDefender` → `FoulResolver.isFoul`), so a foul that is not caused by the possession at all cannot be expressed. §3.14b is **flagrant fouls** and is deliberately NOT this pass (its open questions are in todo.md; it owns the possession-retention fork and the genuine stored-state exception). This is **engine work in the `sim` package**: **no schema change, no OpenAPI change, no new `PlayType`** (a technical is a *kind of foul* — the #025 F / #026 E / #028 D reuse discipline), **no new `FreeThrowSource`**… **one new `outcome` string** (free text since #020, so no migration), **no new coach attribute** (#018's five stand), and **no new player attribute or skill** (Decision C). It also folds in **#030's clamp-helper consolidation at its fourth site** (Decision H). NOT YET BUILT — this entry is the resolved design; the execute-ready plan is todo.md's §3.14a execution plan.

**Decision A — §3.14 SPLITS: §3.14a = technicals, §3.14b = flagrants, and the split is forced by the finding that the two share NOTHING but the word "foul" (user call, and the crux of this pass).** todo.md framed §3.14 as one phase with one taxonomy question ("are they one mechanic or two?"). Answering it honestly decides the phase's shape rather than a detail inside it. **Enumerated, the two have no common surface:**

| | Technical | Flagrant |
|---|---|---|
| Trigger | Behavioral, **not caused by the possession** | A contact foul that already happened |
| Where it rolls | `RotationState` (between possessions) | Inside the possession flow, off an existing foul |
| Committer | Drawn from the on-floor five | **Already picked** — the fouling defender |
| Free throws | 1 | 2 |
| Possession | **Unchanged** — resumes from the point of interruption | **Forks** — FTs *and* the ball (a path no model has) |
| 6-foul limit | Does **not** count (Decision E) | **Does** count |
| Ejection trigger | 2 in a game — **derivable from a counter** | Flagrant-2 — a severity grade, **not derivable** |

The only shared machinery is `awardFreeThrows` (already shared by three situations since #029 D) and the `eligible(...)` ejection seam (#031 H). **Building them as one resolver with one `PlayType` and one rate would be the #013/#015 smell inverted** — one mechanism asserting that two unrelated things are the same, which is exactly the trap #030 A1 sprang when a binary `isContactType` outlived the distinction it encoded. **The split follows #029 A2's precedent** (all-shot-type contact carved out of §3.11 into its own §3.12 so two recalibration sources were not tuned at once), with the same ordering logic: **the self-contained half first, the structural half second.** **Numbered `a`/`b` rather than renumbering §3.15/§3.16 (user call):** §3.16 is referenced by name in calibration.md's escalation rule, risks.md, and — critically — in the **shipped, never-to-be-retro-edited** text of #030 and #031 (calibration.md's "the per-phase landing notes are history" rule). A full renumber would leave live `§3.16` references meaning two different phases; the suffix costs nothing downstream.

**Decision B — the technical rate is PURELY RANDOM, rolled per team in `RotationState`, with NO causal model — and that is a positive design claim, not a simplification (user proposal, adopted).** The user's framing, adopted directly: *make it random; no calculation determines the likelihood a technical occurs.* **The reason this is right is structural rather than merely simple.** Every other foul in the engine hangs off a contest — a defender is drawn, a probability is contested against skills, a foul falls out. A technical has **no contest to hang off**: it is not caused by the shot, the matchup, or the rebound. Forcing it into the possession flow would make it inherit a `pickDefender` draw that has nothing to do with it, manufacturing a causal link the real event does not have. So the roll lives in **`RotationState.advancePossession(rng)`** — the between-possessions step, which §3.13 already established as an RNG consumer (#031, revising #023 C, so **§3.14a need not re-argue determinism**) and which is the engine's only "things that happen to a team, not to a possession" seam. **One roll per team per call**, so both teams can draw one independently in the same possession — correct, since a technical is assessed on a team, not on the possession's orientation.

**Decision B2 — the constant is stored as the PER-TEAM-PER-GAME rate and divided down to a per-check probability, not stored as the per-check probability (user call).** The real-world figure is **0.6–0.8 technicals per game league-wide** (user-supplied), i.e. **~0.35 per team per game**. The roll happens ~100 times per team per game, so the per-check probability is **~0.0035** — a number with no intuitive meaning, impossible to sanity-check by eye, and one that would **silently drift** if `DEFAULT_POSSESSIONS_PER_PERIOD` or the pace factor ever moved. `SimConfig` therefore stores **`TECHNICAL_FOULS_PER_TEAM_GAME ≈ 0.35`** — the number a tuner actually reasons about, and the one directly comparable to calibration.md — and derives the per-check probability by dividing by the nominal check count (`DEFAULT_POSSESSIONS_PER_PERIOD × PERIODS × 2`). **The divisor is NOMINAL, not actual, and the gap is deliberate:** the true possession count is pace-scaled per game (`PossessionEngine.simulate()` blends both coaches' `paceMultiplier`) and overtime adds more, so a fast-paced game takes more checks and draws **slightly more technicals than the constant nominally says**. That is correct behavior — a longer game has more opportunity — but it means the constant reads as "technicals per team per game **at nominal pace**", and **a harness landing a few percent off the constant is not a bug**. Recorded because the alternative reading (treating the miss as a calibration error and back-solving the constant) would chase noise.

**Decision C — the committer is drawn from the ON-FLOOR FIVE, weighted by `foulProne`; NO attributes are threaded into the engine, and the bench is deliberately excluded (both user calls, the second reversing this entry's own first draft).** The user initially proposed weighting by **`aggression`, `composure`, and `ego`** — and correctly corrected the design's claim that those do not exist: they are three of the **attributes** (player.md), not skills. **The design pass's first response was to adopt them, and that is withdrawn.** Two things settle it against:
- **The engine consumes skills, not attributes.** `PlayerGameState`'s constructor reads exclusively from `player.getSkills()` and has never touched `player.getAttributes()`. Attributes reach the sim **only** through the calculators that derive skills from them (player.md:255) — and `foulProne` is documented as *precisely* the composite in question: **"Aggression and reckless energy raise it; composure and awareness lower it"** (player.md:122). Threading the three raw attributes in would open a **second route around the attributes → skills → engine pipeline** for an influence already flowing through it — the #013/#015 duplicate-source shape.
- **The cost of the weaker signal is genuinely low here, which is what makes the trade easy.** `foulProne` is nearly flat (**sd 1.22** against a ~9.66 mean, #031 A), so the draw will look near-uniform — a high-`foulProne` player is perhaps ~2× likelier than a low one, not 10×. **But the weighting only decides WHICH of five broadly similar players wears a technical whose rate is a fixed constant (B).** It moves no aggregate, no calibrated number, and nothing observable at 33–71 events per harness run. This is the rare case where a weak signal is affordable, and it is affordable *because* B made the rate causally inert.

**The bench is excluded, on the user's measurement-bias argument rather than on realism.** In reality a technical can be called on a benched player or a coach. Including the bench was rejected because the pool is **~10 bench players against 5 on the floor**, so **two-thirds of technicals would land on players who are not playing** — and a benched player's ejection has *no* engine consequence (he is already off; `eligible(...)` simply stops selecting him). The rate budget would be spent mostly where nothing observable happens, and `foulProne`-high bench players would absorb technicals they had no opportunity to commit. **The accepted fidelity loss, stated plainly: the bench technical and the COACH technical are not modelled.** A coach is not a `PlayerGameState` at all, so a coach technical could only ever be a committer-less free throw for the other team — carried as a follow-up, not built.

**Decision D — the possession is UNCHANGED, which is what makes §3.14a structurally cheap and is the entire reason it goes first.** A technical awards one free throw and **play resumes from the point of interruption** — the ball does not change hands, and (the user's explicit note) **when an offensive player commits a technical the possession does not switch**. Because B rolls it in `RotationState` *between* possessions, there is **nothing to fork**: the roll fires, the FT is awarded and scored, and the possession loop proceeds untouched. **§3.14a therefore adds no branch to the possession path**, which is why it does **not** modify `possession-flow.puml`'s possession partitions — only the rotation-step block at the top gains a line. **This is the deliberate contrast with §3.14b**, where a flagrant awards FTs *and* returns the ball, breaking #030 B's invariant that every FT path ends the possession — the structural crux, isolated into the second pass where it can be argued on its own.

**Decision E — technicals get their OWN counter and do NOT feed the 6-foul disqualification (user call, and the NBA rule).** A technical does not count toward a player's six personal fouls. So `recordFoul()` is **not** reused: `PlayerGameState` gains a **separate `technicalFouls` counter** with its own `recordTechnicalFoul()`. This deliberately keeps `getFouls()` meaning exactly what `isFouledOut()`, `foulTroubleLevel()`, and the §3.10 penalty derivation already assume it means — **had the counters been merged, a technical would have silently pushed players toward foul-outs and into §3.13's foul-trouble bench curve**, moving two calibrated numbers (foul-outs ~0.39, the 4/5/6 distribution) for a reason unrelated to contact fouls. **The one place the split needs care is the period foul tally**: `GameData.isInBonus` counts `FOUL` events by committing team (#028 A1), so a `TECHNICAL_FOUL` event would join the bonus count unless excluded. **It is excluded** — a technical does not put a team in the penalty — which means the bonus derivation gains its **first outcome-aware filter**. Recorded as the honest cost of E: #028 A1's "one unified derivation over all `FOUL` events, not split per foul type" no longer holds literally, and the filter must be explicit rather than incidental.

**Decision F — the two-technical ejection extends `eligible(...)` and stays DERIVED; §3.14a does NOT take #023 F's exception, which moves to §3.14b (this pass's most useful finding).** Two technicals in one game is an automatic ejection (user call, and the rule). Per #031 H this must extend the **hard/forced** tier and the `eligible(...)` filter rather than add a fourth removal path. **But the anticipated stored-state exception does not arise here, and that is a direct consequence of the split (A).** todo.md, roadmap.md, and #031 H all state that §3.14 "needs real stored state" because "an ejection is not derivable from a counter the way `fouls >= 6` is." **That is true of a flagrant-2 — a severity grade with no counter behind it — and false of the two-technical case**, which is `technicalFouls >= TECHNICAL_EJECTION_LIMIT`: a monotonic counter, structurally identical to `fouls >= FOUL_OUT_LIMIT`. So §3.14a ships `isEjected()` as a **derived predicate** in the #023 F / #031 E mould — no flag, no stored state, no exception — and `eligible(...)` filters on `isFouledOut() || isEjected()`. **The #028 D-style argument for real stored state is deferred to §3.14b, where the flagrant-2 genuinely forces it.** Building the ejection seam in its *derivable* form first, then extending it, is the sequencing payoff A predicted. **The honest cost, budgeted up front: this ejection will essentially never fire in a harness run.** At ~0.35 technicals per team-game spread over five players, two on the same player in one game is on the order of one occurrence every several simulated seasons. **The harness will report 0.00 ejections on most seeds, and that is a correct result, not a failure** — the rule is right, the event is rare. It is built anyway (user call) because it is the substrate §3.14b depends on and because §3.14b's flagrant-2 ejections will exercise the same seam at a higher rate. Tests force the counter directly rather than waiting for the event.

**Decision G — the technical FT shooter is a DETERMINISTIC "best free-throw shooter on the floor" pick, a SECOND rule beside `pickFreeThrowShooter`'s weighted draw, which is untouched (user call).** A technical FT is shot by a player the offense *chooses* — answering todo.md's open question 3, the existing weighted draw is **not** good enough, because it models being *fouled* (weighted by `foulDrawing`, #028 B) and a technical FT involves no one being fouled. §3.14a adds a separate selection: **the highest `freeThrows` among the on-floor five, deterministic.** The two functions encode two genuinely different rules and must not be merged — merging them would either make bonus FTs go to the best shooter (wrong: the player who was fouled shoots) or make technical FTs weighted by `foulDrawing` (wrong: nobody was fouled). **Two consequences to state so a later reader does not "fix" them.** First, **it consumes no RNG**, which is a small determinism win. Second, and more visible: **the same player shoots essentially every technical FT for his team all game**, changing only when the lineup changes. That looks striking in a box score and reads like a bug — it is exactly what the real rule produces (teams do send their best shooter every time), and it is intended.

**Decision H — #030's clamp-helper consolidation is FOLDED IN here, at its fourth site (user call).** #030 set the trigger: two floor-free clamp sites is a coincidence, **a third is the signal**. §3.13 added the third (`foulTroubleSitProbability`), so todo.md records the trigger as already **FIRED**; §3.14a's technical rate is the **fourth**. The three existing sites hand-roll `Math.max(0.0, Math.min(PROB_CEILING, p))` — character-for-character identical in `SimConfig.rareEventProbability` and `SimConfig.foulTroubleSitProbability`, plus `FoulResolver.isFoul`'s floor-free multiply. §3.14a adds **one helper beside `clampProbability`** (a `clampRareProbability`, spelling at execution) and routes all four sites through it. **The `PROB_FLOOR` (0.02) must not apply to the technical rate, and the margin is not subtle: the per-check probability is ~0.0035, so the floor is roughly SIX TIMES the rate itself** — flooring it would inflate technicals by ~6× and make the constant tunable only upward, the exact failure `rareEventProbability`'s javadoc documents. **The user's framing, adopted and worth preserving: it is fine that some players may never commit a technical**, and no floor should manufacture a minimum. (Noted for precision, because the two questions sit at different points: the floor applies to *whether a technical happens*; the *committer* draw (C) is a weighted selection with no clamp at all, where every on-floor player with non-zero `foulProne` retains a non-zero chance. No player is made exempt by construction, and none should be.) **This is a behavior-neutral refactor** — no number moves, no recalibration — and the proof is that §3.10's and §3.13's existing tests must stay green **unchanged**.

**Decision I — the points cost is budgeted at +0.26/team/game, which is BELOW the harness noise floor, so the stop condition INVERTS: points must NOT move measurably.** The #030 E / #031 G discipline requires pricing the cost before building. Computed at the engine's shipped numbers (~1.17 points/possession, ~75% FT):

| Channel | pts/team/game |
|---|---|
| **§3.14a** technical FTs (0.35 × 1 FT × ~0.75) | **+0.26** |
| *(§3.14b, for context — flagrant FTs +0.24, retention +0.19)* | *(+0.43)* |

**+0.26 against a per-seed noise band of ±1.5 points (#029 E) — 0.2% of the 117.0 baseline.** §3.11 was pure-additive *and material*; §3.14a is pure-additive and **undetectable**. **So the stop condition is not "stop if points move too far" but its inverse: if the §3.4 aggregates move measurably at 5 seeds, that is a BUG — most likely the technical counter leaking into `getFouls()` (E) or the bonus tally (E's filter) — not a calibration result.** §3.16 owns re-centering; §3.14a re-centers nothing and must not.

**Decision J — a NEW harness instrument is required, and the rate is a `ballpark`, not a TARGET.** Answering todo.md's open question 6: the existing foul-mix line does **not** suffice — it is a per-shot-type breakdown of *shooting* fouls, and a technical is not a shot foul at all, so it would never appear. `CalibrationHarness` gains a **technicals/team/game line** (plus the ejection count, which will read 0.00 per F). **Resolvability, computed before choosing the rate** (the §3.13 measure-first lesson): at 102 games a 0.7/game league rate yields ~71 events, relative sd **11.8%** — and at **5 seeds, ~357 events, 5.3%**. **So the rate is calibratable, but only as a 5-seed mean and only as a count line; a single-seed reading cannot resolve it.** It enters calibration.md as a **`ballpark` (~0.6–0.8 league-wide / ~0.3–0.4 per team)**, explicitly **not** a TARGET: nothing about the engine is *tuned toward* it, the constant is set directly from it (B2), and calibration.md's own rule is that a TARGET is a number a phase has deliberately tuned against. Per calibration.md's discipline the row and the harness `(target ~N)` string land in the same change, and — like every row in that table — it is **UNSOURCED**, taken from the user's figure and inheriting §3.16's job (1).

**Rationale**: *(A)* The taxonomy question could not be answered "one mechanic" without asserting a shared surface that the enumeration shows does not exist; and once they are two mechanics, building them in one pass tunes two independent rate sources simultaneously — the exact trap #029 A2 split §3.11/§3.12 to avoid. Splitting also lets the ejection seam be built in its easy (derived) form before the hard (stored-state) one, which F shows is a real payoff rather than a hoped-for one. The `a`/`b` suffix protects the shipped, non-retro-editable §3.16 references in #030/#031. *(B)* A technical is the one event in the model with no causal link to the possession, so giving it one would be modelling fiction; `RotationState` is the only seam for team-level, possession-independent events, and §3.13 already paid the determinism cost of making it an RNG consumer. *(B2)* A constant a tuner can read against calibration.md beats one they must multiply by 200 to interpret, and deriving the per-check probability keeps the rate correct if the possession count ever moves — the nominal-vs-actual gap is real behavior (more possessions, more opportunity) rather than error. *(C)* `foulProne` already *is* the aggression/composure composite by construction (player.md:122), so routing the raw attributes in would duplicate an existing path (#013/#015) and breach the attributes→skills→engine pipeline for the first time; the flat signal is affordable precisely because B made the rate causally inert, so the weighting moves nothing measurable. Excluding the bench avoids spending two-thirds of the rate budget on players whose technicals and ejections have no engine consequence. *(D)* The possession-unchanged property is what makes §3.14a cheap, and isolating the retention fork into §3.14b is the whole point of A's ordering. *(E)* Merging the counters would silently move two §3.13-calibrated numbers for an unrelated reason; keeping `getFouls()` meaning "personal fouls" preserves every existing consumer's assumption, and the bonus-tally filter is the honest, explicitly-named cost. *(F)* The two-technical case is a monotonic counter, so #023 F's derivation applies unchanged and the discipline should not be broken a phase early merely because §3.14 was *predicted* to break it — the prediction was correct about flagrant-2 and wrong about technicals, and saying so is more useful than inheriting it. *(G)* Two different real rules deserve two functions; merging them would get one of the two wrong, and the determinism is a side benefit. *(H)* Four independent copies of one clamp with no owner is the definition of a warranted consolidation, and the site that trips the trigger is the honest owner; the ~6× floor-to-rate ratio makes the floor-free requirement quantitative rather than stylistic. *(I)* Pricing first is #030 E/#031 G discipline, and the finding that the cost is sub-noise is *more* useful than a large budget: it converts the aggregates from a thing to re-center into a **correctness check**. *(J)* #026 D's "build the instrument before you claim neutrality", plus the §3.13 lesson that a rare event's resolvability must be computed before a rate is designed — doing that arithmetic first is what establishes the 5-seed rule for this line.  
**Trade-off**: *(A)* Two passes means two design sessions and two close-outs for what the roadmap scoped as one bullet, and §3.14b inherits every hard question (retention, stored state, severity grades) with none of the easy ones to balance it. The `a`/`b` suffix makes the roadmap read less uniformly than a flat sequence. *(B)* A purely random rate means **no player, coach, or game-situation input whatsoever** — a blowout, a rivalry, and a walkover all generate technicals at the same rate, and no coach attribute influences it. Accepted deliberately, but it is a real fidelity ceiling that no amount of tuning can lift. *(B2)* One more derived step, and the nominal divisor means the shipped rate is pace-sensitive in a way the constant's name does not advertise. *(C)* The committer distribution will be very nearly uniform, so "who commits technicals" carries almost no player character — the thing the user's original attribute proposal was reaching for is genuinely lost, not merely deferred. Bench and coach technicals are absent from the model entirely. *(D)* None material — but the flip side is that §3.14a delivers no possession-path fidelity at all, so the phase's visible output is one FT and a counter. *(E)* #028 A1's uniform "all `FOUL` events" bonus derivation acquires its first exception, so any future foul type must now consciously decide whether it counts toward the penalty; a second counter on `PlayerGameState` is also a second thing a box-score/stats consumer may eventually want surfaced (it is not, in this pass — #014/#017). *(F)* The headline deliverable is unobservable: the harness will read 0.00 ejections on most seeds, so the rule's correctness rests on forced-counter unit tests rather than on aggregate evidence. *(G)* One player monopolising his team's technical FTs is visibly odd in a box score and will invite a "fix" from a later reader. *(H)* The refactor touches §3.10's and §3.13's **shipped** code paths, so a mistake there breaks calibrated behavior for no functional gain — mitigated by the requirement that their existing tests pass unchanged. *(I)* A sub-noise cost means the pass cannot *demonstrate* it landed correctly from the aggregates — absence of movement is consistent with both "correct" and "the roll never fires", so J's dedicated instrument is load-bearing rather than nice-to-have. *(J)* One more harness line to maintain, and a number that can only be judged at 5 seeds — slower to check than every other row in calibration.md.  
**Alternatives considered**: *(A)* **One combined §3.14 covering both** — rejected: the table shows no shared surface, and it would tune two independent rate sources at once (the #029 A2 trap). **Flagrants first, technicals second** — rejected: it forces the possession-retention fork *and* the flagrant-2 stored-state argument into the same pass, and gives up F's finding that the ejection seam can be built derived-first. **A full renumber (flagrants §3.15, profiles §3.16, recalibration §3.17)** — rejected (user call): §3.16 is named in the shipped text of #030/#031, which calibration.md forbids retro-editing, so live references would mean two different phases. *(B)* **Model *why* a technical occurs** (frustration from a scoring run, foul trouble, a coach trait) — rejected (user call): it fabricates a causal chain the engine has no inputs for and would need game-situation awareness the rotation step does not have (#031's follow-up). **Roll it inside the possession flow** — rejected: it would inherit a `pickDefender` draw irrelevant to the event, manufacturing a link the real foul does not have. *(B2)* **Store the per-check probability (~0.0035) directly** — rejected: uninterpretable by eye, not comparable to calibration.md, and it silently drifts if the possession count changes. *(C)* **Thread `aggression`/`composure`/`ego` into `PlayerGameState`** — rejected (user call) after being provisionally adopted earlier in this pass: the engine reads skills, `foulProne` already *is* that composite (player.md:122), and a raw-attribute path would be a second route to an influence already flowing (#013/#015). **A new player skill or attribute for temperament** — rejected: fabricating a field ahead of its consumer (#014/#017), for a draw that moves nothing measurable. **A flat uniform draw over the five** — rejected as *slightly* worse than `foulProne` at identical cost; `foulProne` at least leans the right way. **Include bench players in the pool** — rejected (user call): ~2/3 of technicals would land on non-playing players whose ejections have no engine consequence, biasing the spend toward the unobservable. **A zero weight below some `foulProne` threshold, making some players exempt** — rejected: a hard cliff of the kind #031 B rejected for the sit curve, and unvalidatable at 33–71 events per run. *(E)* **Reuse `recordFoul()` for technicals** — rejected: it would silently feed `isFouledOut()`, `foulTroubleLevel()`, and the §3.10 penalty count, moving two §3.13-calibrated numbers for an unrelated cause. **Count technicals toward the period bonus tally** — rejected: not the rule, and it would inflate the penalty rate (#030's measured-sensitive number). *(F)* **Ship a stored `ejected` boolean anyway, to pre-build §3.14b's exception** — rejected: it is a second source of truth for a fact the counter already holds (#023 F / #031 E), and taking the exception a phase before it is forced is exactly the discipline erosion #013/#015 guard against. **Defer ALL ejection work to §3.14b** — rejected (user call): the seam is §3.14b's dependency, and building it where it is derivable is the payoff A's ordering was chosen for. **A fourth removal path in `RotationState`** — rejected pre-emptively by #031 H; ejection is the hard tier and extends `eligible(...)`. *(G)* **Reuse `pickFreeThrowShooter` for technical FTs** — rejected: it weights by `foulDrawing`, modelling *being fouled*, and nobody is fouled on a technical. **Change `pickFreeThrowShooter` to a best-shooter pick for all situations** — rejected: it would send bonus FTs to the wrong player (the fouled player shoots). **A weighted `freeThrows` draw rather than a deterministic max** — rejected: the real rule is a choice, not a lottery, and determinism costs no RNG. *(H)* **Leave the consolidation to §3.15** — rejected (user call): §3.15's validation gate is reproducing §3.14b's landing *exactly*, and folding an unrelated refactor into it muddies that check; the pass adding the fourth site is the honest owner. **Hand-roll a fourth clamp** — rejected: four copies with no owner is precisely what #030's trigger was set to catch. **Apply `clampProbability` (with the floor) to the technical rate** — rejected quantitatively: `PROB_FLOOR` is ~6× the rate, so it would inflate technicals ~6-fold and make the constant tunable only upward. *(I)* **Budget a re-centering pass for the added FTs** — rejected: at +0.26 against ±1.5 noise there is nothing to re-center, and §3.16 owns the aggregates anyway. *(J)* **Reuse the existing foul-mix line** — rejected: it breaks down *shooting* fouls by shot type, so a technical would never appear in it. **Promote the rate to a TARGET** — rejected: nothing is tuned toward it (the constant is set from it directly), which is not what calibration.md means by TARGET.  
**Status of §3.14a decisions**: A–J all resolved by this entry (mirrors #021–#031). **§3.14b (flagrants) is NOT resolved here** — its open questions are in todo.md and it needs its own design pass. Net schema change: **NONE** — no new column, no OpenAPI change, no new `PlayType`, no new `FreeThrowSource`, no new coach attribute (#018's five stand), no new player attribute or skill (C). **One new `outcome` string on the existing `PlayType.FOUL`** — free text since #020, so no migration; spelling `TECHNICAL_FOUL` (mirroring `SHOOTING_FOUL`/`AND_ONE`, and colliding with nothing in the §3.8/§3.9/§3.10 vocabularies — the #027 D discipline). **Its `committingTeamId` is populated** like every other `FOUL` event (#028 D), so the column stays uniform even though E excludes the event from the bonus count. New engine pieces: `SimConfig.TECHNICAL_FOULS_PER_TEAM_GAME` + `TECHNICAL_EJECTION_LIMIT` + a per-check derivation helper (B2) routed through the new shared floor-free clamp (H); a `technicalFouls` counter + `recordTechnicalFoul()` + a **derived** `isEjected()` on `PlayerGameState` (E/F — no flag, no RNG, no coach knowledge, matching #031 E's shape); the technical roll + committer draw in `RotationState.advancePossession(rng)` (B/C); `eligible(...)` extended to `isFouledOut() || isEjected()` (F, #031 H); a deterministic best-FT-shooter pick beside `pickFreeThrowShooter` (G); an outcome-aware exclusion in `GameData.isInBonus` (E); and the clamp-helper consolidation across four sites (H). `CalibrationHarness` gains a technicals/ejections line (J). **`possession-flow.puml` changes only in the rotation-step block at the top — §3.14a adds NO possession branch (D)**, which is itself worth noting on the diagram so a reader does not go looking for one. **Determinism**: `advancePossession` already consumes RNG (#031, revising #023 C) so no new argument is needed — but the **number** of draws per call changes, which re-baselines any seed-pinned stream expectation. **The technical draw must be unconditional and at a fixed point** for the same reason #031's is: a conditional draw forks the stream on rotation state. **Open-at-execution (small, constrained)**: the exact `TECHNICAL_FOULS_PER_TEAM_GAME` value (constrained: back-solved from the ~0.6–0.8 league figure and confirmed against J's new harness line at 5 seeds); the exact spelling of the clamp helper and the new outcome string (constrained by the #027 D non-collision rule); where precisely in `advancePossession` the draw is taken (constrained: fixed point, unconditional — the natural site is beside §3.13's existing draw); and whether the ejection line is worth printing at all once it reads 0.00 (F). **Not open**: the split and its ordering (A), the no-causal-model call (B), the game-level constant shape (B2), `foulProne`-weighted and on-floor-only (C), possession-unchanged (D), the separate counter and the bonus exclusion (E), ejection stays **derived** (F), two distinct FT-shooter rules (G), folding in the consolidation (H), and the no-re-centering fence (I).

**Implementation note (from execution, 2026-08).** Shipped as A–J specify, with **two divergences and one arithmetic correction**, all recorded below. Engine work only, in the `sim` package: no schema change, no OpenAPI change, no new `PlayType`, no new coach attribute, no new player attribute or skill — exactly as the Status block scoped it.

**Divergence 1 — a new `FreeThrowSource.TECHNICAL` WAS added, against the Status block's "no new `FreeThrowSource`".** That line left the call open at execution ("decide whether an existing value fits or the outcome string alone carries it"), and neither alternative survives contact with the instrument: the harness reads a free throw's source straight off its outcome suffix (#029 D), so reusing `SHOOTING` or `BONUS` would **silently inflate a real source's share on the very line §3.14a is judged by**, and emitting an untagged FT would bucket every technical as `UNKNOWN` — the self-check #029 D built the enum to provide. The constraint the Status block was actually protecting is untouched: the source is free text folded into `outcome` since #020, `FreeThrowSource` is internal to the `sim` package, and there is no migration. The harness now prints `FTA TECHNICAL: 1.1% (0.38/team/game)` as its own row.

**Divergence 2 — `advancePossession` consumes THREE draws, not the two the design predicted.** B/J anticipated one new draw (the technical roll). Execution added a **second unconditional draw for the committer selection**, on the same fixed-point reasoning #031 used: drawing it only on a hit would fork the stream on the roll's own outcome and make the draw count per call variable. Drawing it always costs one `nextDouble()` on the ~99.8% of calls that discard it and buys a flat, state-independent count. `RotationStateTest`'s existing draw-count test was re-baselined 1 → 3; **its load-bearing assertion — that the count does not depend on rotation state — was not weakened and still passes.**

**Arithmetic correction to B2 — the per-check probability is ~0.00175, not the "~0.0035" B2 states.** B2 derived that figure from "the roll happens ~100 times per team per game". The true count is **~200**: `PossessionEngine.simulate()` advances **both** rotations on **every** possession, so a team is checked on its defensive possessions too. The divisor shipped is the real call count (`DEFAULT_POSSESSIONS_PER_PERIOD × PERIODS × 2 = 200`), which is what makes the harness land on the configured rate — a divisor of 100 would have doubled technicals to ~0.7 per team per game. **The game-level constant B2 argued for is unchanged and unaffected**; only the derived number in the prose was wrong. Consequently H's "`PROB_FLOOR` is ~6× the rate" is also understated: it is **more than 10×**, so H's floor-free requirement is stronger than argued, not weaker. Javadoc, the diagram note and `SimConfigTest` all carry the correction.

**Resolved the open-at-execution items.** `TECHNICAL_FOULS_PER_TEAM_GAME = 0.35` (unchanged from the design's back-solve; confirmed at 5 seeds, see below). The clamp helper is **`clampRareProbability`**, beside `clampProbability`; the outcome string is **`TECHNICAL_FOUL`** as spelled in the Status block, non-colliding (#027 D) and hoisted to `GameData.TECHNICAL_FOUL_OUTCOME` because `GameData` is the class that must *recognise* it for E's exclusion — a literal on both sides of that agreement is how the two would drift apart. The draw site is beside §3.13's, immediately after the energy drain/recover and before the three removal tiers. **The ejection line DOES print** — a labelled 0.0x is what stops a later reader from reading its absence as an oversight.

**One structural note the plan did not anticipate.** `RotationState` has no access to `GameData`, the team ids, the period or the sequence, so it cannot emit the event or award the FT itself. `advancePossession` therefore **returns the committer** (or null) and `PossessionEngine.simulate()` — which already calls it and holds all four — emits the `FOUL` and the free throw. The roll and the committer draw stay in the rotation where B put them; only the plumbing lives in the engine.

**Two further leaks found and closed during execution, both in the harness rather than the engine.** (1) `CalibrationHarness` computes its **own** per-team-period foul tally from the event log, independently of `GameData.isInBonus` — so E's exclusion had to be applied **twice**, or the harness's bonus-rate line would have disagreed with the engine's penalty derivation (an instrument wrong in the direction of the change it measures). (2) The harness's `Fouls / team / game` line is a tally over **all** `FOUL` events and therefore now includes technicals: §3.13's 19.0 and §3.14a's ~19.4 are **not** like-for-like, and the line says so. Personal fouls did not move — `GameSimulatorIntegrationTest`'s box-score reconciliation pins it.

**Landing (5-seed mean, 102 games/seed, seeds 1000–5000).** **Technicals 0.367/team/game** against the 0.35 configured — ~5% high, which is B2's documented nominal-vs-actual pace gap (real possessions exceed the nominal divisor) and explicitly **not** drift to back-solve against. Points **117.5** (§3.13: 117.0), FG% **46.6%** (46.6%), 3P% **37.0%** (36.6%), assists **26.8** (26.7), turnovers **13.5** (13.8). **Against I's INVERTED stop condition: the aggregates did not move measurably.** The +0.5 points sits inside the ±1.5 noise band and above the +0.26 budget, and the two numbers that would expose a leak are both clean — **the penalty rate is flat at 51.2%** (§3.13: 51.3%), proving E's bonus exclusion holds, and **foul-outs 0.409** sit inside their own seed spread (0.368–0.441 across these five). Nothing was re-centered; §3.16 still owns the contested targets.

**F's "0.00 ejections" prediction was too pessimistic, and the mechanism is correct.** Ejections land at **0.014/team/game** — roughly one per team per season, not "one every several simulated seasons". The arithmetic checks out: at 0.367 technicals spread over five players (~0.073 each), P(a given player reaches 2) ≈ 0.0026, times five ≈ 0.013 per team-game, matching the observation. The rule is right; F's estimate of its frequency was off by about an order of magnitude. The ejection is still asserted by forced-counter unit tests, exactly as F directed — the aggregate is now a weak corroboration rather than no evidence at all.

**H's behavior-neutrality was verified as specified**: all four floor-free sites route through `clampRareProbability`, and **§3.10's and §3.13's existing tests pass UNCHANGED**. The only three shipped tests touched were the draw-count baseline (divergence 2), the FT-source enumeration (divergence 1, rewritten to enumerate off the enum so a future source cannot make it stale again), and the box-score foul reconciliation (whose *premise* changed — it now reconciles against **personal** foul events, which is E working). No calibrated behavior moved.

**Coverage**: 512 tests green; `sim` package at **99.2% line** (985 lines, 8 uncovered — all pre-existing, none in §3.14a's code); `All coverage checks have been met.`

**§3.14a follow-up (carry forward)**:
- ~~**§3.14b (flagrants) inherits every hard question**~~ — **RESOLVED by #034 (2026-08), and one of the two predictions here was WRONG.** The inherited list was: the possession-retention fork, the flagrant-2 stored-state exception, the severity grade, and flagrants counting toward the 6-foul limit. Three landed as stated (retention broke #030 B's invariant as expected — but via the second-chance loop's *existing* `continue` under its existing cap, five lines rather than a new mechanism (#034 B); the severity grade is a flat 15% conditional sub-roll (#034 E); and E's counter split indeed does **not** apply — a flagrant uses `recordFoul()` (#034 I)). **The stored-state claim above — "F showed the two-technical case does not force it; flagrant-2 does" — is simply false** (#034 F): a flagrant-2 ejects **immediately**, so there is no accumulation threshold to remember and `flagrantTwos >= 1` is a monotonic counter exactly like `fouls >= 6` and `technicalFouls >= 2`. It stays **derived**, extending the same `isDisqualified(...)` predicate to a third cause. **The #023 F exception was refused for the third pass running and the prediction is RETIRED, not deferred again** — the general finding is that *every* disqualification in this model is absorbing and monotonic, which is the shape #023 F's derivation was built for.
- **A technical has no causal input of any kind** (B's accepted ceiling) — no player temperament, no game situation, no coach trait. Making it situational needs the **same missing prerequisite** as every parked strategic-substitution idea: game-situation awareness in the rotation step (#031's follow-up, ideas.md). If that ever lands, this is a natural second consumer.
- **Bench and coach technicals are not modelled** (C). A coach technical has no committer in the current model (a coach is not a `PlayerGameState`); it would be a committer-less FT for the other team. Purely additive whenever a consumer wants it.
- **`GameData.isInBonus` now has an outcome-aware exclusion** (E) — #028 A1's "one unified derivation over all `FOUL` events" no longer holds literally. **Any future foul type must consciously decide whether it counts toward the penalty**, and §3.14b's flagrant is the immediate next case (it does).
- ~~**The `technicalFouls` counter is not surfaced anywhere**~~ — **SUPERSEDED by #033 (2026-08, user call), one session later.** It is now on the box score column, the entity and the OpenAPI `BoxScore`. The unparking argument was **parity, not a new consumer**: it is the twelfth per-player accumulator in a set whose other eleven are all already exposed, so omitting it made the box score inconsistent rather than lean. `committing_team_id` remains parked — #033's argument does not extend to it.
- **The ejection rule is essentially untestable in aggregate** (F) — ~~expect 0.00 on the harness for most seeds~~. **Measured at execution: 0.014/team/game**, i.e. roughly one per team per season, an order of magnitude more often than F estimated (the arithmetic is in the implementation note). Still far too rare to *tune* against, so forced-counter unit tests remain the validation — but the harness line is now weak corroboration rather than nothing. **§3.14b duly exercised the same `eligible(...)` seam at a higher rate, as predicted**: flagrant-2 ejections measure 0.023/team/game (a flagrant-2 ejects on the *first* one where a technical needs two), taking the combined ejection line to **0.027** — roughly double the technical kind alone, and the point at which the hard tier stopped being dead-but-correct code.
- **A foul type must apply the bonus-tally exclusion TWICE, or not at all, deliberately.** `GameData.isInBonus` and `CalibrationHarness`'s independent per-team-period tally are two separate derivations over the same events (found at §3.14a execution). **§3.14b was the first test of this and took the "not at all" branch**: a flagrant **does** count toward the penalty, so it added nothing to either — and #034 I required that non-change be **pinned by a test** rather than left to inspection, since "we changed nothing" and "we forgot" are otherwise indistinguishable. It held (penalty rate 51.9% against 51.2%). The warning stands for any *future* foul type that does not count: exclude it in both places, or the instrument silently disagrees with the engine.
- **The clamp-helper consolidation is DONE as of this pass** (H) — #030's follow-up and todo.md's "⚠️ THE CLAMP-HELPER TRIGGER HAS FIRED" note can be retired at close-out. A **fifth** floor-free site now costs one call, not a fourth copy.
- **The technicals rate joins calibration.md as an UNSOURCED ballpark** (J), inheriting §3.16's job (1) like every other row. It is judged **only at 5 seeds** — a single-seed reading cannot resolve it (11.8% relative sd at 102 games).
- (The §3.7-E shot-clock-pressure, §3.9 finer turnover sub-types, §3.10 observed-split, §3.12 3PA-volume, §3.13 saturation/time-awareness/`substitutionAggressiveness`-split, and §3.6 time-column follow-ups still stand.)

### 033 — Surface the `technicalFouls` counter on the box score (DB + entity + OpenAPI), reversing #032's parked-follow-up on a PARITY argument rather than a new consumer
**Date**: 2026-08
**Scope**: A small, additive follow-on to §3.14a, taken immediately after it shipped (user call). #032's follow-up parked the `technicalFouls` counter as "not surfaced anywhere — no consumer yet (#014/#017)". **This entry unparks it.** Net change: one nullable column (`gametime.box_score.technical_fouls`), one entity field, one OpenAPI `BoxScore` property, plus the mapper and simulator writes. **No engine behavior change** — the counter, the ejection predicate, the bonus exclusion and every calibrated number are exactly as §3.14a shipped them.

**Decision A — the field is surfaced EVERYWHERE the other stat columns are, not just in the database (user call).** The alternative on the table was `committing_team_id`'s precedent (§3.10, #028 D): persist it, leave it off the OpenAPI model until a consumer asks. **Rejected in favor of full parity** — DB column, `BoxScoreEntity`, and the OpenAPI `BoxScore` schema. The user's framing, adopted: *the field should live anywhere the rest are exposed.* A stat that is queryable in SQL but invisible to every API client is a half-surfaced field, and the asymmetry is a trap for the Phase-4 consumer that eventually reads it.

**Decision B — the justification is PARITY, not a newly-identified consumer, and that distinction matters because it is what makes this compatible with #014/#017.** The don't-fabricate-ahead-of-a-consumer rule exists to stop speculative fields.
